# OpenTAP Architecture Brainstorm 01: full record

Produced 2026-10-12 by a 46-agent workflow: four specialist personas (round 1), cross-examination (round 2), 32 independent fact-checks of the personas' load-bearing claims, revised positions (round 3), and two critics. The lead architect's executive synthesis and Phase 1 blueprint are in 01-architecture-brainstorm.md. Headings inside each persona's text are demoted two levels so the record's own structure stays navigable.

## Round 1: brainstorms

### Opus-Network

#### Executive take
I would build the mirror as an idempotent, slot-addressed append into a fixed ring in the partner's NVLOG memory, with sequencing, epoch fencing and redelivery above the transport, so that TCP, FC and RDMA are all "put these bytes at this ring offset and tell me when they are there". Phase 1 should ship two transports: a busy-polled userspace TCP ring (lowest latency we can get on 10GbE, ~20-30 us per 4K mirror write) and a "BlockRing" that writes the partner's ring through an in-box NVMe-oF target namespace backed by a DAX file on the partner's NVDIMM; the second one is the FC path, because I verified that RHEL 9 and RHEL 10 kernels ship `nvmet-fc` (Emulex lpfc) but do not build `tcm_qla2xxx` or `efct`, so the brief's implied QLogic+LIO route means maintaining out-of-tree kernel modules forever. The single biggest risk in my area is direct-connect (no switch) FC target mode between two stock HBAs: N2N target mode has a history of fixes in qla2xxx and is unproven for lpfc nvmet, so Phase 1 must include an FC spike and a budget line for a pair of cheap FC switches. The thing I most disagree with is treating FC as the performance goal: through any Linux target stack an FC mirror write lands at 50-150 us, slower than busy-polled 10GbE TCP and roughly 10x slower than 25GbE RoCE; FC buys isolation and distance, not speed, and NetApp only gets speed from FC-VI because its adapters run RDMA-style firmware we cannot load on stock HBAs. Two other holes are design-changing: RAM-only mirroring loses 10-15 s of acknowledged writes on a dual power loss (same rack, same PDU), so the ack must be defined against NVDIMM-N or a PLP NVMe log; and node-local disks make ONTAP-style takeover impossible unless the partner also applies the entire mirror stream to its own array.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Kernel subsystems (verified against Rocky r9 and r10 `kernel-x86_64-rhel.config`)
- **nvmet (`CONFIG_NVME_TARGET=m`, `_FC=m`, `_TCP=m`, `_RDMA=m`, `_LOOP=m`)** — in-box on both RHEL 9 and 10. This is the FC/RDMA/TCP receiver for the BlockRing design. `lpfc` is built with NVMe target support when `NVME_TARGET_FC` is enabled (`lpfc_enable_nvmet=<WWPN list>`; a port is target *or* initiator, per SUSE docs). Red Hat's NVMe/FC article lists an IBM target-mode configuration as supported since RHEL 8.1, so the target stack is not a dead path for Red Hat.
- **`tcm_qla2xxx` — `# CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX is not set` on RHEL 9 and 10.** `qla2xxx` itself (`=m`) carries the target code, but the LIO fabric module must be self-built against kernel-devel or come from ELRepo kernel-ml. Upstream since 3.5; per-port `qlini_mode` (enabled/disabled/dual/exclusive) and N2N target fixes landed 4.16-5.5; "restore initiator in dual mode" fix in 5.15.
- **`efct` (Emulex SCSI target, LIO) — upstream 5.14+, `# CONFIG_SCSI_EFCT is not set` on RHEL 9 and 10.** SCSI only, separate PCI IDs (e307/f407, which I believe are target-personality IDs; verify).
- **`target_core_user` (TCMU, `CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m`)** — in-box on both. Fallback receiver for SCSI-encoded mirroring; no Rust crate exists, a handler is ~1.5K lines against `tcmu-design.rst` (UIO mmap ring, 4-byte write to complete, out-of-order completion flag).
- **ublk (`CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m`) — RHEL 10 only;** absent from the RHEL 9 config (6.0 feature). Phase 1 front door; zero-copy variant needs 6.15.
- **io_uring** — `=y` on both, but RHEL 9.4+ ships `kernel.io_uring_disabled=2` (EPERM) by default; set to 0. Features by kernel: `SEND_ZC`, multishot recv, provided buffer rings 6.0; NAPI busy-poll registration 6.9 (I believe); zero-copy rx 6.15. RHEL 9's 5.14 base has none of these, so RHEL 10 (6.12) is the floor for an io_uring data path.
- **pmem/DAX** — `LIBNVDIMM=m`, `DEV_DAX_PMEM=m`, `X86_PMEM_LEGACY=m` on both: fsdax on NVDIMM-N, and `memmap=16G!64G` emulation of pmem from DRAM on machines without NVDIMM.
- **RDMA** — `mlx5_ib=m` + `rdma-core` userspace on both. `XDP_SOCKETS=y` on both (not recommended, see Tier 1).
- **NVMe native multipath** (`nvme_core.multipath`, default on in RHEL) — gives dual-path failover for the BlockRing for free.

##### Rust crates
- `io-uring` 0.7 (tokio-rs, raw SQ/CQ, mature; supports `UringCmd80` for NVMe passthrough) — the data-path I/O primitive. `tokio` 1.x for control plane/admin only. `monoio` 0.2 (thread-per-core io_uring, young), `glommio` (maintenance slowed), `tokio-uring` 0.5 (not production): a line each, none needed.
- `socket2` 0.5 for `TCP_NODELAY`, `SO_BUSY_POLL`, `TCP_QUICKACK`, `SO_INCOMING_CPU`.
- `zerocopy` 0.8 for the 64-byte record/RPC headers (`FromBytes`/`IntoBytes`/`KnownLayout`); `bytes` 1.x for buffer handles; `rkyv`/`postcard` only for control-plane payloads.
- `crc32c` 0.6 (SSE4.2, ~10+ GB/s) for record checksums; `xxhash-rust` xxh3 as alternative (no advantage once crc32c is hardware).
- `memmap2` 0.9 for DAX mapping; persistence via `core::arch::x86_64::_mm_clwb`/`_mm_sfence` directly (PMDK bindings unnecessary).
- `libublk` 0.4.7 (Ming Lei's crate, actively maintained) for the Phase 1 ublk front door.
- RDMA: `sideway` 0.4.3 (MPL-2.0, wraps the new ibverbs post-send/poll APIs, needs CMake+Clang) or raw `rdma-mummy-sys`/`rdma-sys` plus a ~1K-line in-house wrapper (what I would do; the subset is PD/MR/RC QP/CQ/`rdma_cm`). `ibverbs` 0.8 (jonhoo) is sound but minimal. `async-rdma` 0.5 is GPL — avoid. `rdma-io` 0.0.2 too new.
- RPC: custom framing for the data path; `tarpc` 0.36 or `tonic` 0.13 for the control plane and admin API; `capnp-rpc` is single-threaded (`!Send`) — not for the data path. `iceoryx2` 0.5/0.6 is a credible zero-copy same-node IPC if we do not want to own the SPSC ring.
- `openraft` 0.9/0.10 for the control plane (not my area; note it supports learners/witness-style membership).
- `spdk-rs` (OpenEBS Mayastor) only if the SPDK FC path is chosen; SPDK's FC transport is marked experimental and requires Broadcom's out-of-tree userspace driver `github.com/ecdufcdrvr/bcmufctdrvr` (LPe31000/32000, 35000/36000, 37000/38000; 93 commits; maintenance cadence unknown).

##### Hardware
- FC: **Emulex LPe32002** (32G dual port, SLI-4) for the in-box `nvmet-fc` path; QLogic QLE2692/QLE2772 only if we accept building `tcm_qla2xxx`. 16GFC line rate 14.025 Gbit/s ≈ 1,600 MB/s per port; 32GFC ≈ 3,200 MB/s; max frame payload 2112 B.
- Ethernet: Mellanox ConnectX-5/6 25GbE (RoCEv2, mlx5); any Intel X710 for TCP.
- NVDIMM-N on R740xd: up to 12 x 16 GB = 192 GB, two CPUs and 12 RDIMMs minimum, Dell NVDIMM battery in a 3.5" mid tray (reseller configurators; confirm with the Dell Technical Guide). Skylake/Cascade Lake are ADR-only (no eADR).

#### Proposed architecture
##### The one abstraction: a remote ring, not a stream
The partner reserves, per owner node, a fixed byte ring in its NVLOG arena (pmem if present). The owner *appends self-describing records at ring offsets it chooses*. A transport's only job is: put these bytes at this offset in the partner's ring and complete when they are there. Nothing is in-order, nothing is exactly-once: a record is valid when its header and trailing CRC verify, so duplicates and reordering are harmless, which is what makes FC (SCSI/NVMe commands complete out of order), RDMA and multipath fit without special cases.

**Record** (64 B header, payload, 4 B trailer CRC): magic, version, flags (`CP_BOUNDARY`, `BATCH_END`, `CONTROL`), len, `epoch` (control-plane fencing token / volume generation), `seq` (owner log sequence), `req_id` (N-Blade idempotency key), vol id, LBA, payload crc32c, header crc. The same bytes are the NVLOG entry (Phase 2), the wire format and the replay unit; define it once in Phase 1.

```rust
pub struct Slot { pub off: u64, pub len: u32 }          // offset in partner ring
pub struct Caps { pub ack: AckLevel /*Placed|Durable*/, pub max_msg: u32,
                  pub max_inflight: u32, pub can_reject: bool }
pub enum Done { Ok { tag: u64 }, Rejected { tag: u64, partner_epoch: u64 },
                Failed { tag: u64 }, LinkDown }
pub trait RemoteRing: Send {
    fn caps(&self) -> Caps;
    fn append(&mut self, slot: Slot, iov: &[IoSlice<'_>], tag: u64) -> Result<(), Busy>;
    fn kick(&mut self);                                   // sendmsg / submit / doorbell
    fn poll(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<Done>, max: usize) -> usize;
    fn fail_all(&mut self) -> Vec<u64>;                   // fate unknown -> redeliver elsewhere
}
pub trait LocalRing { fn arena(&self) -> &[u8]; fn durable_to(&self, off: u64); /* clwb+sfence */
                      fn scan(&self, from: u64) -> RecordIter<'_>; }
```
`MirrorLog` (transport-agnostic, one per owner/partner pair) owns `seq`, `epoch`, slot allocation, batching, deadlines, the link set and the state machine; it never sees sockets, LBAs, rkeys or fds.

##### Three implementations
- **TcpRing (Tier 1):** one pinned thread per link, blocking `sendmsg` with iovecs straight from the arena (no sender copy); batch framing = a 32 B batch header listing slots, then the receiver `readv`s payloads directly into `arena[slot]` on pmem, `clwb`s, and sends a 16 B ack `{upto_seq, consumed}`. `TCP_NODELAY`, `SO_BUSY_POLL=50`, `TCP_QUICKACK`, interrupt coalescing off, core pinned to the NIC's NUMA node. io_uring version later (RHEL 10): multishot recv + provided buffers, `SEND_ZC` for ≥16 KiB batches.
- **BlockRing (Tier 2 FC; also TCP/RDMA):** the partner exposes its ring as an NVMe namespace: `nvmet` file-backed namespace whose file lives on an fsdax filesystem on NVDIMM-N (or memmap-emulated pmem), exported over `nvmet-fc` (lpfc), `nvmet-tcp` or `nvmet-rdma`. The sender writes with io_uring `pwritev` O_DIRECT at `offset = slot.off` on `/dev/nvmeXnY`; a CQE means the kernel's DAX write path (`copy_from_iter_flushcache`) has placed the data in the persistence domain: ack level `Durable` with *no userspace receive path at all*. `can_reject=false`: stale-epoch records are rejected at replay time, and fencing is enforced by the control plane removing the host from the nvmet `allowed_hosts` ACL and disabling the port. Dual paths = two FC links + native NVMe multipath (ANA), zero code. The partner daemon `mmap`s the same DAX file and only needs `scan()` at takeover (shared-disk model) or continuously (full-replication model).
- **VerbsRing (Tier 3):** RC QP per link, arena registered as one MR (hugepage-backed, 2 MB pages so a 64 GB arena is 32K PTEs), `RDMA_WRITE_WITH_IMM` to the slot (imm = batch index), receiver polls CQ, `clwb`s the range (DDIO lands inbound writes in LLC), posts a `SEND` ack. Local completion alone would be ack level `Placed`; the `SEND` ack upgrades it to `Durable`.

##### Semantics
- **Ack:** `Placed` = in partner DRAM (survives partner-independent single-node failure); `Durable` = in partner pmem/PLP log after flush. The client ack requires local-durable AND partner-`Durable` when pmem is present, else partner-`Placed`, and the chosen level is a cluster-wide, logged configuration, not a transport accident.
- **Fencing:** every record and every RPC carries `epoch`; the control plane bumps it on ownership change; receivers that can reject (TcpRing, VerbsRing) reject in-line, BlockRing rejects at replay and via ACL.
- **Flow control:** credits are bytes of ring space. In the shared-disk model the sender frees space itself at each CP boundary (no partner round trip); in the full-replication model the partner publishes `consumed` (piggybacked on acks, or an 8-byte status block the sender reads every ~1 ms on BlockRing). The ring must hold ≥ 2 x CP-interval x ingest: at 1.25 GB/s and 10 s that is 25 GB per direction, so 64-96 GB of NVDIMM-N per node, or a 5 s CP cap.
- **Batching:** group commit driven by in-flight count, not timers: send immediately when nothing is in flight, otherwise coalesce up to `max_msg` (256 KiB) while one batch is outstanding. Adds ≤ 1 RTT under load, zero at idle.
- **Deadlines/timeouts:** per batch 200 ms (4 orders above the normal RTT); heartbeat records every 20 ms; link suspect at 3 misses, dead at 1 s; `fail_all` + redeliver on the other link (slot-idempotent, so no gaps/duplicates).
- **Partner unreachable:** `Mirrored` → `Stalled` (stop acking, queue, tell the control plane; bounded by `T_stall` ≈ 5 s and ring space) → either `Mirrored` again or, only after the control plane has fenced the partner (iDRAC power-off confirmed, or lease expiry + witness vote), `Unmirrored` (ack on local durability, CP boundaries logged, partner marked `needs_resync`). Never ack unmirrored on the data path's own authority; iSCSI/NVMe initiators tolerate 20-30 s of stall, so the control plane has a real budget.

##### What must not leak through `RemoteRing`
LBAs/NQNs/LUNs, rkeys/QPNs/fds, exchange IDs, frame/MTU sizes (only `max_msg`), delivery order, the transport's own retry policy (retries are always safe), and the ack level (advertised in `Caps`, never assumed). Raft/epoch semantics stay above: the transport carries the token, never interprets it.

##### Phase 3 RPC (N-Blade <-> D-Blade)
- Same-node: a `memfd`+`MFD_HUGETLB` arena mapped in both daemons; the N-Blade `recv`s iSCSI/NVMe-TCP data segments *directly into an arena slot*; the RPC is a 256 B SPSC ring entry (64 B header + inline args + slot reference), doorbell = eventfd after a 20-50 us spin; response ring back. ~2-5 us, zero payload copies from NIC to NVLOG to mirror to CP flush (`O_DIRECT` with registered buffers).
- Cross-node (LIF on A, volume owned by B): same header over a dedicated TCP/RDMA `RpcLink` built on the same link primitives as TcpRing, multiplexed by `req_id`; the payload crosses the wire, then B mirrors it back to A's ring (two crossings; acceptable because ALUA/ANA advertise the owner's N-Blade as Active/Optimized, so this is the minority path).
- Header: magic, version, type, flags, `req_id` (u64: nblade id, session epoch, counter), `epoch`/volume generation, deadline (us), len, crc. Idempotency: D-Blade keeps a 1K-entry per-session window; the mirror record carries `req_id` so a new owner dedupes after takeover. Cancel: `Cancel{req_id}` → `Aborted|Completed` (maps SCSI TMFs). Versioning: feature bitmap at `Hello`, unknown types → `Unsupported`.
- Ownership: N-Blade watches a control-plane stream of `vol → (owner, gen)`; every RPC carries `gen`; mismatch → `StaleOwner{owner, gen}` → refresh and retry (safe by `req_id`). The old owner self-fences at lease expiry before the new `gen` is served.
- Shared substrate: yes at the link/framing/CRC layer, no at the connection: mirror and RPC get separate connections (or QPs) with independent flow control so a slow RPC consumer cannot stall mirror acks.

#### Bottlenecks
##### Wire serialization (payload only; +2-5% framing)
| Link | 4 KiB | 64 KiB | Payload rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1GbE | 32.8 us | 524 us | 125 MB/s |
| 10GbE | 3.3 us | 52 us | 1,250 MB/s |
| 25GbE | 1.3 us | 21 us | 3,125 MB/s |
| 16GFC (14.025 Gb/s) | 2.6 us | 41 us | 1,600 MB/s |
| 32GFC | 1.3 us | 20 us | 3,200 MB/s |

##### Round trip for one mirror write (data + ack, QD1), my estimates
| Path | 4 KiB | 64 KiB |
|---|---|---|
| 1GbE TCP, interrupts | 90-110 us | 600-650 us |
| 10GbE TCP, interrupts, default coalescing | 40-55 us | 90-110 us |
| 10GbE TCP, busy-polled, coalescing off | 20-30 us | 75-85 us |
| 25GbE TCP busy-polled | 18-25 us | 40-50 us |
| 25GbE RoCEv2 write+imm (+SEND ack) | 5-7 (8-12) us | 26-30 us |
| 16GFC nvmet-fc → DAX file (in-box) | 50-100 us | 90-140 us |
| 16GFC tcm_qla2xxx+TCMU (busy-polled handler / tcmu-runner) | 80-150 / 150-300 us | +40 us |
| 16GFC SPDK userspace target | 25-50 us (unverified) | ~70 us |
FCP SCSI writes cost two round trips (CMND → XFER_RDY → DATA → RSP) plus HBA firmware latency per hop (~5-10 us); NVMe/FC with first burst saves one; neither gets near RDMA. The mirror RTT is added to every client write; with iSCSI itself at ~80-120 us on 10GbE, a busy-polled TCP mirror keeps a 4K client write around 150 us, the in-box FC path around 200-250 us.

##### Queue depth to saturate (bandwidth x RTT / size)
- 10GbE, 4K @ 40 us: 50 KB in flight ≈ 12 records; but 4K at line rate is 305k msg/s, beyond one connection's syscall budget → batching to 64-256 KiB is mandatory (5-20k batches/s is trivial).
- 10GbE, 64K @ 100 us: 125 KB ≈ 2 batches. 1GbE: 3-4 x 4K or 1-2 x 64K — the link is the bottleneck at 125 MB/s, below one HDD.
- 16GFC @ 80 us: 128 KB ≈ 2 x 64K; lpfc nvmet queue depth is ample. 25GbE RoCE @ 10 us: 31 KB ≈ 8 x 4K.

##### Bandwidth ceiling
Ingest ≤ CP flush rate. 24 x 7.2K SAS at ~120-150 MB/s with RAID6 (22 data) is 2.6-3.3 GB/s raw sequential; md raid5/6 with one `raid5d` thread realistically 1-1.5 GB/s. A single 10GbE mirror link (1.25 GB/s) is therefore the write ceiling of the pair, not the disks: the mirror needs 2 x 10GbE, 25GbE, or 16/32GFC. CP sizing follows: 1.25 GB/s x 10 s x 2 halves = 25 GB per direction of NVLOG.

##### Memory/CPU
Copies per 64K write on TcpRing: 1 (kernel → arena on receive) + the sender's skb copy unless `SEND_ZC`; memcpy at ~10 GB/s single-threaded costs ~6 us per 64K, fine. crc32c at 10+ GB/s is <0.5 us per 4K, <7 us per 64K: keep it (end-to-end integrity and torn-record detection on pmem; TCP's 16-bit checksum protects nothing). One busy-polled core per link per node (2-4 cores of 2 x 20+) is the price of the 20 us RTT.

##### Persistence
ADR-only platform: every receive path must `clwb+sfence` before claiming `Durable` (64K = 1,024 lines ≈ 1-2 us); RDMA inbound via DDIO sits in LLC until flushed. pmem store atomicity is 8 bytes: ring head/tail are 8-byte atomics written after the record is persistent, and records are validated by trailer CRC on replay.

#### Creative solutions
- **DAX file as the mirror target (BlockRing).** The partner's NVDIMM becomes addressable over FC, TCP and RDMA with in-box kernel targets and *no* userspace receive path: `nvmet` file-backed namespace on fsdax, so each NVMe write completes only after the DAX copy has flushed to the persistence domain. This replaces the brief's "SCSI WRITE to a mirror LUN through tcm_qla2xxx+TCMU" with something that needs no out-of-tree modules, gives dual-path via NVMe native multipath, and is semantically the FC-VI "remote DMA into NVRAM" NetApp does, minus their firmware. Same code path mirrors to a third site over NVMe/TCP for async DR later.
- **`memmap=` pmem emulation** on every dev box: the identical fsdax + flush code path runs on DRAM-backed fake pmem, so the persistence logic is exercised long before NVDIMM-N parts arrive.
- **Slot-idempotent records make multipath, mixed transports and redelivery free.** Two FC links, or FC primary + NVMe/TCP standby, are just two `RemoteRing`s in the `MirrorLog`'s link set; a failed link's unknown-fate batches are re-posted elsewhere with no dedupe logic.
- **Timer-free group commit**: batch only while a batch is in flight. Latency at idle is one RTT, throughput under load is line rate, no tunables.
- **Free-space accounting without the partner** in the shared-disk model: the owner frees ring space at its own CP boundary, so FC acks carry no information and that is fine.
- **Skip raw verbs in Tier 3 at first**: `nvmet-rdma` → DAX file is the same BlockRing at ~15-25 us; raw `RDMA_WRITE_WITH_IMM` is a 2-3x latency win that costs a verbs wrapper, MR management and a receiver hot loop. Do it only if the latency budget demands it.
- **NVMe passthrough via `IORING_OP_URING_CMD`** on the mirror namespace to skip the block layer (~2-3 us) once the BlockRing works.
- **Dual-use TCMU handler**: if QLogic hardware is mandated, a Rust TCMU handler serves both the FC mirror receiver and an iSCSI ingest front door on RHEL 9 (LIO iSCSI is in-box), so the out-of-tree surface is only `tcm_qla2xxx.ko`.
- **Lease-based self-fencing instead of a third data node**: a 2-node pair plus a tiny witness vote; the loser stops serving at lease expiry, the survivor starts after it, and iDRAC power-off is the belt to that suspender.

#### Holes in the plan
- **FC is not the fast path.** Every Linux FC target stack puts a 4K mirror write at 50-150 us; busy-polled 10GbE TCP is 20-30 us and 25GbE RoCE 5-10 us. The brief treats FC as "primary goal" because MetroCluster uses it, but MetroCluster uses FC-VI (ANSI FC-VI mapping, VI/RDMA semantics in adapter firmware) and NetApp's own firmware; stock QLogic/Emulex firmware exposes only FCP and FC-NVMe, and the FC transport's bsg interface allows ELS/CT passthrough, not a data-path ULP. Reframe FC as "isolation/distance/compatibility tier", not a latency tier, or the owner will be disappointed by measurements.
- **The implied QLogic+LIO route is out-of-tree on RHEL.** Verified: `CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX` and `CONFIG_SCSI_EFCT` are not set in RHEL 9 or RHEL 10 configs; `nvmet-fc` is. Either commit to Emulex + nvmet-fc (in-box), or commit to shipping our own kernel (every SAN vendor does, and it also unlocks ublk/io_uring features), but decide it now.
- **RAM-only write caching has a common-mode failure.** Two nodes in one rack on one PDU: a power event loses up to CP-interval x ingest (10-15 GB at 1 GB/s) of acknowledged writes. "RPO=0 on single-node failure" is satisfied, but a SAN customer reads "RPO=0" as "never loses acked data". Require NVDIMM-N (12 x 16 GB max, battery, two CPUs) or a PLP NVMe log (Optane P5800X at ~10-20 us FUA; a NAND PLP drive at 30-80 us) and define the ack level explicitly. Also: iDRAC must report NVDIMM battery state and the daemon must downgrade acks (ONTAP's "NVRAM battery low" behaviour) when it is bad.
- **2-node Raft cannot elect after one failure** (majority of 2 is 2). Add a witness vote (third small host/VM) or adopt ONTAP's non-Raft pair protocol (mailbox + hardware-assisted takeover). My transport reports link state; it must never be the tiebreaker.
- **Node-local disks break takeover.** With internal disks the partner holds only the last CP interval of writes, not the volumes; takeover is impossible. Either external dual-ported SAS JBODs (MD1400-class, both nodes attached, SCSI-3 PR fencing) or full synchronous replication where the partner continuously applies the mirror ring to its own array (DRBD economics: 2x disk writes, half capacity). The mirror stream is identical in both models; the partner-side consumer and free-space accounting differ, so the choice must be made before Phase 1 freezes flow control.
- **"Stateless N-Blade" is coherent only as "no durable state".** iSCSI sessions carry ISID/TSIH, CmdSN/StatSN windows, task sets and PDU reassembly; NVMe-oF controllers carry queue and ANA state. All of that dies with the N-Blade and initiators re-login (1-5 s), which is fine; persistent reservations, igroups/LUN maps and ANA group state must live in the D-Blade/cluster DB. SCSI ordered/head-of-queue tags require the N→D RPC to preserve per-session ordering when asked.
- **Roadmap ordering.** Phase 1 cannot be tested end to end without a front door (ublk on RHEL 10 or LIO+TCMU on RHEL 9) and a minimal RPC; the record format is shared by Phases 1 and 2. Define record + RPC headers in Phase 1 and build the N→D shared-memory ring as a Phase 1 deliverable.
- **1GbE prototyping hides the real problems.** At 125 MB/s and ~100 us RTT, batching, queue depth and core pinning never matter; results will not transfer to 10/25GbE. Prototype on 10GbE from day one (X710s are cheap).
- **io_uring is disabled by default on RHEL 9.4+** (`kernel.io_uring_disabled=2`); and RHEL 9's 5.14 io_uring lacks multishot/`SEND_ZC`/NAPI polling; ublk is absent. Rocky 10 is the practical floor for the data path.
- **The mirror link must exceed the array's flush rate**: one 10GbE link caps pair ingest at 1.25 GB/s; plan 2 x 10GbE, 25GbE or 16/32GFC.
- **Direct-connect FC target mode is the biggest unknown in my area.** qla2xxx N2N target mode received fixes in 4.16-5.5 and dual mode fixes in 5.15; lpfc nvmet in point-to-point topology between two Linux HBAs is undocumented. 16G/32G HBAs do not run arbitrated loop (I believe; verify in the Marvell/Broadcom user guides), so direct attach is P2P only. Budget two small FC switches (used 16G Brocade) as the fallback and run the spike early.
- **"One massive contiguous stripe" is a D-Blade claim, but from the transport side**: replay after takeover applies logical records (vol, LBA, data) through the partner's allocator, so the record must be logical, not physical-extent based, or the partner cannot replay into its own layout.

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build
1. The record format (64 B header + trailer CRC) and the `MirrorLog` layer: seq/epoch, slot allocation, timer-free batching, deadlines, link set with redelivery, the Mirrored/Stalled/Unmirrored/Resync state machine, and `LocalRing` on DRAM, memmap-emulated pmem and (if available) NVDIMM-N with `clwb+sfence`.
2. `TcpRing`: pinned thread per link, blocking `sendmsg`/`readv` into the arena, busy-poll sysctls; keep io_uring for later.
3. `BlockRing`: io_uring `pwritev` O_DIRECT to an NVMe namespace; receiver = `nvmet-tcp` + file-backed namespace on fsdax (emulated pmem on dev boxes). This is the Tier 2 path, proven in Phase 1 on Ethernet.
4. Front door and harness: ublk device (`libublk`, Rocky 10) feeding the NVLOG; fio; a fault injector (kill partner daemon, `ip link down`, iDRAC power cycle, battery-state simulation).
5. The same-node N→D shared-memory ring and RPC header (Phase 3 seed), so the ublk front door talks to the D-Blade the way the N-Blade will.
6. Parallel two-week FC spike on target hardware: LPe32002 pair, direct-connect, `nvmet-fc` + DAX file; measure; repeat with QLE2692 + self-built `tcm_qla2xxx` + TCMU only if Emulex fails; decide the Tier 2 HBA by data, and whether switches are needed.

##### Success criteria (all on 10GbE unless stated, two R740xd-class nodes)
- 4 KiB QD1 write via ublk → NVLOG → mirror → ack: p50 ≤ 60 us, p99 ≤ 150 us with `TcpRing`; ≤ 120 us p50 with `BlockRing` over nvmet-tcp.
- 64 KiB QD8: ≥ 1.1 GB/s mirrored (≥ 90% of 10GbE); 4 KiB QD32: ≥ 200k records/s with batching, sender and receiver each on one core.
- RPO=0 proof: 10,000 iterations of "client ack observed → owner power-cycled via iDRAC → partner replays" with zero acked writes missing and zero unacked writes reported acked; run on DRAM (expect pass), on emulated/real pmem with both nodes power-cycled (expect pass on pmem, documented loss on DRAM).
- Link cut mid-stream: data path enters Stalled within 200 ms, control plane notified, redelivery over a second link completes with no gaps/duplicates (verified by seq audit); no acks during Stalled.
- Epoch fencing: a writer with a stale epoch gets 100% rejection on TcpRing/BlockRing-replay and ACL removal on nvmet within 1 s of a fence decision.
- FC spike: 4 KiB QD1 and 64 KiB QD8 numbers for nvmet-fc direct-connect recorded; go/no-go on direct connect vs switches.
- Zero copies on the N→D path demonstrated by counters (iSCSI/ublk payload lands once in the arena, mirrored and flushed from the same pages).

#### Open questions
- **Shared external SAS shelves or node-local disks?** Unblocks the partner-side consumer design (scan-at-takeover vs continuous apply), free-space accounting, and the resync protocol. Node-local means full synchronous replication; say so to the owner.
- **Will the nodes have NVDIMM-N (plus battery) or Optane PMem, or a PLP NVMe for the log, or neither?** Unblocks the ack-level definition (`Placed` vs `Durable`) and the ring sizing (how many GB per direction).
- **Rocky 9 or Rocky 10, and may the appliance ship its own kernel?** Unblocks ublk vs LIO front door, io_uring use, and whether `tcm_qla2xxx`/QLogic is even on the table.
- **Is there existing FC hardware (vendor/model) or is Emulex LPe32002 acceptable?** Unblocks the in-box `nvmet-fc` path vs out-of-tree QLogic path.
- **Is direct-connect mandatory, or may we require a pair of FC switches?** Unblocks the N2N spike scope and the redundancy topology (two dual-port HBAs per node vs switches).
- **May a third witness host (or VM elsewhere) exist?** Unblocks the quorum/fencing model the transport's "partner unreachable" behaviour depends on.
- **Client write latency target (p50/p99) and the mirror bandwidth budget?** Unblocks busy-polling core reservations and whether 25GbE RoCE is required now.
- **Node separation: same rack, same building, or metro distance?** Unblocks timeouts, heartbeat cadence and whether FC long-wave optics are the real reason for Tier 2.

#### Load-bearing claims
- [NETWORK-1] (high) The RHEL 9 and RHEL 10 x86_64 kernel configs (Rocky r9/r10 kernel-x86_64-rhel.config) contain '# CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX is not set' and '# CONFIG_SCSI_EFCT is not set', so no in-box FC SCSI target module exists for QLogic or Emulex HBAs on Rocky 9 or 10.
  - why it matters: Drives the recommendation to use nvmet-fc on Emulex rather than LIO/tcm_qla2xxx on QLogic, and the warning that a QLogic path requires shipping our own kernel modules.
- [NETWORK-2] (high) The same RHEL 9 and RHEL 10 configs build CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FC=m (plus _TCP and _RDMA), and in-tree lpfc compiles its NVMe target support (lpfc_enable_nvmet) whenever NVME_TARGET_FC is enabled, so an Emulex LPe31002/LPe32002 can act as an in-box NVMe/FC target on Rocky 9 or 10.
  - why it matters: The entire BlockRing-over-FC design depends on an in-box FC target.
- [NETWORK-3] (medium) A write to a file on an fsdax filesystem backed by pmem completes through the DAX copy path (copy_from_iter_flushcache, non-temporal stores plus flush), so an nvmet file-backed namespace on fsdax acknowledges an NVMe write only after the data is in the persistence domain, without any userspace receiver.
  - why it matters: This is what makes BlockRing a 'Durable' ack level with no receive-side daemon hot path; if nvmet's file backend does not take the DAX path (e.g. O_DIRECT handling on DAX files), the receiver needs a flush step or a different backend.
- [NETWORK-4] (high) RHEL 9.4 and later ship kernel.io_uring_disabled=2 by default (io_uring_setup fails with EPERM), while RHEL 10 builds ublk as a module (CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m) and RHEL 9's 5.14-based kernel has no ublk at all.
  - why it matters: Determines the Rocky 10 recommendation for the data path and the ublk front door for Phase 1.
- [NETWORK-5] (medium) A 4 KiB request/ack round trip over kernel TCP on 10GbE is roughly 40-55 us interrupt-driven and 20-30 us busy-polled, versus 5-7 us for an RDMA write-with-immediate on 25GbE ConnectX-5 and 50-150 us for an FC write through a Linux kernel target stack; serialization alone is 32.8 us per 4 KiB on 1GbE, 3.3 us on 10GbE, 2.6 us on 16GFC (14.025 Gbit/s).
  - why it matters: Underpins the claim that FC is not the latency tier and the queue-depth/batching requirements.
- [NETWORK-6] (medium) qla2xxx supports per-port qlini_mode 'dual' (initiator and target on one port) and direct-connect N2N target mode in mainline kernels since about 5.5 (with a dual-mode fix in 5.15), but N2N target mode has needed repeated fixes and dual-mode N2N between two Linux qla2xxx ports, and lpfc nvmet target in point-to-point topology, are unproven.
  - why it matters: Direct-connect FC without a switch is the owner's implied topology; if it does not work, Tier 2 needs switches or a different redundancy design.
- [NETWORK-7] (high) Skylake/Cascade Lake Xeons with NVDIMM-N provide ADR but not eADR, so data in CPU caches (including DDIO-targeted LLC for inbound RDMA/NIC writes) is not persistent until CLWB/CLFLUSHOPT plus SFENCE; the R740xd supports at most 12 x 16 GB NVDIMM-N with a battery and two CPUs.
  - why it matters: Defines the receive-side flush step required before a 'Durable' ack and the NVLOG ring sizing ceiling.
- [NETWORK-8] (medium) A single 10GbE link (1,250 MB/s payload) is below the sequential flush capability of a 24-drive SAS RAID6 array (roughly 2.6-3.3 GB/s raw, 1-1.5 GB/s through md's single raid5d thread), so the mirror link, not the disks, is the pair's write ceiling unless 2 x 10GbE, 25GbE or 16/32GFC is used.
  - why it matters: Drives the link-count/speed recommendation and the NVLOG ring size (2 x CP interval x ingest).

### Opus-Storage

#### Executive take
I would build the D-Blade as three things in this order of importance: (1) a persistent, HA-mirrored, append-only NVLOG of operation records that carry the data, which is the only durability mechanism in the system; (2) an in-RAM extent index over those records that gives read-your-writes and sorted CP assembly; (3) a log-structured segment store on md RAID6 written only as full stripes, with a copy-on-write extent map persisted as checkpoint-plus-deltas, and an NVMe tier that is not optional (NVLOG, hot map pages, read cache). The single biggest risk is the brief's throughput premise: 2x10GbE ingests about 2.2 GB/s, a 12-HDD RAID6 absorbs about 1.4 GB/s before garbage collection and 0.7-0.9 GB/s after it, and 12-24 HDDs deliver only 1-6K random read IOPS, so the write cache buys latency and roughly one minute of burst absorption, not sustained "network speed"; the plan must say so and must budget SSDs. What I disagree with most is the durability/HA framing: a RAM write cache mirrored to the partner is neither RPO=0 under dual power loss nor useful on takeover with node-local disks (the survivor holds the last 10-15 s of writes but none of the flushed data), so the real choices are dual-ported SAS shelves (ONTAP's model) or a full shadow replica fed by the NVLOG mirror, plus PMem/NVDIMM-N/PLP-NVMe for the log. Phase 1 should therefore fix the NVLOG record format, local persistence and replay before any transport work, because that record format is the contract the Fibre Channel transport will later have to carry.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Rust crates (data path)
| Crate | Use | Maturity (my view) | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `io-uring` (tokio-rs, 0.7.x) | raw SQ/CQ for O_DIRECT writes to `/dev/mdX` and the NVLOG NVMe; `WRITE_FIXED` with registered arena/staging buffers, `FSYNC`, `IOPOLL` for NVMe, optional SQPOLL | mature, thin, unsafe-heavy; the base of monoio/tokio-uring; I would hand-roll a thread-per-core reactor on it rather than adopt a runtime | kernel >= 5.1 for every op used; on Rocky 9.4+ `kernel.io_uring_disabled` defaults to 2 (verified in the RHEL 9.4 release notes) so it must be set to 0, and SELinux's `io_uring` class must allow it |
| `rustix` / `libc` | mmap/mlock/madvise, `pwritev2(RWF_DSYNC)`, `fdatasync`, `BLK*` ioctls, sysfs | mature | — |
| `memmap2` | map `/dev/dax0.0` (devdax) or an fsdax file for a PMem NVLOG | mature | libnvdimm/NFIT (4.2+), devdax (4.7+); `ndctl`/`daxctl` are in Rocky 9 AppStream |
| `asm!` (std) | `clwb`/`clflushopt`/`sfence` and non-temporal copies for PMem persistence | stable since Rust 1.59; `_mm_clwb` is **not** in stable or nightly `core::arch` (both doc pages 404), `_mm_clflush` (SSE2) is the only stable fallback | Cascade Lake is ADR-only (no eADR): CPU caches are not in the persistence domain |
| `crc32c` (SSE4.2) or `crc-fast` | per-4 KiB block CRC and NVLOG record CRC; ~10+ GB/s per core | `crc32c` mature; `crc-fast` newer, faster (PCLMULQDQ/AVX-512), verify | — |
| `xxhash-rust` (XXH3-128), `blake3` | metadata page/superblock checksums; scrub digests | mature | — |
| `parking_lot`, `crossbeam` (SPSC/MPSC queues, epoch), `arc-swap` | shard locks; per-core queues between ingest, NVLOG writer and mirror cores; RCU-style swap of active/frozen dirty sets | mature | — |
| `std::collections::BTreeMap<u64, Extent>` (hand-rolled split-on-overlap), `rangemap` 1.5 as a reference | the dirty index keyed by start LBA: ordered range scans (CP assembly, overlap resolution) are the dominant operation; ART/radix (`blart`, `art-tree`) wins point lookups but not ordered scans, and interval trees are unnecessary because normalized extents never overlap | BTreeMap mature; `rangemap` moderate; ART crates low | — |
| `zerocopy` / `bytemuck` | `repr(C)` NVLOG records, segment summaries, superblock | mature | — |
| `slab`, `bytes` | **not** on the data path: slots must be page-aligned, pinned, io_uring-registered memory; `bytes` refcounting fights registered buffers. `bytes`/`tokio`/`serde`+`postcard` for the control plane only | mature | — |
| `hdrhistogram`, `metrics`/`prometheus` | per-stage latency (ingest, local persist, mirror ACK, CP) | mature | — |
| `proptest`, `loom`, `cargo-fuzz`, Miri, `cargo-careful` | the arena and index crates get all four | mature | — |

##### Kernel subsystems and versions
- **md raid456**: full-stripe writes skip read-modify-write when the stripe is fully covered in the stripe cache. Tunables: `stripe_cache_size` (max 32768; 32768 x 4 KiB x 12 members = 1.6 GB), `group_thread_cnt` (3.12+), `skip_copy` (3.16+). FUA/PREFLUSH are honored and forwarded to members (I believe raid5 FUA support dates to 2.6.37; verify). PPL: 4.12+, RAID5 only, max 64 devices, 30-40% write penalty (kernel docs, verified). Journal: 4.4 write-through, 4.10 write-back, RAID4/5/6; write-back journal failure degrades to write-through, write-through journal failure re-exposes the write hole (kernel docs/commits, verified).
- **Block direct I/O**: `dio_bio_write_op()` in `block/fops.c` (6.12, verified) sets `REQ_FUA` on a DSYNC iocb, so `O_DIRECT|O_DSYNC` or `RWF_DSYNC` on `/dev/mdX` gives FUA writes; the 5.14 equivalent is in `fs/block_dev.c` (verify).
- **libnvdimm/NFIT, devdax**: present in 5.14 and 6.12. **libata FUA**: historically off by default (`libata.fua`), SATA FUA may be emulated by write+flush; the design relies on flush ordering, FUA is only an optimization.
- **ublk** (6.0+): present in Rocky 10 (6.12); I believe absent from Rocky 9's 5.14 (verify `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK`). Not needed by the D-Blade itself.
- **9 vs 10**: the D-Blade runs on both; prefer Rocky 10 (io_uring sane defaults, newer md, ublk, 6.x block layer). Rocky 9 is acceptable with the sysctl and SELinux boolean.

##### Hardware (NVLOG and shelves)
- NVDIMM-N on R740xd: 16 GB modules, max 12 = 192 GB, dual-CPU with Gold/Platinum-class CPUs per the VMware HCL entry, battery kit incompatible with the 2.5in mid-drive tray (third-party spec sheets + Dell NVDIMM-N guide; verify with Dell's memory guide). Intel Optane PMem 100 (Cascade Lake only, 128/256/512 GB, up to 12 modules in Dell's SQL test config; EOL, used market). Optane SSD P4800X (30 DWPD; ~2 GB/s write, ~10 us; verify) and P5800X (100 DWPD, 292 PBW at 1.6 TB, 6.2-7.4 GB/s write; verified). NAND PLP NVMe (Micron 7450 PRO/MAX, Kioxia CD6/CD8, Samsung PM9A3): 1-3 DWPD.
- HBA330 (SAS3008, PCIe 3.0 x8, internal ports only). External JBOD needs the Dell 12Gb SAS HBA (LSI 9300-8e class) or H830/H840; MD1400 (12x3.5) / MD1420 (24x2.5) with dual EMMs, "redundant path/unified" mode, spec sheets list SAS/NL-SAS only, SATA not listed (verify with Dell's support matrix).

Sources: [RHEL 9.4 kernel parameter changes](https://docs.redhat.com/fr/documentation/Red_Hat_Enterprise_Linux/9/html/9.4_release_notes/kernel_parameters_changes), [io_uring_disabled sysctl commit](https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/2308.2/00994.html), [md PPL docs](https://docs.kernel.org/driver-api/md/raid5-ppl.html), [md raid5-cache docs](https://docs.kernel.org/7.1/_sources/driver-api/md/raid5-cache.rst.txt), [Dell NVDIMM-N user guide](https://pcserverandparts.com/content/Spec%20Sheets/Servers/Dell/Dell%20PowerEdge%20R740XD/Dell%20EMC%20NVDIMM-N%20Persistent%20Memory%20User%20Guide.pdf), [VMware PMem HCL table](https://kb.vmware.com/s/article/54444), [MD1420](https://www.dell.com/en-in/shop/povw/storage-md1420), [P5800X review](https://www.storagereview.com/review/intel-optane-ssd-p5800x-review), [Lenovo P5800X](https://lenovopress.lenovo.com/LP1484), [Dell R740xd Optane PMem](https://aws.principledtechnologies.com/Dell/PowerEdge-R740xd-Intel-Optane-1019.pdf), [ublk docs](https://docs.kernel.org/6.5/block/ublk.html), [Rust _mm_clflush](https://doc.rust-lang.org/nightly/core/arch/x86/fn._mm_clflush.html).

#### Proposed architecture
##### Principle
The NVLOG is the source of truth until a CP commits; the DRAM arena and the dirty index are a cache of it and are rebuildable by replay. Everything below follows from that.

##### Components and write flow (one process per aggregate, thread-per-core)
1. **Ingest**: `WriteOp{vol, lba, len, data, crc[]}` arrives from the N-Blade with per-4 KiB CRC32C computed at the edge. Take the 1 GiB LBA-shard lock, assign a per-volume monotonic `seq`, copy into arena slots (4 KiB slots, 64 KiB slabs for large writes), build a 4 KiB-aligned NVLOG record.
2. **Local persist** and **mirror append** are issued concurrently: PMem = non-temporal copy + `sfence`, then header with `seq` written last (8-byte atomic, the commit marker), `clwb`+`sfence`; NVMe = O_DIRECT, IOPOLL. Mirror = `LogTransport::append` to the partner's mirror ring.
3. When both complete: insert into the dirty index under the shard lock (superseded extents release their slots: overwrite coalescing is free), then ACK. Visibility only after durability, so a read can never observe a write that a takeover would lose.
4. **Partner side**: append the record to its mirror ring, CRC-check, ACK. It does not index or copy; takeover replays the ring into its own dirty index (6M records per CP at ~1 us each: ~6 s per CP of replay, plus CRC at ~10 GB/s).

Interface sketch:
```rust
#[repr(C)] struct RecHdr { magic: u32, hdr_crc: u32, seq: u64, cp_epoch: u32, op: u16 /*Write|Discard|Caw|Snap|Clone*/,
                           vol: u16, lba: u64, len: u32, data_crc: u32 }          // 4 KiB-aligned record, payload follows
trait NvLog      { fn append(&self, h: &RecHdr, data: &[SlotRef]) -> impl Future<Output = Seq>;
                   fn retire(&self, upto: Seq); fn replay(&self, from: Seq) -> impl Iterator<Item = Record>; }
trait LogTransport { fn append(&self, rec: &Record) -> impl Future<Output = Result<Seq, Gap>>;   // "remote append-only log"
                     fn retire(&self, upto: Seq); fn resync(&self, from: Seq) -> impl Future<Output = ()>; }
struct Extent    { lba: u64, len: u32, slot: SlotRef /*idx+generation*/, seq: u64, sector_mask: u8, crc: SmallVec<[u32; 4]> }
struct DirtyIndex { shards: Vec<RwLock<BTreeMap<u64, Extent>>> }   // per volume; keyed by start LBA; split-on-overlap
impl DirtyIndex  { fn insert(&self, e: Extent) -> Vec<SlotRef>;      // returns superseded slots
                   fn lookup(&self, lba: u64, len: u32) -> Vec<Piece>; fn drain_sorted(self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Extent>; }
```
The mirror semantic is deliberately "append record, get seq back", not general RPC: it maps to a TCP stream, to an RDMA WRITE into the partner's ring, or to a SCSI WRITE at a ring offset on an FC-exported LUN.

##### Memory layout and accounting
- Per-socket pinned arenas (`mlock`, 2 MiB THP via `madvise`, `MADV_DONTDUMP`), registered with io_uring. Ingest and NVLOG threads pinned to the socket owning the client NIC and the PMem; CP flush threads on the HBA330's socket. Slot handles carry a generation counter; the arena crate is the only `unsafe` crate, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` elsewhere.
- Two dirty generations, `active` and `frozen`, swapped with `arc-swap` at CP start. Reads check active, then frozen, then the retained-slot read cache.
- Budget: ingest at 2x10GbE is ~2.2 GB/s of payload, so 22 GB per 10 s CP (33 GB at 15 s); active+frozen = 44-66 GB; if the partner mirror ring is in DRAM rather than PMem, add 44-66 GB; index ~6M extents x ~64 B = 0.4 GB per CP. A 384 GB node is comfortable, 768 GB leaves room for a large RAM read cache.
- Backpressure: CP triggers at 10 s, or dirty >= 50% of budget, or NVLOG ring >= 50%. At 90% the ingest cores delay completions with a token bucket set to the measured flush rate (never fail a write; keep stalls under ~5 s, well inside the 30 s initiator timeouts). Partial 4 KiB writes (512e LUNs) are stored with a sector mask and filled from disk at CP time in a batched, PBA-sorted read pass, off the client latency path. Large sequential writes still go through the NVLOG in v1 (RPO=0 requires it); a direct-to-segment bypass is a v2 optimization.

##### CP commit protocol
1. Swap active/frozen; new records carry `cp_epoch+1`.
2. Drain frozen sorted by (vol, lba) into stripe-sized staging buffers (full stripes only, pad the last one); write segments with `WRITE_FIXED`, 16-32 stripes in flight; each segment ends with a summary stripe (vol, lba, seq, CRC per block).
3. Write the map delta run for this CP (sorted (vol, lba, len, pba, crc) tuples, 24 B each: 6M x 24 B = 144 MB, 0.6% of a 22 GB CP) and any merged map leaves/checkpoint pages into the log.
4. `fdatasync(md)` = PREFLUSH to every member.
5. Write the superblock stripe with FUA (DSYNC): one full stripe per slot (A/B alternating), the 4 KiB superblock replicated in every chunk, so a torn write never leaves zero valid copies; `fdatasync` again.
6. `NvLog::retire(seq_max_of_CP)` locally and on the partner; release frozen slots to the read-cache LRU.

##### On-disk layout (md RAID6, 12-24 HDDs)
- Chunk 256-512 KiB (each member sees >= 256 KiB I/Os), stripe = 10 x chunk (12 drives) = 2.5-5 MiB; segment = 64-128 stripes = 256-512 MiB. Segment table in RAM: 100 TB / 320 MB = ~330K entries x 32 B = 10 MB.
- RAID6 over RAID10 because log-structured full-stripe writes remove parity's write penalty while keeping 83% capacity; RAID10 only if read IOPS matter more than capacity and no SSD read cache is bought. A 24-bay chassis gets two 12-drive arrays (two aggregates, two failure domains, two parallel CP streams).
- Write hole: data stripes are never overwritten in place and only become referenced after step 4-5, so a torn data stripe is unreferenced garbage. The only in-place structure is the superblock, handled by replication-in-stripe. No PPL (RAID5-only anyway), no journal device (write-through journal is a bandwidth SPOF). Write-intent bitmap with a large bitmap chunk to bound resync after a crash.
- Checksums: CRC32C per 4 KiB stored in both the map entry (free at read time) and the segment summary (for GC and scrub without map lookups). End-to-end: computed at the N-Blade, verified at mirror receipt, before the CP write, and on every read.
- Map: per-volume CoW B+tree, LBA -> (pba, len, crc[]), persisted as periodic checkpoint pages plus per-CP delta runs; recovery = root from superblock + apply deltas since the checkpoint. Hot leaves in RAM, cold leaves on the NVMe tier; GC rewrites adjacent live extents contiguously, which merges entries.
- GC: Sprite-LFS cost-benefit selection, hot/cold separation, runs inside CPs when free segments < 20%; reads whole segments sequentially (0.2 s per 320 MB at 1.4 GB/s) so the cost is bandwidth share, not seeks. Frees are explicit (overwrite, UNMAP, snapshot deletion via deadlists) so `live_bytes` per segment is exact.

##### Read path
Active -> frozen -> retained slots -> NVMe read cache (2x3.84 TB, 16 KiB lines, hash index ~4 GB RAM) -> map -> HDD at QD 8-16 per member. Sequential-stream detector prefetches physical runs; a sequential logical range that needs more than k physical I/Os is queued for read-reallocation (rewritten contiguously in the next CP, essentially free in a log structure).

##### HA data model (weighed in the holes/creative sections)
Production: dual-ported SAS shelves, md assembled on one node, fenced by iDRAC + SCSI-3 PR; takeover = assemble + replay mirror ring + CP (10-30 s). Software must also support a shadow replica built from the mirror ring for node-local-disk deployments.

##### Client consistency
A write is durable when its record is persisted locally and ACKed by the partner (or, in degraded mode, by policy). SYNCHRONIZE CACHE and FUA are no-ops. Ordering: per-volume `seq` assigned under the shard lock; overlapping concurrent writes resolve by `seq`, replay applies in `seq`. COMPARE AND WRITE executes atomically under the shard lock and logs the result. Snapshots: a `Snap` record, taken at the next CP boundary, pins a root (O(1), crash-consistent across all LUNs of the volume); clones are writable children; deadlists make freeing and GC correct.

#### Bottlenecks
##### Sustained write throughput (the premise)
- Ingest: 2x10GbE = 2.5 GB/s raw, ~2.2 GB/s of iSCSI/NVMe-TCP payload = 22 GB per 10 s CP.
- Absorb: 12x 7.2K 3.5in RAID6 (10 data): 10 x 230 MB/s outer = 2.3 GB/s, 10 x 110 inner = 1.1 GB/s, mid-platter ~1.6, after md overhead ~1.4 GB/s. GC write amplification = 1/(1-u); cleaned-segment utilization u = 0.3-0.5 gives 1.4-2x, so net 0.7-0.9 GB/s (5.6-7 Gb/s, under one 10GbE link). 24x 10K SAS 2.5in: ~22 x 160 = 3.5 GB/s raw, ~1.7-2.2 GB/s net.
- Consequence: a 22 GB CP takes 16 s at 1.4 GB/s and 24-32 s with GC, so CP duration exceeds the interval at line rate ("back-to-back CPs"); the dirty set grows at 1.3-1.5 GB/s and a 64 GB budget lasts ~45 s. After that, throughput equals the array rate. Line-rate sustained writes need 36-48 spindles per node, i.e. external shelves.

##### Random reads
- 7.2K HDD: ~12 ms unloaded random I/O = ~80 IOPS at QD1, 150-200 at QD32. 12 drives = 1-2.4K IOPS (4-10 MB/s at 4 KiB); 24x 10K = ~6K. A 10GbE link can carry 300K 4 KiB IOPS. Without an SSD read cache the read side is 100-1000x off the "network speed" claim. With a 95% hit rate on NVMe: 0.95 x 0.2 ms + 0.05 x 12 ms = 0.8 ms mean; at 90%: 1.4 ms.

##### Indirection map size
- 100 TB at average 64 KiB extents: 1.6G x 24 B = 38 GB (RAM-cacheable); at 16 KiB: 154 GB; at 4 KiB worst case: 600 GB. The map lives on the NVMe tier with a RAM cache; this is the central scaling problem of every log-structured block store and the main reason the SSD tier is mandatory. Random 4 KiB writes across a huge volume also touch one map leaf per write (up to 2x metadata amplification) unless deltas are logged and leaves merged lazily, which the design does.

##### NVLOG bandwidth and endurance
- Each node's NVLOG device takes its own ingest plus the partner's mirror: up to 2 x ingest. P4800X writes ~2 GB/s (two devices or a P5800X needed at line rate; the R740xd's PCIe 3.0 x4 caps a P5800X at ~3.3 GB/s). PMem: 6 interleaved Optane PMem 100 modules per socket ~8-12 GB/s; NVDIMM-N: DRAM speed.
- Endurance at a modest 100 MB/s average own + 100 MB/s partner = 17 TB/day: 1.6 TB 3-DWPD NAND (8.8 PBW) lasts ~1.4 years; 750 GB P4800X (41 PBW) ~6.6 years; 800 GB P5800X (146 PBW) ~23 years; PMem/NVDIMM-N unlimited. At 500 MB/s average, NAND dies in months. NAND NVLOG is only acceptable for light average ingest.

##### Other limits
- md raid456: `stripe_cache_size` default 256 (12 MB) starves large sequential writes; set 16384-32768 (0.8-1.6 GB). RAID6 parity on AVX2/AVX-512 is >10 GB/s per core, not a limit; `group_thread_cnt` for parallel stripe handling.
- Takeover replay: 2 CPs = 44-66 GB of records; ~6 s per CP for index rebuild plus CRC at ~10 GB/s; 15-30 s total, comparable to ONTAP.
- Transport on the ACK path: 10GbE kernel TCP ~40-80 us RTT plus 52 us serialization per 64 KiB; 1GbE ~100-200 us plus 520 us per 64 KiB, bandwidth-bound at 110 MB/s; FC 16G ~2 RTT per SCSI write (~30-60 us) plus 40 us per 64 KiB; RoCE RDMA write ~10-30 us. Local persist: PMem 5-10 us per 64 KiB, Optane SSD 30-40 us, NAND PLP 50-100 us. D-Blade-side 4 KiB write ACK on 10GbE: 100-200 us; client-observed via iSCSI: 200-400 us.
- UPI: cross-socket copies at ~20 GB/s/direction are fine for data; keep the NVLOG path socket-local because PMem interleave sets are per socket.

#### Creative solutions
- **Shadow replica for free**: the partner already receives every mutation record in its mirror ring. Instead of discarding records at `retire`, the partner can apply them to a shadow log-structured volume on its own array (a "shadow CP"). Full data mirroring then costs zero extra network traffic, only partner array bandwidth and capacity; on takeover the shadow is promoted. This is the only way to get RPO=0 plus data availability with node-local disks, and it degrades gracefully to "no shadow" for a reduced-availability deployment.
- **PMem as the arena**: with NVDIMM-N/Optane PMem, DAX-map the NVLOG and let the dirty index point into it; the CP reads directly from PMem (6-8 GB/s read per socket). No DRAM copy of dirty data, DRAM requirement halves, and an arena corruption bug cannot destroy the durable copy because there is only one copy.
- **Superblock-in-a-stripe**: write each superblock slot as a full stripe with the 4 KiB superblock repeated in every chunk; recovery reads both slots and takes the highest CP with a valid checksum from any chunk. Removes the one in-place overwrite that the RAID6 write hole could hurt, with no PPL/journal and no partition games.
- **Zoned checksum option**: if map-resident CRCs prove too costly, WAFL-style zoned checksum blocks (1 checksum block per 63 data blocks in the same sequential stream) cost 1.6% capacity and no seeks.
- **Read reallocation**: fragmentation from aging is repaired opportunistically; blocks read by a detected sequential stream that needed many physical I/Os are rewritten contiguously in the next CP. Writes are free in a log structure, so defragmentation is a side effect of reading.
- **FC mirror as SCSI WRITE into a PMem ring**: export the partner's mirror ring (an fsdax file) as a LIO fileio/iblock LUN over tcm_qla2xxx; the sender writes records at ring offsets with SCSI WRITE+FUA; the partner daemon polls the mapped ring. The receive path is kernel-only (no tcmu-runner), which is why the transport abstraction should be "remote append-only log" from day one. Mark: plausible, unverified; the transport specialist should attack it.
- **Two aggregates per 24-bay node**: two 12-drive RAID6 arrays, two CP streams, two failure domains; also halves rebuild blast radius (16 TB rebuild at ~150 MB/s is ~30 h).
- **Adaptive CP sizing**: CP every min(10 s, time for dirty >= 50% budget) and an "idle CP" after 2 s of quiet; shorter CPs cut replay time, RAM, and the size of the window a dual failure could lose if NVLOG is ever DRAM-only.
- **Discard is the cheapest GC**: honor UNMAP/TRIM from guests as log records; freed extents raise cleaned-segment free ratio and directly reduce write amplification.
- **2-node quorum without a third node**: ONTAP solves it with mailbox disks on shared shelves; with shelves that is available here too (SCSI-3 PR on a mailbox drive plus iDRAC STONITH). With node-local disks, a tiny witness (VM or iDRAC-reachable third host) is required; a 2-node Raft alone cannot make progress with one member down.

#### Holes in the plan
- **"RAM write cache" is not RPO=0.** ECC DRAM survives bit flips, not power loss; dual PSU + UPS is policy. Under simultaneous dual power loss (shared PDU, site event, a firmware bug halting both) a DRAM-only design loses up to two CPs of ACKed writes and the clients never learn it. Fix: NVLOG on persistent media (Optane PMem > NVDIMM-N > Optane SSD > NAND PLP) on both nodes; DRAM-only is a prototype mode that must be labelled.
- **"Network speed" is a latency claim, not a throughput claim.** The arithmetic above: 12 HDDs absorb 0.7-0.9 GB/s net, 1-2.4K random read IOPS. Fix: re-scope the objective to "ACK at SSD-class latency, absorb bursts, serve reads from an SSD tier", and plan spindle counts (shelves) for sustained throughput.
- **Node-local disks are incompatible with ONTAP-style takeover.** The survivor can replay the mirrored log but cannot read the partner's flushed data. Fix: shelves (MD1400/MD1420 + external 12Gb SAS HBAs, SAS drives only; roughly $0.5-1.5K per used shelf) for production; shadow replica (50% capacity, partner array takes 2x writes) for node-local; or a written reduced-availability statement.
- **Phase ordering hides the real decisions.** Phase 1 ("intercept writes into RAM, mirror") precedes the persistence-medium and record-format decisions, yet both constrain the transport (record framing, ring semantics, retire/resync). Fix: Phase 1 defines the NVLOG record and ring protocol; the transport carries it.
- **1GbE prototyping proves nothing about the D-Blade.** 110 MB/s ingest is 1.1 GB per CP; any HDD array absorbs that in a second and all throughput, GC and backpressure problems stay invisible. Fix: drive the D-Blade with an in-process load generator at 2+ GB/s independent of the fabric.
- **The write hole is not solved by "mdadm as a single block device".** The bitmap only shortens resync. PPL is RAID5-only with a 30-40% penalty; a journal device is a bandwidth SPOF. Fix: log-structured full-stripe writes plus superblock-in-a-stripe (above); verify md honors FUA/PREFLUSH through raid456 on the chosen kernel.
- **io_uring is disabled on Rocky 9.4+ by default** (`kernel.io_uring_disabled=2`); SELinux also gates it. Fix: document the sysctl/boolean or choose Rocky 10.
- **NVLOG endurance on NAND** is a time bomb at moderate sustained ingest (numbers above). Fix: Optane or PMem, or an explicit ingest ceiling.
- **The map is the real memory consumer**, not the write cache: 38-600 GB for 100 TB depending on fragmentation. Fix: NVMe-resident map with RAM cache; GC-driven extent merging.
- **Partial-block and misaligned writes** cost a disk read each at CP time (~10 ms). Fix: 512e with 4 KiB physical exponent so guests align; consider 4Kn for NVMe-oF namespaces (ESXi support for 4Kn over iSCSI/FC is doubtful; verify).
- **Memory safety in unsafe arenas is a data-integrity risk.** A stray write into the wrong slot is silent without checksums. Fix: CRC at ingest, verify before CP write and on read; generation-checked slot handles; a single unsafe crate under Miri/fuzz; and, decisively, the NVLOG (not the arena) as source of truth.
- **Stateless N-Blade is incoherent for iSCSI** (not my area, but it touches durability): CmdSN/StatSN windows, task sets, persistent reservations and unit attentions are state; PRs must survive node failure and belong in the Raft-replicated DB; sessions re-login on N-Blade loss. COMPARE AND WRITE must be atomic in the D-Blade.
- **FC "replication" is not an RPC fabric.** Target mode (tcm_qla2xxx, dual initiator/target on one port) is complex and the receive path either goes through tcmu-runner (slow) or kernel backstores. Fix: make the mirror a remote-log semantic so it can be a SCSI WRITE; let the transport specialist own the rest.
- **2-node Raft** cannot survive one node down without a witness; with shelves, mailbox disks + PR; without, a third voter.
- **Degraded-mode ACK policy is undefined.** When the partner is down, either ACK on local persistence only (RPO=0 now depends on local PMem) or stop ACKing (availability loss). ONTAP picks the former; the owner must choose.

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build
- The NVLOG crate: record format (`RecHdr` above), 4 KiB-aligned ring on (a) devdax PMem with `clwb`/`sfence` commit, (b) O_DIRECT NVMe with IOPOLL, (c) DRAM-only prototype mode behind a loud flag. `append`, `retire`, `replay` with CRC and gap detection.
- The arena + dirty index crates with read-your-writes, overlap resolution, partial-sector masks, generation-checked handles, memory accounting and watermark backpressure.
- The mirror ring on the partner (append, CRC, ACK, retire, resync-from-seq) over TCP, behind the `LogTransport` trait; replay-on-takeover into a fresh index.
- A "CP-lite" that drains the frozen set sorted by (vol, lba) into full-stripe O_DIRECT writes on a real md RAID6, plus the superblock-in-a-stripe commit, so the flush pipeline and FUA/flush ordering are exercised early even without the map and GC.
- An in-process load generator (random 4-64 KiB, mixed overwrite, discard) independent of any fabric.

##### Prove (success criteria)
- Durability: 1,000 crash tests (SIGKILL, `echo c > /proc/sysrq-trigger`, PMem emulated with `memmap=` in a VM, and real power pulls on the lab R740xd) with zero ACKed-write loss and zero phantom writes; replay of a 20 GB ring under 20 s.
- Latency on 10GbE with Optane/PMem NVLOG: 4 KiB write ACK at the D-Blade boundary p50 <= 150 us, p99 <= 500 us, with local persist and mirror overlapped, not serialized.
- Ingest: >= 2 GB/s into local NVLOG + mirror sustained for 60 s on a 10GbE pair with CPU use under 4 cores.
- Flush: measured sequential write MB/s on the actual 12-drive md RAID6 at chunk 256/512 KiB and `stripe_cache_size` 256 vs 32768 (expect 3-5x difference), full-stripe confirmed by zero RMW (`iostat` member read bytes ~0 during a CP); CP of 20 GB completes and the superblock commit survives power pulls.
- Reads: measured random 4 KiB read IOPS of the array (expect 1-3K) recorded as the baseline that sizes the SSD tier.
- Integrity: injected arena corruption and NVLOG bit flips are detected by CRC 100% of the time; Miri and fuzzing clean on the arena/index crates.

#### Open questions
- **Is RPO=0 required under simultaneous dual power loss?** Decides whether PMem/NVDIMM-N/Optane SSD must be purchased for both nodes or DRAM NVLOG is acceptable for the prototype.
- **Hardware budget per node for NVMe/PMem** (2 NVLOG devices + 2 read-cache/map devices, or Optane PMem modules)? Decides NVLOG medium, read-cache size and whether the map can be NVMe-resident.
- **SAS or SATA drives, and are external shelves acceptable** (rack adjacency, two external 12Gb SAS HBAs)? Decides shelves vs shadow replica vs reduced availability, and RAID geometry.
- **Expected sustained write rate and read working-set size** for the target workload? Decides spindle count, GC headroom (free-space reserve), SSD read-cache size and NVLOG endurance class.
- **Capacity or read IOPS priority?** Decides RAID6 vs RAID10 per aggregate.
- **Degraded-mode ACK policy** when the partner is down: ACK on local persistence or stop ACKing?
- **Snapshots/clones in v1, LUN logical sector size (512e vs 4Kn), UNMAP support required at launch?** Decides map/deadlist scope and the partial-block path.
- **Rocky 9 or 10 baseline?** Decides io_uring defaults, ublk availability and which md version we validate FUA/PREFLUSH on.
- **Is a third voter/witness host allowed** (or do shelves provide mailbox disks)? Decides the quorum model for Phase 4.

#### Load-bearing claims
- [STORAGE-1] (high) On RHEL/Rocky 9.4 and later the sysctl kernel.io_uring_disabled defaults to 2, so io_uring_setup() returns EPERM for all processes until it is set to 0.
  - why it matters: The entire D-Blade I/O path (registered buffers, fixed writes, IOPOLL on the NVLOG NVMe) assumes io_uring; if the default cannot be changed in the deployment, the fallback is pwritev2 worker pools with higher latency.
- [STORAGE-2] (medium) In block/fops.c, dio_bio_write_op() sets REQ_FUA on a direct-I/O write when the iocb is DSYNC, and md raid456 forwards FUA/PREFLUSH to member devices, so O_DIRECT|O_DSYNC writes to /dev/mdX are FUA writes.
  - why it matters: The CP commit protocol (data, flush, FUA superblock, flush) and the retire-NVLOG step depend on this ordering actually reaching the platters.
- [STORAGE-3] (high) md PPL works only for RAID5 (not RAID6), is limited to 64 member devices, and costs 30-40% of write throughput; the md journal is the only md-native write-hole closure for RAID6.
  - why it matters: Justifies closing the write hole by design (never overwrite live data, superblock replicated in a full stripe) rather than with PPL or a journal SSD on a RAID6 aggregate.
- [STORAGE-4] (high) A 7200 rpm 3.5in NL-SAS/SATA drive sustains roughly 200-250 MB/s sequential on outer tracks and 100-130 MB/s on inner tracks, and delivers about 80 random 4 KiB IOPS at QD1 and 150-200 at QD32.
  - why it matters: All throughput arithmetic (1.4 GB/s absorb for 12 drives, 16-32 s CP at line rate, 1-2.4K read IOPS) and therefore the SSD-tier and spindle-count recommendations rest on these figures.
- [STORAGE-5] (medium) The Dell R740xd supports at most 12 NVDIMM-N modules of 16 GB (192 GB) in dual-CPU configurations with Gold/Platinum-class Xeon Scalable CPUs, and the NVDIMM battery kit excludes the 2.5in mid-drive tray.
  - why it matters: Sizes the PMem NVLOG option (192 GB covers two CPs of own log plus the partner mirror at 2x10GbE only at a 10 s interval) and constrains chassis configuration.
- [STORAGE-6] (medium) Dell MD1400/MD1420 shelves with dual EMMs support SAS/NL-SAS drives (SATA is not listed) in redundant-path mode and require an external 12 Gb SAS HBA or PERC H830/H840, not the internal-only HBA330.
  - why it matters: The recommended production HA model (shared dual-ported shelves, md assembled on one node, fenced) requires SAS drives and one extra HBA per node; if SATA is in play, only the shadow-replica model remains.
- [STORAGE-7] (medium) Cascade Lake platforms provide ADR but not eADR, so CPU caches are outside the persistence domain and each NVLOG record must be written back with CLWB/CLFLUSHOPT plus SFENCE before it is durable; stable Rust exposes no _mm_clwb intrinsic, so this must be emitted with asm!.
  - why it matters: The PMem NVLOG commit sequence (payload, fence, header-with-seq, writeback, fence) and the claim that ACKed data is durable on power loss depend on it.
- [STORAGE-8] (medium) 2x10GbE carries about 2.2 GB/s of block payload, i.e. 22 GB per 10 s CP, while a 12-drive RAID6 of 7.2K HDDs absorbs about 1.4 GB/s before GC and 0.7-0.9 GB/s after, so sustained line-rate writes overflow a 64 GB write-cache budget in roughly 45-60 s.
  - why it matters: This is the basis for re-scoping the objective (latency and burst absorption rather than sustained throughput), the backpressure design, and the spindle-count/shelf recommendation.

### Opus-Consensus

#### Executive take
I would build the control plane as a three-voter openraft cluster (the two HA nodes plus a tiny witness VM) whose replicated state machine is a few hundred KB of ownership, epoch and pair-state records, and I would keep every data-path decision (mirror acks, CPs, I/O admission) out of Raft, gated instead by an epoch carried on every message plus a lease that is either quorum-attested or partner-attested and backed by a hardware watchdog. The consensus library is not the risk; the plan as written has no safe automatic takeover at all: a 2-voter Raft tolerates zero failures, node-local disks mean the survivor cannot read the partner's flushed data, and a volatile-RAM NVLOG means one rack power event breaks RPO=0. The thing I most disagree with is node-local disks: ONTAP takeover rests on dual-ported shared shelves, SCSI-3 persistent-reservation fencing and mailbox disks, and OpenTAP should copy that with external dual-EMM SAS JBODs (MD1400-class, SAS/NL-SAS drives only) cabled to both R740xd nodes through HBA355e adapters. PR preemption is a fence enforced by the drive itself, which no lease-and-clock argument can match, and the SAS fabric doubles as a third heartbeat path and disk-based tiebreaker. If the owner insists on internal disks, Phase 1 must choose between MetroCluster-style full synchronous data replication (2x capacity, half the write bandwidth) and an explicit "RPO=0 but RTO=repair time, manual failover" statement. The single biggest cross-area hole I found: Red Hat removed qla2xxx target mode (tcm_qla2xxx) from RHEL kernels in RHEL 8, so the Tier 2 FC mirror link almost certainly needs a non-Rocky kernel or an NVMe/FC path; that needs verifying before FC is called the primary goal.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Consensus crates (honest view)

| Crate | Shape | Version / activity | Membership | Linearizable reads | Leases / disruptive nodes | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| openraft (databend-labs) | async library: you implement `RaftNetwork`, `RaftLogStorage`, `RaftStateMachine`; it runs the core on an `AsyncRuntime` (`TokioRuntime` shipped) | 0.9.25 stable, 0.10.0-alpha.3x in progress; "API is currently unstable ... prior to 1.0.0" | joint consensus only ("does not support single step membership change"); learners via `add_learner`; `change_membership(members, retain)` | `ensure_linearizable()` / `get_read_log_id()` (ReadIndex without blank-log check) | follower-side leader lease: a node with a committed vote "WILL NOT handle a VoteRequest before the leader_lease expires"; no classic pre-vote | Recommend |
| raft (tikv/raft-rs) | sans-I/O core (`RawNode`, `Ready`): you provide log, storage, transport, timers | crates.io 0.7.0 (2023); master active (commits Mar-May 2026, prost-only codec) | ConfChangeV2 joint consensus | ReadIndex (`ReadOnlyOption::Safe`) and `LeaseBased` | `pre_vote`, `check_quorum` | Strong fallback; best shape for deterministic simulation, but protobuf-typed API, thin docs, slow crates.io cadence |
| omnipaxos (KTH) | library ("plain Rust struct", any storage/network/runtime) | 0.2.3, "in-development", EuroSys'23 paper, no production claims | reconfiguration via stop-sign | not documented | Ballot Leader Election with quorum-connectivity (resists partial connectivity where Raft livelocks) | No for v1; the only credible Rust Paxos-family option |
| async-raft | openraft's ancestor | abandoned | - | - | - | No |
| raftify, ruraft (hashicorp port), little_raft | wrappers / young ports | small, young | partial | - | - | No |
| etcd / external | Go service | mature | - | yes | - | Violates "Rust daemons", adds an ops dependency; keep as benchmark only |
| write our own | - | - | - | - | - | Only if the "disk-hosted voter" idea (creative section) is pursued, and then with TLA+ first |

Recommendation: openraft 0.9.x pinned, with a thin wrapper so a swap to raft-rs is a bounded job. Reasons: async-native, joint consensus only (that is the safe variant), follower-side lease rejection of votes solves the 3-node asymmetric-partition flip-flop without pre-vote, `AsyncRuntime` lets us plug a simulated runtime for deterministic tests, and it has years of production use in Databend's meta-service (I believe; verify). Costs: pre-1.0 API churn (0.8 to 0.9 to 0.10 each broke storage traits), the `Vote`/log-id semantics differ from the paper ("in every term there could be more than one leader elected, and the last one is valid"), and we own the transport and storage code.

##### Supporting crates and subsystems
- Raft log/snapshot storage: a hand-written append-only file with `fdatasync` plus serde snapshots is enough for a KB-scale RSM; `redb` 2.x (pure-Rust, ACID, stable file format) if we want a real KV. Avoid RocksDB for a 200 KB state.
- Wire format: serde + postcard (or bincode 2) with versioned enums; no tonic/gRPC. Raft RPCs ride the same `Transport` trait as the mirror so Raft can run over Ethernet or FC.
- Fencing: Redfish over HTTPS (`reqwest`) to iDRAC9 `ComputerSystem.Reset` `ForceOff`; SCSI-3 PR via `SG_IO` ioctls (`nix`) or shelling to `sg_persist`/`mpathpersist` (sg3_utils, device-mapper-multipath); `/dev/watchdog` via raw ioctls (`iTCO_wdt` on the C62x PCH or `ipmi_watchdog` against the iDRAC BMC). IPMI over LAN is disabled by default on current iDRAC9 firmware (Dell DSA-2024-099), so Redfish, not `fence_ipmilan`.
- mdadm: invoked as a process; `AUTO -all` in mdadm.conf and udev masking on shared disks; `--bitmap=internal` for fast re-assembly.
- Kernel dependencies for this area: none beyond 5.14. SCSI PR, md, pmem/DAX (`fsdax`/`devdax`), watchdog drivers all exist in Rocky 9 and 10. The 9-vs-10 choice does not matter for the control plane; it matters for io_uring defaults and, decisively, for FC target mode, which neither ships (see holes).
- Testing: turmoil 0.7 (tokio network simulation; partitions, latency), madsim 0.2.34 (deterministic tokio replacement, `--cfg madsim`), shuttle (randomized scheduler), loom (exhaustive model checking for the lock-free epoch seqlock and NVLOG ring), stateright (model checker for the takeover protocol as an actor model), TLA+/TLC for the ownership-lease-fence invariant, Jepsen + elle (or a Rust port) for linearizability of ownership and client I/O on real hardware, nftables/tc/`ip link`/switch `portdisable`/iDRAC power/`kill -STOP`/sysrq-c as nemeses, a switched PDU for real power pulls.

#### Proposed architecture
##### Components
- `otap-ctl` (one per node, also runs as the witness with `--role witness`): embeds openraft; modules: failure detector, lease manager, fence ladder, takeover state machine, address manager (ALUA/ANA group state, optional floating IPs).
- RSM (`ClusterState`): `aggregates[id] -> {home_node, owner_node, epoch, mode: Mirrored|UnmirroredGranted|TakeoverInProgress|GivebackInProgress}`, `volumes[id] -> {aggregate, epoch_at_create}`, `pairs[id] -> {a, b, state}`, `nodes[id] -> {addr, health, version, capabilities}`, `portals/LIFs -> node, TPG ids`, `cluster_version`, config. Takeover granularity is the aggregate (one md array set); per-volume epochs exist so volume moves between aggregates are possible later. Size: ~200 B per object, snapshot every 1,000 entries.
- Not in Raft: client I/O, mirror writes and acks, NVLOG contents, CP scheduling and contents, allocator metadata. Raft is written at most a few times per minute; its commit latency (one RTT to the witness plus fsync, 5-50 ms) is irrelevant.
- Epoch feed: `otap-ctl` publishes the applied ownership table into a memory-mapped seqlock page read by the D-Blade and N-Blade on every admission (no syscall). Every mirror record, CP commit record and internal RPC carries `(aggregate_id, epoch)`; receivers reject anything with epoch < their applied epoch; the aggregate superblock stores the epoch of the last CP and an importer refuses an aggregate whose on-disk epoch is newer than the epoch it was granted (fail-safe).

```rust
enum Cmd { GrantUnmirrored{aggr, owner, epoch}, BeginTakeover{aggr, from, to, epoch},
           CommitTakeover{aggr, to, epoch}, BeginGiveback{..}, CommitGiveback{..},
           NodeHealth{node, state}, PlaceLif{lif, node}, SetClusterVersion(u32) }

struct Lease { aggr: AggrId, epoch: u64, valid_until: Instant /* monotonic, local */ }

trait Fence { async fn fence(&self, node: NodeId) -> FenceResult; }   // PrPreempt, RedfishForceOff, WatchdogBound
trait Transport { /* shared with mirror: send/recv framed msgs over TCP/FC/RDMA, per-link health */ }
```

##### Quorum, leases, fencing (the 2-node problem)
- Voters: A, B, W (witness). A 2-voter Raft tolerates zero failures and only adds latency; it is not an option. W stores the log (fsync on a small SSD, never SD card) and never owns anything. It should sit on a different switch path from the cluster network.
- Lease rule: a node may admit I/O for an aggregate it owns iff `now < last_quorum_contact + L` OR `now < last_partner_heartbeat + L`, with L = 3 s, heartbeats every 100 ms on every link (Ethernet, mirror link, and the shared-disk mailbox if a JBOD exists), renewal every 500 ms. Quorum contact means an AppendEntries from the current-term leader, or being leader with check-quorum satisfied. Partner-attestation is safe because takeover needs partner silence longer than L on all links; it keeps an Ethernet switch failure from becoming a storage outage.
- Hardware watchdog: `otap-ctl` pets `/dev/watchdog` (T_wd = 5 s) only while its leases are valid and the data daemons report I/O progress (completion counters advancing). A paused, wedged or partitioned node resets itself within L + T_wd = 8 s of its last contact. Refuse to start if the watchdog cannot be opened.
- Fence ladder before any takeover: (1) PR PREEMPT-AND-ABORT on every shared drive with the survivor's key (device-enforced, ~ms per drive, parallel); (2) Redfish `ForceOff` to the partner's iDRAC, confirmed by polling `PowerState == Off` (2-5 s typical; verify on 14G firmware); (3) if neither is available, wait until `t_last_heard + L*(1+rho) + T_wd + 100 ms`. Takeover of writes also waits at least `t_last_heard + L*(1+rho) + pause margin` so a still-alive partner has stopped admitting reads (linearizability, not just data loss).
- Unmirrored mode rule (the invariant that makes the matrix close): a node may ACK a write it could not mirror only after Raft has committed `GrantUnmirrored` for that aggregate, and that grant disables takeover of the aggregate. A node without quorum cannot get the grant, so it blocks writes (or, with a shared SAS-SSD journal, writes there instead). Without this rule, "mirror link down then node dies" silently loses acknowledged writes.

##### Partition matrix (A, B nodes; W witness; E Ethernet, M mirror link, S shared SAS fabric if JBOD, I iDRAC)
| Scenario | Nodes do | Clients see | RPO=0 | Split-brain impossible because |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E down, M up (W reachable or not) | Raft and heartbeats fall back to M; A+B are 2/3 even without W; no ownership change | nothing | yes | no ownership change occurs |
| M down, E up | owner of each aggregate commits GrantUnmirrored; keeps serving from durable NVLOG; takeover disabled; alert | one Raft commit of latency, then normal | yes for the moment; a later single-node failure makes that aggregate unavailable (not lost) until repair, as ONTAP "interconnect down" does | takeover is disabled by committed state |
| E and M down between A and B, both reach W | leader (say A) keeps W (W ignores B's votes during A's lease); B loses leader contact, lease expires at +3 s, stops I/O, stops petting, resets by +8 s; A declares B silent at +2 s, fences, takes over B's aggregate (shared JBOD) at new epoch | B's paths die; ALUA failover to A; stall 9-12 s | yes: everything B ACKed was mirrored to A (B held no unmirrored grant) | B cannot admit after expiry; A cannot admit before expiry bound plus fence; epochs reject stragglers |
| same, only A reaches W | identical; if B was leader it loses check-quorum, steps down, lease expires; A elects with W and takes over | as above | yes | as above |
| E, M down and neither reaches W | both lose quorum and partner: both block after L (fail-safe outage); with S up they see each other via the disk mailbox and can keep serving only if the journal is on shared SAS SSDs; otherwise block | outage until a link returns | yes (nothing unmirrored is ACKed) | nobody holds a lease |
| node wedged (process hung, kernel alive) | app-level heartbeats stop; partner treats as dead; watchdog resets the wedged node | 10-16 s stall | yes | reset plus PR plus epoch |
| node reboots with stale state | mdadm auto-assembly disabled; no NVLOG replay until the RSM confirms it still owns at that epoch (it does not; entries are discarded because A replayed them); joins Raft as follower; giveback is explicit | nothing | yes | returning node does nothing until told |
| both nodes lose power (same PDU/UPS) | NVLOG survives only if on NVDIMM-N/PMem/PLP NVMe | outage | only with durable NVLOG; with RAM, up to 15 s of ACKed writes are lost | n/a |
| fence unreachable (I on same failed network) and no JBOD | survivor waits the watchdog bound (8 s) before takeover | 12-16 s stall | yes | bound holds only if the watchdog is hardware and petting is lease-gated; operational risk, hence the startup check |

##### Takeover (shared JBOD, witness, durable NVLOG), t0 = B dies
