+0.3 s suspect (3 missed heartbeats per link); +2 s silent on all links; +2.05 s `BeginTakeover` committed; PR preempt all drives (<0.5 s) and Redfish ForceOff in parallel; wait to max(fence confirmed, t_last_heard + 3.2 s); +3.5 s `mdadm --assemble` B's arrays (1-3 s; internal bitmap avoids resync); +6 s replay B's NVLOG from A's mirror region into the allocator for B's aggregate (10 GB / 4 KB = 2.6 M records at 1-2 M/s = 1.5-3 s; if the mirror lives in volatile RAM, first append it to A's local durable journal, ~1-2 s at 200 MB/s per 1 GB... size the journal); +9 s `CommitTakeover` epoch e+1, ALUA/ANA group for B's LUNs flips to Active/Optimized on A's ports (LIO configfs ALUA for iSCSI, nvmet ANA groups for NVMe/TCP), optional gratuitous ARP for floating IPs. Client stall 9-12 s, inside ESXi's 10 s RecoveryTimeout path-dead plus multipath retry, Windows MPIO PDORemovePeriod 20 s, Linux replacement_timeout 120 s, NVMe ctrl_loss_tmo 600 s. CPs for B's aggregate resume on B's spindles through A's HBA355e; A's NVLOG is split half/half local/partner from day one (ONTAP does the same), so under takeover the CP interval halves at full load.

##### Giveback
B returns, catches up the Raft log, reports healthy; after a stability window (600 s default, or operator command): A quiesces the aggregate (new writes get SCSI BUSY / ANA transitioning for 2-3 s), final CP, `CommitGiveback` epoch e+2, PR keys re-registered for B, B assembles and starts a fresh NVLOG, paths flip. With full replication instead of a JBOD, giveback needs resync first: ship the CP segments B missed (log-structured segments make this a sequential copy, no dirty bitmap), then mirror writes to both copies during catch-up; this is DRBD's entire problem space and should be acknowledged as such.

##### Membership, upgrades, clocks
- Adding a pair: add both nodes as learners (`add_learner`, blocking catch-up), then one joint `change_membership` to 5 voters (A, B, C, D, W). Keep W: 4 voters cannot survive losing a whole pair, 5 can. Beyond three pairs cap voters at 5 or 7 and keep the rest learners; takeover is always intra-pair, as in ONTAP.
- Rolling upgrade: takeover, upgrade, giveback per node (ONTAP's ANDU). `Cmd` is a versioned enum tolerant of unknown variants; features gate on a committed `cluster_version`; openraft wire types are serialized by us and must stay compatible across adjacent releases, tested in CI with mixed versions.
- Clocks: CLOCK_MONOTONIC only for safety; leases measured from the local send time of the renewal; wall clock for logs. Assume <= 500 ppm drift (1.5 ms over L); pauses are bounded by the watchdog, not by assumptions. W's clock is irrelevant.

##### Testing
- Deterministic: the whole control daemon plus transport under turmoil (partitions per link, latency, reorder) with a seeded schedule; openraft under a madsim `AsyncRuntime` for whole-cluster determinism (verify feasibility); loom on the epoch seqlock and mirror ring; stateright or TLA+ for the ownership protocol with the invariant "no two nodes admit I/O for an aggregate in overlapping real-time intervals, and every ACKed write is on a surviving durable copy".
- Hardware: Jepsen-style harness issuing iSCSI/NVMe writes with unique payloads, nemeses on each link class, iDRAC power, `kill -STOP`, PDU pulls; elle-checked histories; nightly planned takeover/giveback rehearsal as a product feature.

#### Bottlenecks
##### Failover time budget (client-visible)
detection 2 s + fence 0.5-5 s + md assemble 1-3 s + replay 1.5-3 s + commit and ALUA flip ~1 s = 6-14 s. Floor without a confirmed fence is L + T_wd = 8 s plus the rest, 12-16 s. Initiator windows: ESXi iSCSI RecoveryTimeout 10 s (path dead, failover), Windows MPIO PDORemovePeriod 20 s, Linux open-iscsi replacement_timeout 120 s, NVMe ctrl_loss_tmo 600 s with 10 s reconnect delay. Target 15 s worst case; beyond ~20 s Windows guests start erroring.

##### Lease and detection arithmetic
L = 3 s at 500 ppm drift = 1.5 ms slack; heartbeat 100 ms gives a 300 ms false-suspect floor; 2 s silence threshold tolerates a 1 s GC-free stall (Rust, mlock'd). Raft itself: heartbeat 250 ms, election 1.5-3 s; the witness RTT (even 50 ms cross-building) only affects the rare commit, never the data path.

##### NVLOG sizing drives takeover
10GbE ingest 1.25 GB/s x 15 s CP = 18.75 GB per window; double-buffered (CP draining while the next fills) = 37.5 GB; same again reserved for the partner's mirror = 75 GB per node. At 1GbE it is 1.9 GB and 3.75 GB. NVDIMM-N tops out at 12 x 16 GB = 192 GB on the R740xd, enough; Optane PMem (Cascade Lake only) is roomier. Replay cost scales with records, not bytes: 4 KB writes at 18.75 GB = 4.7 M records, ~3-5 s of replay; cap NVLOG by record count too.

##### Disk drain bounds "network speed"
12 x 7.2k NL-SAS in RAID6 (10 data) at a 100-180 MB/s sequential profile gives 1.0-1.8 GB/s theoretical, 0.7-1.0 GB/s realistic full-stripe mdadm throughput. 10GbE ingest exceeds that, so sustained writes are disk-bound; the RAM cache buys latency and bursts up to the NVLOG size, not sustained throughput. With node-local full replication each array also absorbs the partner's CPs, halving sustainable ingest to ~0.35-0.5 GB/s per node. With a shared JBOD the partner's spindles stay separate and takeover does not steal bandwidth.

##### Fence paths
PR preempt: 24 drives in parallel <100 ms through expanders. Redfish ForceOff: 2-5 s to confirm (verify). Watchdog bound: 8 s. mdadm assemble of a 12-drive RAID6 with bitmap: 1-3 s; without bitmap after an unclean stop, a resync of hours (still importable, degraded performance).

##### Raft is not a bottleneck
A few commits per minute, 200 KB state; openraft's single-core loop would handle 10^4 ops/s. The only Raft-side cost that matters is the election timeout during a leader loss (1.5-3 s), and leases bridge it.

#### Creative solutions
- SAS fabric as the third heartbeat path and disk arbiter: with a dual-EMM JBOD, reserve a small mailbox region on three drives (SBD-style majority). Each node writes a heartbeat slot every second and the partner reads it, so "Ethernet and FC both dead" no longer looks like partner death. PR preempt on one arbiter drive first, data drives second, makes the fence race atomic: the loser sees RESERVATION CONFLICT on its own mailbox write and self-resets.
- Disk-hosted third voter for witness-less sites: a Raft voter whose persistent state (term, vote, log) lives on the mailbox drives and is impersonated only by the current PR holder. The PR gives mutual exclusion, the disk gives durability, and the pair gets automatic failover with no third box. Exotic; it needs a TLA+ model and would be a custom voter outside openraft's normal node model.
- Raft and heartbeats over the mirror transport (including FC): the `Transport` trait already exists for the mirror, so Raft messages ride it as a second path for free; Ethernet loss then costs nothing.
- Shared SAS-SSD journal as the degraded path: two dual-port 12G SAS SSDs (Kioxia PM6/PM7, Samsung PM1643a class) in the JBOD as an NVLOG journal both nodes can read. Normal path: PMem + RAM mirror (fast ACK). Mirror-link-down path: write to the shared journal (100-200 us) instead of blocking or going unmirrored, so RPO=0 survives even the "both links down" row and takeover never depends on the mirror being current.
- Partner-attested leases: the pair keeps serving with no witness as long as the partners hear each other; quorum is needed only to change ownership.
- Lease-gated hardware watchdog (diskless-SBD semantics) as the universal backstop fence, with the data daemons' progress counters as the "alive and useful" signal.
- Epoch seqlock page: the control daemon publishes the ownership table into a mapped page; every I/O admission reads epoch and lease expiry with two loads and no syscall.
- Segment shipping for resync: if full replication is chosen, CP sequence numbers make catch-up "send segments > last_cp_seen"; the LFS gives DRBD's activity-log function for free.
- Nightly takeover rehearsal as a product feature: a planned takeover/giveback at low load with latency SLOs, so the fence ladder and initiators are exercised before a real failure.

#### Holes in the plan
1. Two-node Raft is a null feature. Majority of 2 is 2: any single failure stalls the control plane and nobody can commit a takeover. Fix: witness voter, or the disk-arbiter route if a JBOD exists; otherwise manual failover only, stated in writing.
2. Node-local disks are incompatible with ONTAP-style takeover. ONTAP's HA pair assumes both controllers see every shelf (multipath SAS), ownership is a soft label (SANOWN) with PR as the hard fence, and the 2-node cluster quorum uses mailbox disks. The survivor here could only serve the 15 s of NVLOG; everything older is unreadable. Options: (a) external dual-EMM SAS JBOD per pair (recommended; MD1400/MD1420 class with HBA355e; SAS/NL-SAS only, SATA is single-ported); (b) MetroCluster-style full sync replication of every CP to the partner's disks (2x capacity, half ingest, resync machinery); (c) RPO=0, RTO=repair-time, manual failover. Decide before Phase 1 because it changes the mirror wire format (b needs CP segment shipping) and the allocator layout (a needs importable, self-describing aggregates).
3. Volatile RAM NVLOG breaks RPO=0 on a rack power event, which is a common correlated failure (same PDU/UPS). NVDIMM-N on the R740xd (12 x 16 GB, battery-backed, note the 2.5" mid-tray excludes the battery) or Optane PMem App Direct or a PLP NVMe journal is required, and it changes takeover: the survivor's mirror copy is already durable, so there is no exposure window.
4. "Mirror down, keep ACKing" is a data-loss trap unless gated by a Raft-committed unmirrored grant that disables takeover. Also decide the policy explicitly: block writes vs unmirrored-with-grant vs shared journal.
5. Fencing network topology: if iDRAC shares the Ethernet that just failed, Redfish fencing is unreachable exactly when it is needed. Separate iDRAC network, plus PR and the watchdog bound as independent fences.
6. mdadm on shared disks: no multi-host protection outside md-cluster (which needs DLM/Corosync, the thing we are replacing). Must disable auto-assembly and only assemble after PR is held. A md write-journal or PPL on node-local NVMe makes the array unimportable on the partner; full-stripe LFS writes make the write hole moot instead.
7. FC mirror link vs Rocky kernels: Red Hat removed tcm_qla2xxx in RHEL 8 ("Management decided to disable qla2xxx target support", Bugzilla 1666377); RHEL 9/10 and therefore Rocky inherit that (I believe; verify). A userspace FC mirror does not exist. Options: ELRepo/custom kernel, NVMe/FC (lpfc + nvmet-fc, availability in Rocky unverified), or demote FC to Tier 3 and make 25GbE RoCE the Tier 2 goal. The control plane is transport-agnostic, but the roadmap's "primary goal" is at risk.
8. "Stateless N-Blade" for iSCSI: sessions, CmdSN/StatSN windows and ITT tables are soft state and can be lost; SCSI-3 PR registrations/reservations from VMware and Windows clusters, LUN unit attentions and ALUA TPG/relative port IDs are persistent and must live in D-Blade metadata or the RSM; TPG and port IDs must be stable constants assigned by the control plane.
9. "One massive contiguous stripe" is only contiguous if the allocator writes whole RAID stripes (chunk x data disks) and the CP commit record is FUA'd after the stripe completes; under takeover with full replication the same spindles take 2x writes.
10. Heartbeats must be application-level with progress evidence; a wedged process keeps TCP alive. The data daemons must feed completion counters into the lease renewal.
11. The returning node's NVLOG and arrays are poison until the RSM speaks: no auto-assembly, no auto-replay; epoch comparison decides discard vs replay.
12. Wall clocks: nothing in the safety argument may use them; NTP steps and the witness VM's clock must be irrelevant by construction.
13. Phase ordering: a Phase 1 mirror daemon without `(aggregate, epoch, seq)` in its wire format and without a stale-epoch reject path will be rewritten in Phase 4. Put the epoch and the fence token in the first packet format.
14. Transport latency reality check: 1GbE kernel TCP mirror RTT 100-200 us plus 33 us serialization per 4 KB; 10GbE 30-60 us; FC 20-50 us; RDMA 5-10 us. Client-visible 4 KB write latency at 10GbE lands around 150-300 us, a 20-50x win over HDD; 1GbE still wins on latency but saturates at 125 MB/s.

#### Phase 1 recommendation
Phase 1 is the mirror daemon, but it must carry the control-plane seeds or it will be thrown away:
1. Wire format: every mirror record carries `(aggregate_id, owner_epoch, nvlog_seq, cp_seq)`; the receiver rejects stale epochs; the ACK carries the receiver's applied epoch. Criterion: 100 percent rejection of stale-epoch records in a 10,000-seed turmoil run with partitions and reordering; zero acknowledged-but-unmirrored writes in any run.
2. Ownership oracle stub: a local file/CLI that bumps epochs, exposed through the mmap seqlock page the data daemons will keep in Phase 4. Criterion: epoch bump to the receiver causes all in-flight sender writes to fail-closed within one RTT.
3. Failure detector and partner-attested lease over the same `Transport` (100 ms heartbeats, L = 3 s), plus lease-gated `/dev/watchdog` petting. Criterion: `kill -STOP` of the daemon resets the node within L + T_wd = 8 s; the partner declares silence at 2 s.
4. Fence library: Redfish ForceOff with confirmation, PR register/preempt over `SG_IO`, watchdog bound. Criterion: ForceOff confirmed in under 5 s on R740xd iDRAC9; PR preempt makes the old owner's next write return RESERVATION CONFLICT.
5. Hardware decision spike (cheap and decisive): one MD1400-class dual-EMM shelf, two HBA355e, 12 NL-SAS drives, both nodes cabled. Prove dual-host visibility, PR preempt through the expanders, `mdadm --assemble` on the survivor after preempt, and auto-assembly disabled. Criterion: planned takeover rehearsal under 15 s client stall with ESXi and Linux initiators using ALUA (LIO for the Phase 1 target).
6. Durability spike: NVDIMM-N or PLP NVMe NVLOG with 50 PDU power pulls under load. Criterion: zero lost acknowledged writes, verified by unique-payload checker.
7. Simulation harness in CI from day one (turmoil for transport and detector; loom for the seqlock and ring). Criterion: every control-plane PR runs the seeded matrix.
Deliverable for Fable 5.1: a one-page decision record choosing JBOD vs full replication vs degraded statement, and witness vs disk arbiter vs manual, before Phase 2 starts.

#### Open questions
- Is a witness allowed (a VM on a third host or a management box), and can it live on a separate switch path? Unblocks: witness-based quorum vs disk arbiter vs manual failover.
- Is an external dual-EMM SAS JBOD per pair acceptable in cost and rack space, and are SATA drives negotiable (shared JBOD implies SAS/NL-SAS)? Unblocks: takeover design, allocator importability, resync machinery.
- Is the iDRAC network physically separate from the cluster and client networks? Unblocks: whether Redfish counts as a fence or only the watchdog bound does.
- NVLOG budget: NVDIMM-N with battery (conflicts with the 2.5" mid-tray) vs Optane PMem (Cascade Lake only) vs PLP NVMe? Unblocks: RPO=0 for correlated power loss, mirror-down policy, takeover replay path.
- Acceptable client-visible stall during takeover and giveback (10 s, 20 s, 60 s), and the initiator population (ESXi, Windows MPIO, Linux, NVMe/TCP)? Unblocks: detection and lease timers.
- Mirror-down policy: block writes, unmirrored-with-grant, or shared SAS-SSD journal? Unblocks: NVLOG and JBOD BOM.
- Is a non-Rocky kernel (ELRepo or self-built) acceptable for FC target mode, or should FC be demoted behind 25GbE RoCE? Unblocks: the Tier 2 plan and the transport abstraction's FC leg.
- Expected growth (how many pairs, whether volumes ever move between pairs)? Unblocks: voter cap, learner policy, per-volume vs per-aggregate epochs.
- Is manual failover acceptable for v1 if the witness and JBOD are both refused? Unblocks: whether Phase 1 ships automatic takeover at all.

#### Load-bearing claims
- [CONSENSUS-1] (high) openraft 0.9.25 is the latest stable release (0.10 is alpha), supports only joint-consensus membership change, exposes ensure_linearizable()/get_read_log_id() for linearizable reads, provides an AsyncRuntime trait with a TokioRuntime implementation, and its followers ignore VoteRequests while their leader lease is valid.
  - why it matters: The library recommendation, the 3-voter asymmetric-partition argument (no flip-flop without pre-vote) and the deterministic-simulation plan all rest on these features.
- [CONSENSUS-2] (high) tikv/raft-rs is a sans-I/O core whose crates.io release is 0.7.0 (2023) while its master branch received commits in March-May 2026, and it supports ConfChangeV2 joint consensus, ReadIndex and lease-based reads, pre-vote and check-quorum.
  - why it matters: It is the designated fallback if openraft's API churn or runtime abstraction proves unworkable; if it were unmaintained the fallback would be writing our own.
- [CONSENSUS-3] (medium) Red Hat removed qla2xxx target mode (the tcm_qla2xxx module) from RHEL 8 kernels and targetcli (Bugzilla 1666377, 'Management decided to disable qla2xxx target support in RHEL8'), and RHEL 9/10 and Rocky inherit that removal.
  - why it matters: If true, the Tier 2 FC mirror link cannot be built on a stock Rocky kernel with QLogic HBAs and the roadmap's primary goal needs a kernel or protocol change; the RHEL 9/10 inheritance is inferred, not verified.
- [CONSENSUS-4] (high) A SCSI-3 PERSISTENT RESERVE OUT PREEMPT AND ABORT issued by the survivor causes the preempted node's subsequent writes to the same SAS drive to fail with RESERVATION CONFLICT, enforced by the drive firmware, and this works through SAS expanders on dual-EMM JBODs with dual-ported SAS/NL-SAS drives, whereas SATA drives are single-ported and cannot be shared by two hosts without interposers.
  - why it matters: The device-enforced fence is the core safety argument for the shared-JBOD recommendation and the atomic arbiter-disk idea; if PR were unreliable through this path the design falls back to lease-plus-watchdog bounds.
- [CONSENSUS-5] (medium) The Dell R740xd supports up to 12 NVDIMM-N modules of 16 GB (192 GB) backed by an NVDIMM battery, and that battery cannot be installed together with the 2.5-inch mid-drive tray.
  - why it matters: Durable NVLOG sizing (up to 75 GB per node at 10GbE) and the claim that RPO=0 survives correlated power loss depend on this capacity and on the chassis configuration allowing the battery.
- [CONSENSUS-6] (high) iDRAC9 supports Redfish POST /redfish/v1/Systems/System.Embedded.1/Actions/ComputerSystem.Reset with ResetType ForceOff and reports PowerState for confirmation, and IPMI over LAN is disabled by default on current iDRAC9 firmware (Dell DSA-2024-099).
  - why it matters: The fence ladder's second rung is Redfish, not fence_ipmilan; if ForceOff confirmation were unavailable the survivor would always have to wait the 8 s watchdog bound.
- [CONSENSUS-7] (medium) Default initiator path-failover windows are: ESXi iSCSI RecoveryTimeout 10 s, Windows MPIO PDORemovePeriod 20 s, Linux open-iscsi node.session.timeo.replacement_timeout 120 s, and Linux NVMe ctrl_loss_tmo 600 s with a 10 s reconnect delay, so a takeover that completes in 15 s or less does not surface I/O errors to guests using ALUA/ANA multipath.
  - why it matters: The detection, lease and watchdog timers (2 s, 3 s, 5 s) and the 15 s takeover target are derived from these windows.
- [CONSENSUS-8] (medium) mdadm provides no protection against two hosts assembling the same array from shared disks except through md-cluster (which requires DLM/Corosync), and the Dell HBA355e is a supported external 12G SAS HBA for the R740xd that can attach an MD1400/MD1420 in redundant-path (dual-EMM) mode.
  - why it matters: The shared-JBOD design requires our daemon to be the only assembly guard (auto-assembly disabled, assemble only after PR) and requires a Dell-supported dual-host cabling path; the MD1400-with-HBA355e combination is inferred from the MD24 matrix and needs checking.

### Opus-Security

#### Executive take
Build the Phase 1 N-Blade as a thin Rust ublk server behind the kernel's own targets (LIO iSCSI, nvmet-tcp) on Rocky 10.1, not as a Rust protocol stack: the kernel gives us interoperable iSCSI/NVMe-oF, CHAP, ALUA/ANA, VAAI and five fabrics (iSCSI, FC via tcm_qla2xxx, NVMe/TCP, NVMe/RDMA, NVMe/FC) for one backstore, and Phase 1's real deliverable ("block write → RAM → mirror → ack") is a block-level daemon anyway. Long term the paths diverge only for NVMe: keep LIO for iSCSI/FC-SCSI permanently (a from-scratch SCSI target that ESXi and Windows Failover Clustering accept is 2+ engineer-years of negative-value work) and write a native Rust NVMe/TCP+RDMA target once we need zero-copy and cluster-aware reservations. The single biggest risk is outside my area but hollows it out: with node-local disks there is nothing for the partner to take over, so ALUA/ANA failover to the partner is theatre unless disks move to dual-ported SAS JBODs or every CP is also replicated onto the partner's disks. The thing I most disagree with is FC as the mirror transport: a SCSI-WRITE-to-partner-LUN mirror over tcm_qla2xxx in dual mode is the most fragile stack in Linux storage and buys no latency over busy-polled 10GbE TCP (my estimate 60-100 us RTT vs 40-60 us), while 25GbE RoCE gets 10-15 us; FC belongs on the client side. "Stateless N-Blade" is coherent only if defined as "nothing that must survive the N-Blade": connection state dies with it and initiators reinstate sessions; LUN-scoped SCSI state (PR, unit attentions, mode params, NAA identity) must live in the D-Blade/control plane, which LIO does not do for us and which forces Active/Standby (not Active/Active) ALUA while LIO is the front end.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Kernel subsystems (Rocky 9 = 5.14 + backports; Rocky 10 = 6.12)
- **LIO core + `iscsi_target_mod`** (3.1+, both). In-tree docs claim MC/S and ERL0-2; treat both as unexercised, since every mainstream initiator runs ERL0 and only Windows uses MC/S. SPC-3 PR incl. APTPL, ALUA (implicit+explicit), COMPARE AND WRITE, XCOPY, UNMAP/WRITE SAME, VPD 0x80/0x83/0x86/0xB0/0xB2 are all emulated in `target_core_*`. PR state is per node (`/var/target/pr`).
- **`target_core_user` (TCMU)** (3.18+, both). `pgr_support`/`alua_support` writable since 5.8 (Bodo Stroesser): a userspace handler can own PR and ALUA itself, receiving nexus info per command. One ring + one lock per device; `tcmu-runner` (C) is the reference handler and implements INQUIRY/MODE SENSE/CAW/XCOPY/UNMAP in userspace, which is what a Rust handler would also have to do.
- **`ublk_drv`** (6.0+). Enabled as Technology Preview in RHEL 10.1 release notes; no RHEL 9 release note mentions it (I believe absent from 5.14-based kernels; verify `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK` in `/boot/config`). USER_RECOVERY 6.1, USER_COPY 6.4, zero-copy via registered buffers (upstream 6.15 I believe; not in 6.12). No block `pr_ops` (I believe; verify).
- **`target_core_iblock` pr_ops passthrough** ("Use block pr_ops in LIO", Mike Christie, merged ~6.4 I believe): useful only when the bdev has `pr_ops`, which ublk lacks.
- **`nvmet`**: tcp (5.0), rdma (4.8), fc (4.11, lpfc only), ANA groups (4.19), DH-HMAC-CHAP in-band auth (6.0), TLS 1.3 PSK via kTLS + `tlshd` (`CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS`, 6.7 I believe), reservations (committed Nov 2024 → 6.13 I believe, so NOT in 6.12), secure concatenation (newer than 6.12).
- **`tcm_qla2xxx` + `qla2xxx`** (3.5+). Dual mode: load with `qlini_mode=dual`, then per-port `/sys/class/scsi_host/hostX/qlini_mode` (commit 0645cb83); needs the 5.15 "restore initiator in dual mode" fix. Marvell: no NVMe target mode and none planned (Hannes Reinecke, linux-nvme, Apr 2025).
- **`lpfc`**: NVMe/FC target via `lpfc_enable_nvmet=<WWPNs>`; no in-tree SCSI target.
- **io_uring**: provided buffer rings 5.19, multishot recv + SENDZC 6.0, DEFER_TASKRUN 6.1, NAPI busy-poll registration 6.9, zcrx 6.15. RHEL 9 ships `kernel.io_uring_disabled=2` (confirmed in 9.4 release notes): `io_uring_setup()` fails with EPERM until the sysctl is changed.
- **kTLS** (TX 4.13, RX 4.17), `tlshd` from ktls-utils; **iDRAC9** Redfish/IPMI for fencing.

##### Rust crates (maturity as I see it)
- `libublk` 0.4.7 (Ming Lei, ublk maintainer): young API, right author; the base for `nbladed`. `rublk` is its reference binary.
- `io-uring` 0.7 (tokio-rs): mature low-level bindings; use directly in pinned per-queue threads. `monoio` 0.2 if we want a thread-per-core runtime; `tokio` for the control plane only.
- `zerocopy` 0.8 (header/CDB views without copies), `bytes`, `crc32c` (hardware CRC32C, used by both iSCSI and NVMe/TCP digests).
- Auth: RustCrypto `md-5`, `sha2`, `hmac` (mature) for CHAP and DH-HMAC-CHAP; `num-bigint` or `openssl` for RFC 7919 FFDHE groups.
- TLS: `rustls` 0.23 has no external-PSK API (I believe; verify); `openssl` 0.10 gained TLS 1.3 external PSK callbacks via PR #2687 (verify released); `ktls` crate to hand the session to the kernel afterwards.
- RDMA (Tier 3): thin FFI over `rdma-core` (`rdma-sys`); `async-rdma` is not production-grade.
- Testing: `cargo-fuzz` + `arbitrary`, `proptest`; observability: `tracing`, `metrics-exporter-prometheus`.
- Reference only, not dependencies: `ioutgt` (Rust NVMe/TCP+RDMA target on io_uring; early development, ~4 stars, needs ≥6.11, no in-band auth yet; passes xfstests quick); SPDK 24.x (`nvmf_tgt` production-grade, `iscsi_tgt` still shipped, no deprecation found); no pure-Rust iSCSI target exists (`scst` crate is an SCST config wrapper).

##### Hardware
- FC SCSI target: Marvell QLogic QLE2692 (16G) / QLE2742, QLE2772 (32G) with qla2xxx. FC-NVMe target: Emulex LPe32002/LPe35002 with lpfc. One card family cannot do both.
- Ethernet: Mellanox ConnectX-5/6 (RoCEv2, nvmet-rdma, header/data split for future zcrx); Intel X710/XXV710 fine for TCP-only tiers.
- NVLOG: Dell 16 GB NVDIMM-N, max 192 GB (12 modules, 2 CPUs, battery; incompatible with the 2.5" mid-drive tray per reseller docs; verify with Dell).

#### Proposed architecture
##### Phase 1 shape (identical on both nodes)
```
initiator --iSCSI/NVMe-TCP--> LIO / nvmet (kernel) --bio--> /dev/ublkbN --io_uring--> nbladed (Rust)
nbladed: queue thread i (pinned, 1:1 with NIC RSS queue i) -> ownership cache {lun -> dblade, epoch}
         -> DBladeClient: local (shm/UDS, same box) | remote (TCP now, RDMA later, FC-encapsulated if ever)
D-Blade: append NVLOG -> mirror to partner NVLOG -> ack -> nbladed completes ublk IO -> LIO/nvmet sends status
```
1. **Fabric config applier**: Rust writes configfs directly (no `targetcli`/`rtslib` at runtime; mask `target.service`). Desired state from the control plane: targets, TPGs, node ACLs (IQN/NQN + CHAP secrets), LUN maps, ALUA/ANA groups and states, identical `vpd_unit_serial`/NGUID on both nodes so the initiator sees one device with two paths.
2. **`nbladed` ublk server**: one ublk device per LUN, `nr_hw_queues` = data cores, `UBLK_F_USER_RECOVERY` so a daemon restart keeps `/dev/ublkbN` alive and initiators never see a path drop. Read/Write/Flush/Discard/WriteZeroes map 1:1 onto RPC ops; Flush is a no-op because every completed write is already mirrored.
3. **Ownership cache with epoch**: every RPC carries `(lun, epoch)`; the D-Blade rejects stale epochs; on `StaleEpoch`/`NotOwner` the daemon re-resolves from the control plane and retries, holding (not failing) IO for a bounded window under the initiators' timers.
4. **SCSI-state service (Phase 3+)**: PR table, UA queue, mode params as replicated per-LUN objects owned by the D-Blade. Served through a TCMU passthrough handler for "cluster" LUNs, or natively by the Rust NVMe target. Not possible through ublk+iblock.

##### Interface sketch
```rust
pub struct Epoch(u64);
pub enum BlockOp { Read, Write, Flush, Discard, WriteZeroes }
pub struct IoReq<'a> { obj: ObjectId /* LUN today, file extent later */, epoch: Epoch,
                       op: BlockOp, lba: u64, nblocks: u32, data: Option<&'a [u8]> }
pub enum IoStatus { Ok, StaleEpoch { current: Epoch, owner: NodeId }, NotOwner,
                    Media(SenseKey), Timeout }
pub trait DBladeClient: Send + Sync {
    /// exactly one call per client IO; `done` fires only after the mirror ack
    fn submit(&self, req: IoReq<'_>, done: Completion<IoStatus>) -> Result<(), SubmitError>;
    fn resolve(&self, obj: ObjectId) -> (NodeId, Epoch);
}
pub trait Transport: Send + Sync {            // Tcp (Phase 1) | Rdma | FcScsi (encapsulated)
    fn send(&self, hdr: &[u8], payload: &[IoSlice<'_>], on_sent: Completion<()>);
    fn recv(&self, ring: &BufRing, on_frame: &mut dyn FnMut(Frame<'_>));
    fn fence_epoch(&self, epoch: Epoch);      // partner refuses frames below this
}
```
##### Key decisions
- **Kernel targets, not Rust stacks**: interoperability with ESXi/Windows/Linux initiators in week 1; every SCSI/NVMe corner case already handled; attack surface is the kernel's, not ours.
- **ublk over TCMU**: one backstore serves iSCSI, FC, NVMe/TCP/RDMA/FC; multi-queue scales per core; it is where upstream investment goes; `libublk` exists. TCMU stays as the bridge for cluster-correct PR/ALUA when needed.
- **Active/Standby ALUA (owner AO, partner Standby) while LIO fronts the LUN**: LIO's COMPARE AND WRITE and PR execute in that node's kernel, so two active nodes would break VMFS ATS and WSFC reservations. NVMe mirrors this with ANA optimized/inaccessible.
- **Copies on 6.12**: socket→LIO page (kernel), request page→ublk buffer (kernel), ublk buffer→mirror socket (SENDZC pins instead of copying): two copies per write; 6.15+ zero-copy removes one.
- **One binary, two roles**: `DBladeClient` is a trait object; Phase 1 runs N and D in-process on a per-core SPSC ring, Phase 3 swaps in TCP/RDMA. Even the in-process path serializes the header (not the payload) so the RPC contract is frozen now.
- **NFS/SMB door**: `IoReq` keys on `ObjectId` + 64-bit offset, not `LunId`+LBA, so a file later is "many objects". File semantics still need an inode/metadata layer, a lock manager, Ganesha's grace-period recovery backend and a ctdb replacement for Samba: the Phase 1 RPC does not preclude them, but it is 20% of the work.

#### Bottlenecks
##### 4K write at QD1, iSCSI, Phase 1 stack (estimates; measure in week 2)
| Stage | 10GbE | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Initiator (open-iscsi; ESXi similar, Windows worse) | 15-25 us | |
| Wire 4,210 B (BHS+data+TCP/IP/Eth) + NIC/IRQ/TCP to LIO rx thread | 3.4 + 10-20 us | coalescing off, RSS aligned |
| LIO parse, iblock bio, ublk wake | 5-10 us | 1 context switch |
| ublk 4K copy + local RPC (shm) | 2-4 us | |
| D-Blade NVLOG append + mirror send | 2 + 3.4 us | |
| Partner NIC→daemon→NVLOG→ack (kernel TCP) | 15-25 us | busy-poll: 8-12 us |
| Ack return + completion | 10-20 us | |
| LIO status PDU → client | 10-15 us | |
Sum ≈ 90-150 us → 6.5-11K IOPS per QD1 stream; with `IORING_REGISTER_NAPI` busy-poll on both ends 70-100 us. **1GbE**: +34 us serialization per 4K hop twice plus ~50-80 us NIC RTT → 250-350 us, ~3K IOPS QD1; mirror link cap 125 MB/s ÷ 4.3 KB ≈ 28K writes/s (the Phase 1 throughput ceiling, fine for protocol work). **FC 16G mirror** via tcm_qla2xxx: 2.4 us wire but HBA DMA/IRQ 10-15 us per side + LIO + ublk on the partner ≈ 60-100 us RTT → 130-200 us total: no gain. **25GbE RoCE**: 5-10 us RTT → 60-80 us total, the only transport that materially moves the number.

##### QD32 and per-node ceilings
- CPU/IO: LIO ~10-15 us (rx+tx threads), ublk 3-5 us, D-Blade+mirror 5-8 us → ~25 us/IO ≈ 40K IOPS per core; 8 data cores ≈ 250-300K 4K write IOPS theoretical.
- Mirror link: 10GbE 1.25 GB/s ÷ 4.3 KB ≈ 290K/s wire max, 200-250K practical. CP drain: 250K × 4K = 1 GB/s sustained into 12 HDDs (10 data × ~200 MB/s = 2 GB/s raw full-stripe; after parity, GC and read competition 0.8-1.5 GB/s): 10GbE mirror and HDD flush rate are balanced, which is the sweet spot; 25GbE would starve the disks.
- LIO: one rx/tx thread pair per connection ≈ 100-150K IOPS per session; ESXi uses one session per path. NVMe/TCP (queue per core) sidesteps this; TCMU ≈ 100-200K IOPS per device (single ring); ublk ≈ 1M+ per device upstream.
- Memory: NVLOG = interval × ingest × 2 (double buffer) × 2 (own + partner) = 15 s × 1 GB/s × 4 = 60 GB per node (fits 192 GB NVDIMM-N max); 1GbE prototype 15 s × 110 MB/s × 4 = 6.6 GB. N-Blade in-flight budget: window × MaxBurstLength = 64 × 256 KB = 16 MB per session; 1,000 sessions = 16 GB worst case → global cap 2-4 GB enforced by MaxCmdSN/SQ depth, not TCP window.
- Reads: 12 HDDs × ~150 random IOPS ≈ 1.8K IOPS at 8-12 ms; this, not writes, is what initiators will feel; a cold-read storm from one VM will dominate per-LUN queues.
- Takeover: ESXi iSCSI RecoveryTimeout/NoopOut ~10 s class, Windows MPIO PDORemovePeriod ~20 s, Linux NVMe ctrl_loss_tmo 600 s. Detection + Raft + ownership flip + ALUA/ANA flip must land in ≤10 s target, 25 s hard ceiling.

#### Creative solutions
- **The mirror is the fence.** RPO=0 means no ack without the partner; stamp every mirror frame with the owner epoch and have the partner refuse stale epochs (`fence_epoch`). A stale owner then physically cannot acknowledge a write; the external witness is only needed to decide who may run unmirrored.
- **LUN as a replicated SCSI object.** NAA/serial, PR table, UA queue, mode params, ALUA/ANA state live in the D-Blade log, so they are mirrored with RPO=0 for free; N-Blades read them at session setup and subscribe to changes. This is what makes "stateless" honest.
- **Flush is free.** Because completion implies mirrored-in-NVLOG, FLUSH/FUA/SYNCHRONIZE CACHE are no-ops and we advertise WCE=0 / VWC=0, provided NVLOG is on NVDIMM. That removes the fsync-storm class from the latency budget.
- **1:1:1 core mapping.** NIC RSS queue i ↔ LIO/nvmet threads pinned by cpuset ↔ ublk queue i ↔ D-Blade shard i; `IORING_REGISTER_NAPI` busy-poll on data cores; core 0 and management cores untouched.
- **FC as a client fabric, not the mirror.** tcm_qla2xxx on the same ublk backstore gives FC SCSI targets for free; the mirror goes 10GbE TCP → 25GbE RoCE. Keep `FcScsi` as a `Transport` impl (mirror LUN = append-only log where LBA = log offset, header in the payload) that we write only if a customer insists.
- **Differential conformance.** The future Rust NVMe target runs side by side with nvmet on the same backstore under blktests/nvme-cli/fio-verify; LIO is the iSCSI oracle. Fuzz seeds come from pcaps of ESXi, Windows and open-iscsi logins.
- **ublk `pr_ops` upstream patch** (~300-500 LOC forwarding PR ops as ublk commands) unlocks cluster-wide PR on the ublk path without TCMU; worth proposing to Ming Lei in Phase 3.
- **Slowloris by construction.** No data-buffer allocation before a fully validated header; login phase on a cheap epoll thread with 30 s negotiation timeout and ≤4 half-open logins per source; nftables connlimit (≤16/source) in front of LIO, whose half-open connections each cost a kernel thread pair.
- **Read-miss transparency.** Serve recent writes from the NVLOG index before touching HDD, and export per-LUN read-miss latency so initiator timeouts can be tuned instead of guessed.
- **UPS-triggered CP as a belt, NVDIMM as the braces**: never the belt alone.

#### Holes in the plan
- **Node-local disks make takeover impossible.** The partner's RAM mirror holds only unflushed CPs; everything older sits on the dead node's internal bays, which the R740xd backplane cannot share. Either (a) move data disks to dual-ported SAS JBODs (MD1420-class, both HBA330s cabled; SATA drives are single-ported and need interposers, so "SAS/SATA" becomes "SAS") and fence with per-drive PR plus iDRAC STONITH before `mdadm --assemble` on the survivor, or (b) replicate every CP to the partner's disks too (SyncMirror/DRBD-style: half the capacity and disk bandwidth, and the mirror RPC must carry logical (obj, offset, len) writes, not raw NVLOG bytes, so the replica can apply them independently). Decide before Phase 1 because it defines the mirror frame.
- **"Stateless N-Blade" is incoherent as written.** Sessions carry StatSN/CmdSN windows, ITT maps, R2T state, negotiated parameters; LUNs carry PR, UA, mode pages. Only the first group may die with the N-Blade (initiators reinstate via session reinstatement/reconnect); the second must be cluster state. LIO keeps PR per node and executes COMPARE AND WRITE locally (read-then-write under a per-device semaphore), so Active/Active across two nodes corrupts VMFS ATS and WSFC arbitration: use Active/Standby ALUA and ANA optimized/inaccessible until PR/CAW move to the D-Blade.
- **PR survives failover only by luck.** WSFC re-arbitrates reservations periodically (I believe every ~3 s; verify); VMware in-guest clustering with RDMs does not. Phase 1 must document "PR state lost on takeover".
- **2-node Raft is worse than no Raft**: majority of 2 is 2, so one node down halts the control plane. Add a third voter (`opentapd --witness` on a management host/VM) or a quorum device; ONTAP's mailbox-disk trick needs shared disks.
- **RAM without NV is not RPO=0.** Both nodes on one PDU/UPS lose up to 15 s × ingest (GBs) together, while we advertise WCE=0. NVDIMM-N (192 GB max) or Optane PMem for NVLOG, with checksummed, sequence-numbered log records replayed on boot; never in-place structures. Otherwise advertise WCE=1 and let SYNCHRONIZE CACHE force a mini-CP, which destroys the sequential-CP premise under fsync-heavy VMs.
- **FC replication: wrong tier, right fabric.** Only qla2xxx has a SCSI target; it needs module-wide `qlini_mode=dual` because each port must be initiator (send mirror) and target (receive); dual mode has a regression history (the 5.15 restore-initiator fix is one), and NVMe/FC target exists only on Emulex, which has no SCSI target. No latency win (see budget), no RPC semantics, no lab testing without real HBAs and a switch or point-to-point link. Demote to Tier 2 client-facing.
- **Rocky 9 cannot host this design**: io_uring disabled by default, no ublk, nvmet without auth/TLS. Rocky 10.1 is the baseline; 10.0 may lack `ublk_drv` entirely (verify).
- **Windows needs iSCSI**: no GA NVMe/TCP initiator (Insider preview, no multipath). "NVMe-oF/TCP first" is fine for ESXi 7.0U3+/Linux only.
- **nvmet reservations are post-6.12** (I believe 6.13): NVMe LUNs for WSFC/VMware clustering need a backport or the native target.
- **"One massive contiguous stripe"** needs full-stripe-aligned writes (12-drive RAID6, 512 KiB chunk → 5 MiB stripes), `stripe_cache_size` raised ~32768, `group_thread_cnt` > 0, and a write journal or NVDIMM-backed PPL to close the RAID6 write hole; GC reads of live blocks hit the 150-IOPS-per-HDD wall and compete with client reads. "Network speed" is true for writes only.
- **Takeover timing vs initiator timers** is unbudgeted; ESXi APD begins after ~10-25 s of no paths.
- **LIO per-connection threads** cap per-session IOPS at ~100-150K; irrelevant for HDD-backed Phase 1, relevant for any SSD tier.
- **Attack surface in Phase 1 is the kernel's** (LIO login parser and nvmet-tcp have had CVEs); our Rust surface is the RPC/mirror framing and the configfs applier: fuzz them anyway, bind data listeners only to data LIFs, management (gRPC/`tonic` with mTLS) only on a separate VLAN/NIC, nftables default-deny on data NICs except 3260/4420/8009, CHAP and `allow_any_host=0` mandatory by default, discovery auth on.

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build
- Rocky 10.1, `nbladed` (Rust, `libublk` + `io-uring`) exporting one ublk device per LUN with user recovery, fronted by LIO iSCSI and nvmet-tcp configured by a Rust configfs applier from a declarative spec. D-Blade and mirror in the same binary behind `DBladeClient`/`Transport` traits with TCP as the only transport implementation; a loopback `Transport` for tests.
- Mirror frame = `{epoch, seq, obj, offset, len, crc32c, data}`; partner applies, fences by epoch, acks; owner completes.
- ALUA (owner AO / partner Standby) and ANA (optimized / inaccessible) wired to the ownership epoch, flipped by a manual `takeover` command (Raft arrives in Phase 4).
- Decide disk topology (shared JBOD vs full replica) before coding the frame.

##### Prove (measurable)
1. ESXi 8, Windows Server 2022 (iSCSI, MPIO) and Rocky/Ubuntu (iSCSI + NVMe/TCP) initiators connect with CHAP, format and run fio for 24 h; `libiscsi` test suite and `blktests` nvme group pass.
2. 4K write QD1 ≤ 150 us end-to-end with mirror over 10GbE (≤ 350 us over 1GbE); QD32 ≥ 100K 4K write IOPS per node over 10GbE, ≥ 20K over 1GbE.
3. Kill the owner (power off via iDRAC) during `fio --verify`: zero acknowledged writes missing when reading back from the partner's NVLOG; repeat 50 times.
4. Restart `nbladed` under load: no initiator path drop, no I/O error (ublk user recovery).
5. Manual takeover: ESXi path failover with no VM I/O errors and ALUA/ANA flip visible on both nodes within 5 s (requires the partner to have the data: the disk-topology decision).
6. 24 h `cargo-fuzz` on mirror-frame and RPC parsers with no crash or OOM; allocation caps enforced under a 1,000-session login flood.
7. Latency table above re-measured with and without io_uring NAPI busy-poll, published as the Phase 3 RPC baseline.

#### Open questions
- **Shared JBOD or full disk replication?** Unblocks the mirror frame format (raw NVLOG bytes vs logical writes), the SAS-vs-SATA purchase, and whether ALUA failover to the partner is real in Phase 1.
- **Will the pair get NVDIMM-N/Optane for NVLOG?** Unblocks WCE/VWC advertisement, flush semantics and CP sizing (60 GB NV vs DRAM + UPS).
- **Is Rocky 10.1 acceptable as the baseline** (ublk and io_uring), or must we support Rocky 9? Unblocks ublk-vs-TCMU and io_uring-vs-epoll.
- **Is FC replication a customer requirement or an architectural preference?** Unblocks whether `FcScsi` transport and dual-mode qla2xxx hardware enter the plan at all, versus FC as a client fabric only.
- **Which clustered workloads must survive takeover with reservations intact** (WSFC, VMware in-guest clustering)? Unblocks the TCMU passthrough handler vs waiting for the native NVMe target.
- **Third vote**: may a witness process run on a management host/VM? Unblocks Phase 4's Raft topology.
- **Read-performance expectation**: is a RAM/SSD read cache in scope? Unblocks whether the N-Blade needs read-side caching and what latency we promise initiators.
- **Client fabric priority for Tier 3**: NVMe/RDMA (RoCE) or 100GbE TCP? Unblocks NIC choice (ConnectX-6 vs commodity) and whether the native Rust target needs ibverbs in year one.

#### Load-bearing claims
- [SECURITY-1] (high) RHEL/Rocky 9 ships with kernel.io_uring_disabled=2 by default, so io_uring_setup() fails with EPERM for all processes until the sysctl is changed.
  - why it matters: Decides whether the Phase 1 data path (libublk, io-uring multishot recv, SENDZC) can run on Rocky 9 at all; drives the Rocky 10.1 baseline recommendation.
- [SECURITY-2] (medium) ublk_drv is not shipped in RHEL 9's 5.14-based kernel and is first enabled (as Technology Preview) in RHEL/Rocky 10.1; Rocky 10.0 may lack the module.
  - why it matters: ublk is the chosen bridge between kernel targets and the Rust daemon; if ublk is unavailable, Phase 1 falls back to TCMU and loses nvmet fronting.
- [SECURITY-3] (medium) TCMU's pgr_support and alua_support attributes are writable since kernel 5.8 so a userspace handler can implement PR and ALUA itself, whereas ublk exposes no block pr_ops, so LIO iblock over ublk cannot pass PR commands to the daemon.
  - why it matters: Determines that cluster-wide persistent reservations require the TCMU path (or a native NVMe target), not the ublk path, and forces Active/Standby ALUA in Phase 1.
- [SECURITY-4] (high) Marvell qla2xxx supports FC SCSI target mode via tcm_qla2xxx (dual initiator+target mode via qlini_mode=dual with per-port sysfs control) but has no NVMe/FC target mode and none planned, while Emulex lpfc supports NVMe/FC target but has no in-tree SCSI target.
  - why it matters: Fixes the HBA purchase (QLogic for FC SCSI, Emulex for FC-NVMe, never both on one card) and underpins the argument that FC as the mirror transport is the most fragile option.
- [SECURITY-5] (high) No usable pure-Rust iSCSI target exists, and the only Rust NVMe/TCP+RDMA target (ioutgt) is early-stage (about 4 GitHub stars, requires Linux 6.11+, lacks in-band auth).
  - why it matters: Justifies using kernel LIO/nvmet in Phase 1 and treating a native Rust NVMe target as a later, from-scratch effort rather than a crate dependency.
- [SECURITY-6] (high) Windows has no generally available NVMe/TCP initiator as of mid-2026 (Insider preview only, without multipathing), so Windows clients require iSCSI.
  - why it matters: Means iSCSI (and therefore LIO) cannot be deferred or dropped; 'NVMe-oF/TCP first' applies only to ESXi and Linux initiators.
- [SECURITY-7] (high) LIO implements COMPARE AND WRITE in target_core_sbc as a read-then-write serialized by a per-device semaphore, and keeps persistent reservations per node, so neither is atomic or consistent across two nodes exporting the same LUN.
  - why it matters: Forces Active/Standby (owner AO, partner Standby) ALUA while LIO fronts the LUN; Active/Active would corrupt VMFS ATS locking and WSFC arbitration.
- [SECURITY-8] (medium) A 4K write costs ~3.4 us of serialization on 10GbE and ~30-50 us kernel-TCP round trip between userspace daemons, so a mirrored 4K write via LIO→ublk→D-Blade→partner totals ~90-150 us at QD1 (about 7-11K IOPS per stream), a 1GbE mirror link caps at ~28K 4K writes/s, and an FC-SCSI mirror via tcm_qla2xxx (~60-100 us RTT) is no faster than 10GbE TCP.
  - why it matters: The latency budget, the 1GbE prototype ceiling, and the recommendation to demote FC from mirror transport to client fabric all rest on this arithmetic.

## Round 2: cross-examination

### By Opus-Network

#### Opus-Network on Opus-Storage
**1. `LogTransport::append -> Result<Seq, Gap>` plus "partner appends, CRC-checks, ACKs".** That is an in-order stream with a userspace receiver that assigns seq and sees gaps. Your own FC leg (SCSI WRITE at a ring offset, kernel-only receive path, daemon polls the mapped ring) has no such receiver: nobody returns `Seq`, nobody sees `Gap` in-line, and SCSI/NVMe commands complete out of order, so a gap is a normal transient. The trait cannot be implemented by the transport you want. Instead: sender-chosen slot offsets, self-validating records (header CRC, trailer CRC, seq, lap/epoch), out-of-order tolerant, gap detection only at replay (maximal contiguous prefix); `retire(upto)` becomes a status record the sender writes.

**2. FC ring over `tcm_qla2xxx` + LIO fileio.** `# CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX is not set` in the Rocky r9 and r10 kernel configs (verified): out-of-tree forever. The semantics you want (kernel DAX copy, no daemon) ship in-box as an `nvmet-fc` file-backed namespace on fsdax over Emulex lpfc (`CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FC=m` on both); the sender writes the ring at `offset` on `/dev/nvmeXnY`. Same bytes, same persistence path, no kernel builds.

**3. "PMem as the arena" with `WRITE_FIXED`/registered buffers.** Verified in `mm/gup.c` `check_vma_flags()` for v5.14 and v6.12: `FOLL_LONGTERM && vma_is_fsdax(vma)` returns `-EOPNOTSUPP`. `IORING_REGISTER_BUFFERS` and `ibv_reg_mr` on an fsdax mapping fail on both Rocky kernels; only devdax (`/dev/daxX.Y`, `S_ISCHR`) is exempt. But the ring a kernel target writes must be an fsdax *file*: a bdev namespace on `/dev/pmem0` leaves the daemon's mmap page-cache-backed and stale against nvmet's direct bios. So: local NVLOG/arena on devdax (pinnable), partner mirror ring on an fsdax file (replay/CP from it via plain O_DIRECT, short-term pins). Spec the split; it also partitions the 192 GB.

**4. 4 KiB-aligned records.** A 64-B header plus 4 KiB payload rounded to 4 KiB is 8 KiB per 4 KiB write: 2x NVLOG bytes, 2x mirror wire bytes, half the NAND endurance, and the 10GbE 4K ceiling falls from ~290K/s to ~150K/s. pmem needs 8-byte atomicity, not 4 KiB: pack records byte-contiguously behind a trailer CRC and 4 KiB-align only NVMe-NVLOG batches.

**5. The throughput arithmetic stops at the client NIC.** 2x10GbE client ingest (2.2 GB/s) means the mirror link also carries >=2.2 GB/s per direction and the NVLOG device absorbs 4.4 GB/s (own + partner): that excludes NAND, P4800X (~2 GB/s) and even a P5800X on PCIe 3.0 x4 (~3.3 GB/s), leaving NVDIMM-N/PMem. Your criterion ">= 2 GB/s into NVLOG + mirror on a 10GbE pair" is unachievable on one link (1.25 GB/s payload), and LACP hashes per flow, so it needs >=2 mirror connections on 2 links. Write the per-node BOM: 2x client NICs, 2x mirror NICs (or 25GbE), pmem.

**6. "D-Blade 4 KiB ack 100-200 us, p50 <= 150 us"** bakes in interrupt-driven, unpinned kernel TCP. Pinned busy-polled links do 20-30 us RTT with local persist overlapped; target <= 60 us at the D-Blade boundary, or iSCSI clients see 250-400 us and the "network speed" claim dies at first measurement. (You are right that `_mm_clwb` is absent from `core::arch`; my brainstorm was wrong, `asm!` it is.)

#### Opus-Network on Opus-Consensus
**1. "Raft RPCs ride the same `Transport` as the mirror so Raft can run over FC"; matrix row "E down, M up: Raft falls back to M".** The in-box FC mirror is `nvmet-fc`, and an lpfc port in nvmet mode is target-only: it refuses and never sends FCP logins (lpfc nvmet Pt2Pt commit). The FC "link" is two unidirectional NVMe write paths with no receive endpoint and no request/response. Raft and heartbeats over it are a polled mailbox (100 us write, ~1 ms poll): fine for a few commits a minute, but a different transport whose completion means "placed", never "received". Run Raft on TCP over cluster and management networks; use ring `CONTROL` records as the mailbox second path; model it in turmoil as a polled channel. Partner attestation on FC needs both unidirectional paths: one cable loss is "I can write to B but cannot hear B", which your lease rule must classify.

**2. "Epochs reject stragglers" / "receivers reject anything with epoch < applied epoch".** Not on the NVMe/FC ring. The kernel target accepts any write from a connected host; removing a host from nvmet `allowed_hosts` blocks new connects, and I found no path that drops live controllers (verify); the reliable rung is unlinking the subsystem from the port (`nvmet_port_del_ctrls`). A stale owner's records land and its client acks succeed unless its own lease has expired: on FC the lease+watchdog bound is the *only* fence and epoch is cleanup at apply/replay. Your design holds; the ladder needs the unlink rung and the matrix must say "lease expiry prevents stragglers".

**3. Timers.** You: 100 ms heartbeats, 2 s silence, L = 3 s, `GrantUnmirrored` after one commit. Me: 20 ms heartbeat records, link dead at 1 s, `Stalled` up to 5 s. An owner queueing writes for 5 s past a 3 s lease admits after expiry; `Stalled` must be bounded by the lease, and the grant must reach the data path via the seqlock page, since the mirror is down. One table, owned by you.

**4. Resync as "ship the CP segments B missed".** Physical segment shipping needs identical array geometry and freezes the partner's allocator for that aggregate; Storage's shadow replica applies *logical* records into the partner's own LFS and Security's frames are logical. Pick logical: within two CP intervals catch-up is ring replay; beyond that ship records by seq range, never segments.

**5. "FC 20-50 us" in the reality check.** Through any Linux target stack a 4K FC write is 50-150 us (two FCP round trips or NVMe first burst, HBA firmware per hop, DAX copy); busy-polled 10GbE TCP is 20-30 us. Your timers are indifferent; the roadmap pitch is not.

**6. Shared SAS-SSD journal.** Good, and it is my `RemoteRing` over `/dev/sdX`: per-node regions, PR keys per SSD, 100-200 us FUA writes, JBOD only. Say it is the same abstraction so it does not become a fourth transport.

#### Opus-Network on Opus-Security
**1. "Five fabrics for one backstore" and "FC as a client fabric for free via `tcm_qla2xxx`".** `CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX` is not set on Rocky 9 or 10 (verified): FC-SCSI as a *client* fabric is exactly as out-of-tree as the FC mirror. The only in-box FC is NVMe/FC on Emulex, which can front `/dev/ublkbN` as an nvmet bdev namespace and which Windows cannot initiate. Moving FC to the client side does not dodge the kernel decision. State the matrix: Emulex NVMe/FC in-box, ESXi/Linux only; QLogic FC-SCSI needs our own kernel, Windows OK.

**2. "The mirror is the fence"; `fence_epoch` "partner refuses frames below this".** True only with a userspace receiver. For the NVMe/FC ring, including your own `FcScsi` mirror-LUN idea, the kernel target refuses nothing: a stale owner lands records and acks its client. Lease expiry is the fence; `StaleEpoch` comes from the seqlock page, never from the mirror; the trait must advertise `can_reject`.

**3. `UBLK_F_USER_RECOVERY` → "restart under load, no I/O error".** The ublk recovery docs: plain `USER_RECOVERY` *aborts* requests already issued to the daemon; only `UBLK_F_USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE` requeues them, and it "may issue the same I/O request twice". The criterion needs REISSUE plus idempotent mirroring: duplicate records for one client write, deduped by `req_id` or applied as idempotent block writes in seq order, where the first copy may already be mirrored under an older seq.

**4. "10GbE mirror ≈ HDD flush rate is the sweet spot; 25GbE would starve the disks".** The cache exists to absorb bursts *above* the flush rate; a mirror link equal to it caps pair ingest at 1.25 GB/s and makes NVLOG size irrelevant to throughput. The mirror must exceed peak client ingest (Storage's 2x10GbE client needs 25GbE or 2x10GbE mirror); Storage's token bucket protects the disks, not the link.

**5. Copies and the N→D contract.** LIO/nvmet → ublk (USER_COPY on 6.12) → daemon → `sendmsg` is two copies per write; my "payload lands once in the arena" criterion is unachievable behind LIO, and ublk zero-copy (6.15) is not reaching Rocky 10 soon. Acceptable for Phase 1 (4.4 GB/s of memcpy ≈ one core), but freeze the *slot* contract too: the ublk copy target must be the NVLOG slot (devdax or DRAM) with the record header prepended, or the native NVMe/TCP target later needs a second RPC.

**6. Active/Standby ALUA + ANA inaccessible** means no cross-node data path exists until the native target: the N-Blade ownership cache and `StaleEpoch` retry loop are dead code in Phases 1-3 and takeover is an ALUA flip. Say so; Phase 3's RPC shrinks to same-node, and my two-crossing cross-node path is deferred with it.

#### Cross-cutting conflicts (Opus-Network)
- **Transport trait shape.** Opus-Storage `append → Seq` (ordered stream, receiver assigns seq); Opus-Security and Opus-Consensus `send/recv/fence_epoch` (messaging, receiver rejects, Raft rides it); Opus-Network `RemoteRing` (put bytes at a slot, no receiver). Only put-at-offset is implementable over `nvmet-fc`; Raft needs messaging. Two traits (data ring + control messages over TCP, ring `CONTROL` records as a mailbox fallback); it cannot be one.
- **Receiver in the hot path.** Storage, Security and Consensus all assume the partner daemon CRC-checks, epoch-checks and acks every record; Network's BlockRing has no userspace receiver (kernel DAX write completion is the ack). Both cannot be the primary path: the choice decides whether in-line epoch rejection exists, whether FC gets a `Durable` ack, and whether the receive side costs a core.
- **What fences.** Security: the mirror. Consensus: lease + watchdog + PR. Network: the FC/NVMe ring cannot reject. Only the lease works on every transport; epoch is cleanup.
- **Full-replication model.** Storage: logical shadow replica applied into the partner's own LFS. Consensus: physical CP segment shipping. Security: logical frames. Physical and logical cannot both be the resync protocol.
- **Ring granularity.** Consensus: takeover unit = aggregate. Storage: two aggregates per 24-bay node. Network: one ring per node pair. Must become one ring and credit budget per aggregate, which splits the 192 GB NVDIMM-N four ways.
- **pmem mapping mode.** Storage's registered `WRITE_FIXED` arena and Network's VerbsRing MR need devdax (fsdax long-term pins return `-EOPNOTSUPP` on 5.14 and 6.12, verified); BlockRing's receive ring needs an fsdax file. One region cannot be both. Also `_mm_clwb`: Storage is right, Network was wrong, `asm!`.
- **Bandwidth BOM.** Storage: 2x10GbE client ingest (2.2 GB/s). Security: 10GbE mirror "balanced". Network: mirror >= ingest. Storage's "2 GB/s on a 10GbE pair" criterion cannot be met as written; NAND/P4800X NVLOG cannot absorb own + partner at that rate.
- **FC port topology.** lpfc nvmet ports are target-only, so a bidirectional mirror needs 2 ports per node for one path each way, 4 for redundancy; Security's qla2xxx dual mode is the only single-port option and is out-of-tree. Consensus's "FC as a second heartbeat path" inherits both.
- **Timers.** Consensus 100 ms / 2 s / L=3 s / 8 s watchdog vs Network 20 ms / 1 s dead / 5 s `Stalled` / 200 ms batch deadline vs Security's <=10 s takeover target.
- **Phase 1 front door.** Security's LIO/nvmet + ublk vs Network's ublk + N→D shared-memory ring vs Storage's in-process load generator: layerable (generator → D-Blade API ← ublk ← LIO) only if Network's zero-copy success criterion is dropped.

#### Questions for the lead architect (Opus-Network)
1. **Shared dual-ported JBOD, node-local shadow replica, or manual failover?** Decides the receive-side consumer (scan-at-takeover vs continuous logical apply), the resync protocol, ring-per-aggregate, and the BOM.
2. **Is a receive-side daemon in the mirror hot path acceptable (Storage/Security/Consensus), or is the kernel-only BlockRing the primary path (Network)?** Equivalently: must FC give a `Durable` ack with no daemon?
3. **Which FC:** Emulex NVMe/FC in-box (ports target-only, no Windows FC clients), QLogic FC-SCSI on a self-built/ELRepo kernel (dual mode, Windows OK), or demote FC below 25GbE RoCE? Is direct-connect mandatory, or may we require switches?
4. **May the appliance ship its own kernel?** Unlocks `tcm_qla2xxx`, ublk zero-copy and newer io_uring; otherwise Rocky 10 in-box is the ceiling.
5. **Is lease + hardware watchdog (+ PR where a JBOD exists) accepted as the sole data-path fence, with epoch as cleanup?** And is a witness voter allowed?
6. **Is pmem (NVDIMM-N or Optane PMem) in the BOM?** If yes: capacity per direction and the devdax/fsdax split. If no: is a `Placed` (DRAM) ack acceptable for v1 with a written RPO caveat for dual power loss?
7. **Peak client ingest per node (1 or 2 x 10GbE)?** Fixes the mirror link spec (2x10GbE, 25GbE, 16/32GFC) and the NVLOG device class.
8. **Initiator population and maximum tolerated stall (10/20/60 s)?** Produces the single timer table: heartbeat, silence, L, `Stalled` bound, batch deadline, watchdog.
9. **Who owns the record format:** Storage's 4 KiB-aligned `RecHdr`, or a packed 64-B header + trailer CRC that is also the wire format and the replay unit?
10. **Takeover granularity = aggregate (Consensus): one mirror ring and credit budget per aggregate, yes or no?**

### By Opus-Storage

#### Opus-Storage on Opus-Network
**1. Dense record framing defeats the zero-copy CP flush.** You propose a 64 B header + payload + 4 B trailer, packed at sender-chosen offsets. Every payload after the first is then misaligned by 68 B; O_DIRECT on `/dev/mdX` requires 512 B (4 KiB on 4Kn members) alignment of each iovec and `WRITE_FIXED` cannot fix that up, so "zero copies from NIC to NVLOG to mirror to CP flush" silently becomes a memcpy of every CP (22 GB per CP, 2+ s of a core, 2x staging DRAM). Instead: 4 KiB-aligned records (padded header, or a parallel 64 B header ring with payload at 4 KiB granularity); 1.5% space at 4 KiB writes is cheaper than the copy. This is the contract to settle first.

**2. fsdax rings cannot be registered buffers or RDMA MRs.** You put LocalRing/BlockRing on an fsdax file and register "the arena" as one MR for VerbsRing. The kernel refuses FOLL_LONGTERM pins on fsdax VMAs (`check_dax_vmas`, EOPNOTSUPP; [patchwork](https://patchwork.kernel.org/comment/22997513), [lkml](https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/1904.3/05751.html)), and both io_uring buffer registration and `ib_umem_get` use long-term pins; devdax pages are pinnable. Instead: a devdax namespace for the owner's NVLOG (my "PMem as the arena", CP reads straight from it) and an fsdax namespace only for the kernel-written inbound BlockRing; verify on 6.12.

**3. Ring sizing ignores CP duration.** "Ring >= 2 x interval x ingest, freed at the owner's CP boundary" frees space at CP *start*; it can only be freed after the superblock FUA. At line rate a 22 GB CP takes 16-32 s on 12 HDDs (back-to-back CPs), so the ring must hold active + frozen + the CP in flight: 3-4 intervals, 70-90 GB per direction at 2x10GbE, which with the own NVLOG overflows 192 GB of NVDIMM-N. Instead: size = ingest x (interval + max CP duration), `retire` driven by CP commit, my 50%/90% watermarks as the shared backpressure contract, or an explicit ingest cap.

**4. BlockRing has no consumer.** With node-local disks the partner must apply every record to its own log-structured volume at ingest rate; that needs a polling consumer with its own `consumed` pointer, the owner's "free at own CP boundary" rule no longer holds, and CRC verification before ACK vanishes on the FC path. Instead: define the consumer and `consumed` pointer in the ring protocol for both models and document that FC verifies CRC lazily. (Also consider disabling DDIO allocating writes on the HBA/NIC root port so inbound DMA lands in the ADR domain and an RDMA READ after WRITE proves persistence without a receiver CPU.)

**5. "The link is the ceiling" holds only without GC.** With cleaning amplification 1.4-2x and read competition, a 12-HDD RAID6 nets 0.7-0.9 GB/s, under one 10GbE; only 24 drives make the link bind. Extra link bandwidth therefore buys burst absorption bounded by NVLOG size, not throughput. Instead: a sizing table per spindle count, with link count chosen for the burst target.

#### Opus-Storage on Opus-Consensus
**1. Replay arithmetic.** "Replay 2.6 M records in 1.5-3 s; if the mirror is in RAM, first append to A's durable journal, 1-2 s at 200 MB/s per 1 GB" is 5 s per GB: 18.75 GB is ~94 s, and even an Optane journal at 2 GB/s is ~10 s. Replay also covers up to two CPs (active + frozen: 44-66 GB at 2x10GbE) and index insertion runs ~1 us/record (~6 s per CP). Instead: the mirror ring must already be on PMem (no re-journal), plus a records-per-CP cap derived from the takeover budget (<=3 M records => <=6 s) exposed as cluster config; that is my adaptive CP sizing.

**2. Shelves change the member device type.** Dual-EMM shelves present each drive twice, so md members become `dm-multipath` maps, PR keys must be registered on every I_T nexus (`mpathpersist`, both paths, both nodes), and full-stripe/FUA/PREFLUSH behaviour must be re-verified through dm-mpath -> sd -> expander. Instead: add md-over-multipath to the hardware spike's acceptance list (zero member reads during a CP, FUA observed at the drive, RESERVATION CONFLICT on the fenced node), and read the MD1400/MD1420 dual-path matrix in the HBA355 user guide; the [MD24 matrix](https://www.dell.com/support/kbdoc/000223325/powervault-md24-driver-and-firmware-matrix) only confirms HBA355e on 14G.

**3. The bitmap is not "1-3 s and done".** After an unclean stop the bitmap-dirty regions (the CP in flight, up to two CPs) resync in the background at ~1 GB/s on the same spindles the survivor's CPs need, and each newly dirtied bit costs a FUA to every member on the write path. Instead: budget 30-60 s of degraded flush after takeover, and set the bitmap chunk >= segment size so one CP touches a handful of bits.

**4. The unmirrored grant maroons data on the dead node.** "M down: keep serving unmirrored; a later failure makes the aggregate unavailable, not lost" is only true if the dead node's NVLOG media survives: the last unmirrored interval exists only in its NVDIMM. Instead: make the NVLOG self-describing (own superblock, `owner_epoch`, CRC) so transplanted NVDIMM-N modules (which keep contents on their own flash) can be replayed on the survivor, document that procedure, and prefer your shared SAS-SSD journal for this mode, with the NVLOG crate gaining a merge-by-seq replay across two media.

**5. Epoch vocabulary and superblock contract.** You put `(aggregate_id, owner_epoch, nvlog_seq, cp_seq)` on every record and require the importer to compare on-disk epoch; my `RecHdr.cp_epoch` is a CP generation and my superblock has no owner epoch. Instead: `owner_epoch` (Raft) and `cp_seq` (storage) as distinct fields everywhere; the superblock-in-a-stripe carries `owner_epoch` plus the last committed `nvlog_seq`, which is also the ring's replay cut point.

#### Opus-Storage on Opus-Security
**1. PPL/journal is the wrong write-hole fix.** PPL is RAID5-only and costs 30-40% ([kernel docs](https://docs.kernel.org/driver-api/md/raid5-ppl.html)); a journal device is a bandwidth SPOF and, on node-local NVMe, makes the array unimportable on the partner. The LFS never overwrites a referenced stripe, so a torn stripe is unreferenced garbage; only the superblock is in-place and it is replicated across a full stripe. Instead: no PPL, no journal, write-intent bitmap only.

**2. Bottleneck table errors.** "GC reads of live blocks hit the 150-IOPS wall": the cleaner reads whole segments sequentially (320 MB in ~0.2 s) and rewrites live extents in the next CP, costing a bandwidth share 1/(1-u), not seeks; client random reads are what hit the wall. "10GbE mirror ~ HDD flush is the sweet spot; 25GbE would starve the disks": net flush is 0.7-0.9 GB/s after GC (0.35-0.5 under full replication), and a faster link never starves disks, it only engages backpressure sooner. Instead: size the link for burst absorption and resync.

**3. USER_RECOVERY cannot hide replay.** Restarting the combined N+D binary restarts the D-Blade, which must replay the NVLOG and rebuild the index (~6 s per CP, 12-30 s for two) before completing any queued ublk request; ESXi's 10 s RecoveryTimeout expires. Instead: separate N-Blade and D-Blade processes (Network's memfd arena), a records-per-CP cap sized for restart as well as takeover, and reads of not-yet-indexed ranges that wait rather than fail.

**4. LUN sector format decides my partial-block path.** ESXi before 9.0 does not natively support 4Kn ([Broadcom KB](https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/453249/high-storage-latency-and-kavg-seen-when.html)) and the support statement covers local drives, so iSCSI LUNs must be 512e (`logical_bs_shift=9`, `physical_bs_shift=12`); sub-4 KiB and misaligned guest writes then cost a disk read at CP time. Instead: fix 512e for iSCSI, consider 4 KiB LBAs for NVMe/TCP namespaces, and export alignment counters so the CP-time RMW rate is visible.

**5. CAW and PR ordering.** Via ublk, COMPARE AND WRITE arrives as a read plus a write; the D-Blade never sees an atomic op, so VMFS ATS atomicity rests entirely on LIO's per-device semaphore on one node (Active/Standby mandatory, agreed; my `Caw` record is dead until a native target). For PR, a PREEMPT must be ordered against in-flight writes from the preempted initiator; only the D-Blade's `seq` gives that, so PR belongs in the NVLOG as a `PrUpdate` record, not in Raft (I withdraw my own Raft placement). Instead: `PrUpdate` records replayed with I/O and served to N-Blades at login.

#### Cross-cutting conflicts (Opus-Storage)
- **One record, four definitions.** Opus-Network (64 B header + trailer, dense), Opus-Storage (4 KiB-aligned `RecHdr`), Opus-Security (`{epoch, seq, obj, offset, len, crc32c, data}`), Opus-Consensus (`(aggregate_id, owner_epoch, nvlog_seq, cp_seq)`). Alignment decides whether the CP is zero-copy; \"epoch\" means ownership in three proposals and CP generation in mine.
- **Fencing on FC.** Opus-Security's \"the mirror is the fence\" and Opus-Consensus's \"100% in-line rejection\" cannot hold on Opus-Network's BlockRing (`can_reject=false`): FC fencing is ACL removal plus replay-time rejection. My own tcm_qla2xxx mirror-LUN idea is out-of-tree per NETWORK-1; I withdraw it.
- **What FC is for.** Opus-Network's in-box FC is NVMe/FC on Emulex (mirror only); Opus-Security's client FC-SCSI needs QLogic plus out-of-tree `tcm_qla2xxx`; one card cannot do both; Opus-Consensus demotes FC. \"FC primary goal\" cannot be in-box for clients and for the mirror at once.
- **Write hole.** Opus-Security (PPL/journal) vs Opus-Storage and Opus-Consensus (never-overwrite, no journal, importable array).
- **NVLOG/ring size.** Network 25 GB/direction, Security 60 GB/node, Consensus 75 GB/node, Storage 90-130 GB/node at 2x10GbE with back-to-back CPs; different ingest assumptions, and only mine counts CP duration; 192 GB NVDIMM-N may not fit.
- **Which resource is the ceiling.** Network: the link; Security: balanced; Storage and Consensus: the disks (0.7-0.9 GB/s; 0.35-0.5 under full replication).
- **Process topology.** Security: N+D in-process; Network: two daemons sharing a memfd arena; Storage: one D-Blade per aggregate (two per 24-bay node). USER_RECOVERY, replay-on-restart and NUMA pinning depend on it.
- **SCSI PR placement.** Storage (Raft) vs Security (D-Blade log) vs Consensus (either); ordering against I/O resolves it toward the log.
- **Replay budget.** Consensus 1.5-3 s; Storage ~6 s per CP for two CPs; Security \"no path drop on restart\". Needs a records-per-CP cap nobody currently owns.
- **Baseline.** Rocky 10/10.1 (Network, Security) vs either (Storage, Consensus); the ublk front door forces 10.

#### Questions for the lead architect (Opus-Storage)
1. **Ingest targets** (sustained and burst GB/s) and **spindles per node or shelf**: sizes NVLOG, ring, link count and the CP cap, and decides whether 2x10GbE fits in 192 GB of NVDIMM-N.
2. **Shared SAS shelves** (dm-multipath members, SAS-only) or **node-local disks** (shadow replica: half capacity, 0.35-0.5 GB/s per node)?
3. **FC for clients, for the mirror, or both**; are out-of-tree kernel modules acceptable; Emulex or QLogic?
4. **NVLOG medium** (NVDIMM-N, Optane PMem, Optane SSD, NAND PLP) and must **RPO=0 survive dual power loss**?
5. **Takeover and daemon-restart budgets in seconds**: fixes the records-per-CP cap and the adaptive-CP policy.
6. **Who owns the single record-format document** (alignment, fields, `owner_epoch` vs `cp_seq`), and when is it frozen?
7. **Process topology**: in-process, two daemons with a shared arena, or per-aggregate D-Blades?
8. **LUN LBA format** (512e vs 4Kn) and the **initiator population** (ESXi, Windows, Linux, NVMe/TCP)?
9. **Rocky 10.1 as the baseline**, or must Rocky 9 be supported?
10. **Is a witness host allowed**, and is the iDRAC network physically separate from the data and cluster networks?

### By Opus-Consensus

#### Opus-Consensus on Opus-Network
1. **`Unmirrored` only after the partner is fenced** (the Mirrored/Stalled/Unmirrored state machine) is the wrong precondition. "Mirror link down, Ethernet up" leaves two healthy nodes with quorum: the partner's lease never expires, so the owner either stalls forever or powers off a healthy node, and since both nodes own aggregates each would try to fence the other. ONTAP's behaviour for exactly this case is "takeover disabled, unsynchronized log" with both nodes still serving (NetApp KB confirms the takeover-disabled half). Instead: the precondition for unmirrored acks is a quorum-committed `GrantUnmirrored{aggr, epoch}` that disables takeover of that aggregate; fencing is a takeover precondition, never an unmirrored one. `T_stall` becomes one Raft commit (tens of ms), not 5 s.

2. **BlockRing fences the ring, not the ack.** With `can_reject=false`, a stale owner receives NVMe completions from a kernel that knows no epochs and keeps acking clients until the ACL change lands; records placed after the survivor's takeover scan are acked and never replayed. I also believe removal from `allowed_hosts` is checked at connect time and does not tear down a live controller (unlinking the subsystem from the port, which calls `nvmet_port_del_ctrls`, does; verify). Instead: treat BlockRing's only fence as the sender's lease plus hardware watchdog; the survivor's scan waits for `t_last_heard + L(1+rho) + pause margin` and a confirmed controller teardown; replace the success criterion "ACL removal within 1 s" with "zero acked-but-unreplayed writes under a linearizability checker".

3. **Slot reuse makes "retries are always safe" false (ABA).** The owner frees ring space at its own CP boundary with no partner round trip, and failed links are `fail_all`ed and redelivered. A delayed duplicate (an FC exchange retried by HBA firmware, a segment from a half-dead TCP link) can land on a slot already reallocated to a newer, acked record; its CRC verifies, so replay resurrects the old record and loses the new one. Instead: a slot is reusable only after every link that could carry a write to it has been reset and its command lifetime expired (NVMe io timeout 30 s, FC R_A_TOV 10 s), i.e. a quarantined generation per ring region, or a per-slot generation in the record that replay checks.

4. **Two failure detectors.** 20 ms heartbeat records, suspect at 3 misses, dead at 1 s, beside the control plane's 100 ms / 2 s / L=3 s. Acceptable only if the data path's "dead" means "stop and redeliver" and never triggers takeover; export link state as evidence, not verdict. Also unstated: lpfc ports are target *or* initiator, so a bidirectional BlockRing with dual paths is four FC ports per node, and a write-only ring gives Raft no request/response channel (heartbeats become polled slots: SBD, not RPC), which collides with my "Raft rides the mirror Transport".

5. **Epoch semantics are conflated.** `epoch` is "fencing token / volume generation" and the RPC carries a per-volume `gen`. Takeover is per aggregate; a volume generation changes only on volume moves. Replay must reject records whose epoch is below the epoch stored in the aggregate's last CP superblock, not below "current", or a takeover/giveback cycle (e, e+1, e+2) discards unflushed e+1 records. Instead: `(aggregate_id, owner_epoch)` from the RSM in every record; defer volume gen.

#### Opus-Consensus on Opus-Storage
1. **`RecHdr` has no fence token.** `cp_epoch: u32` is a CP counter; there is no owner epoch, so the partner's mirror ring cannot reject a stale owner's appends, and `retire(upto)` is unfenced: a lease-expired owner whose final CP squeaked through can retire records the survivor is replaying. Instead: add `aggr` and `owner_epoch`; `retire` carries the epoch and is refused once `BeginTakeover` is applied at the receiver.

2. **15-30 s of takeover replay breaks every initiator budget** ("2 CPs = 44-66 GB, ~6 s per CP of index rebuild"). Added to detection, fence and assemble it is 25-45 s, past Windows MPIO's 20 s and the 30 s class SCSI command timeouts, and "back-to-back CPs" at line rate make it worse. Instead: the partner indexes its mirror ring continuously on a dedicated core (hundreds of ns per record, ~0.4 GB of index per CP) so replay at takeover is near zero; cap the NVLOG by record count; admit I/O at the new epoch while residual replay finishes, with reads consulting the ring index.

3. **Degraded-mode ACK is framed as a local policy; it is a consensus decision.** "ACK on local persistence or stop" omits the half that matters: the node going unmirrored must hold a quorum-committed grant that disables takeover of that aggregate, otherwise "mirror down, then that node dies" loses acked writes or the partner takes over on a stale ring. Local PMem is necessary, not sufficient.

4. **The shadow replica has no fence and no resync.** With node-local disks there is no PR; both nodes hold full copies of both aggregates, so a partition without a witness lets each promote the other's shadow and the copies diverge: the only split-brain in the whole design that corrupts data instead of stalling. Giveback (ship segments newer than `last_cp_seen`, mirror to both during catch-up) is unmentioned. Instead: shadow mode requires the witness, lease-gated watchdog and iDRAC confirmation before promotion, segment shipping as a Phase 2 deliverable, and a plain statement that per-node ingest halves to 0.35-0.45 GB/s.

5. **"One process per aggregate" collides with the rest.** Opus-Security puts N and D in one binary; my `otap-ctl` publishes one epoch page and pets one watchdog on progress counters. Two aggregates per node means two D-Blade processes, two rings, two leases, and a restart of one must not stop the pet for the node. Settle the process topology before the lease and progress-counter interfaces are written.

6. **4 KiB-aligned records halve the mirror's 4 KiB record rate** if the header occupies its own block (8 KiB per 4 KiB write); Opus-Network's 64 B header is the right shape, and it is the same bytes on the wire and in the NVLOG.

#### Opus-Consensus on Opus-Security
1. **Stale-node behaviour is unspecified and "hold I/O" is the wrong default.** On `StaleEpoch` the N-Blade "holds (not fails) IO for a bounded window". A lease-expired owner must fail closed at once: flip its TPG/ANA group to Standby/Inaccessible, return NOT READY (ASC 04h/0Bh) and raise the ALUA-state-changed UA so initiators re-issue RTPG and move to the new AO path. Holding I/O behind LIO/ublk makes initiators wait out a 30 s command timeout before failing over, which alone blows the 10 s target. Hold only on the healthy side during an ownership refresh.

2. **"The mirror is the fence" is half a fence.** It stops acks only when the partner is reachable and rejects inline; under partition the stale owner is stopped by its lease and watchdog, and under Opus-Network's BlockRing the partner never rejects anything. "The witness is only needed to decide who may run unmirrored" is backwards: the witness is needed for every ownership change under partition, and going unmirrored needs a committed grant, not a fencing vote.

3. **FC "for free" via `tcm_qla2xxx` is not free on Rocky.** Opus-Network verified `CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX` is unset on RHEL 9 and 10, so "five fabrics for one backstore" is three in-box (iSCSI, NVMe/TCP, NVMe/RDMA) plus NVMe/FC on Emulex only; FC-SCSI as a client fabric means an out-of-tree module or our own kernel, and SECURITY-4's QLogic purchase collides with Opus-Network's LPe32002. Also "both HBA330s cabled" to an MD1420 is impossible: the HBA330 has no external ports; it is HBA355e or the 12Gb SAS HBA.

4. **Restart-without-path-drop vs lease and watchdog.** `UBLK_F_USER_RECOVERY` keeps `/dev/ublkbN`, but with D in the same process a restart loses the DRAM dirty index and Opus-Storage rebuilds it by replaying up to two CPs (15-30 s): a path stall by another name. My watchdog is petted only while progress counters advance, so a restarting daemon must publish a bounded "recovering" state or the node resets itself mid-recovery. Instead: the D-Blade as its own process whose ring state survives an `nbladed` restart; recovery grace below `T_wd`.

5. **PR state in the D-Blade log is right; its cadence and identity are not budgeted.** WSFC defends reservations by re-registering every ~3 s per disk (I believe); through the NVLOG that is cheap, through Raft it is an fsync per disk every 3 s.
