Budget for iDRAC9 HTTPS latency (TLS session setup typically 1-3 s on iDRAC9, worse on a shared-LOM NIC) and for a slow or hung iDRAC, so the 8 s watchdog stays the hard bound and Redfish is only an early-exit. Explicitly disable IPMI over LAN in provisioning (do not rely on the factory default) and pin 14G firmware at 7.00.00.172 or later.

### [CONSENSUS-7] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: Default initiator path-failover windows are: ESXi iSCSI RecoveryTimeout 10 s, Windows MPIO PDORemovePeriod 20 s, Linux open-iscsi node.session.timeo.replacement_timeout 120 s, and Linux NVMe ctrl_loss_tmo 600 s with a 10 s reconnect delay, so a takeover that completes in 15 s or less does not surface I/O errors to guests using ALUA/ANA multipath.
- Correction: The conclusion (a ≤15 s takeover is survivable) is roughly right, but three of the four numbers are the wrong knob. (1) ESXi: RecoveryTimeout 10 s is one of three timers; the stock software-iSCSI stack is NoopOutInterval 15 + NoopOutTimeout 10 + RecoveryTimeout 10 ≈ 35 s of host-side I/O pause. Guests still see no errors because ESXi holds I/O and VMware Tools sets guest disk timeouts to 60 s (Windows) / 180 s (Linux). (2) Windows: PDORemovePeriod 20 s is how long a PDO survives after ALL paths are gone, not the failover window; with MPIO the iSCSI initiator's LinkDownTime (15 s) governs when held requests are failed to MPIO, and disk TimeOutValue is 60 s. (3) Linux open-iscsi: iscsid.conf default is 120 s, but multipathd overrides the session recovery_tmo with multipath's fast_io_fail_tmo (default 5 s); the effective path is NOP-out 5+5 s detect + 5 s ≈ 15 s before I/O is failed to dm-multipath. Crucially, dm-multipath's default no_path_retry is "fail" for an unknown vendor, so if the partner's ALUA ports are still STANDBY when the dead path fails, I/O errors reach the application immediately. (4) NVMe: ctrl_loss_tmo 600 s / reconnect_delay 10 s govern controller deletion, not failover; REQ_NVME_MPATH requests to a non-live controller are failed with host-path-error and re-routed at once (nvme_fail_nonready_command, present in both 5.14 and 6.12), and nvme-mpath queues when no path is available. KATO (~5 s + grace) is the detection timer.
- Evidence: Dell KB 000418707 (ESXi defaults sum to 35 s); VMware KB 1009465/1014 (guest timeouts 180/60 s); Red Hat RHEL 7 "modifying link loss behavior" (replacement_timeout 120 s default, NOP 5/5 s, fast_io_fail_tmo takes precedence for DM-multipath, recommend 15–20 s); NetApp HCI Windows iSCSI KB (PDORemovePeriod 20, LinkDownTime 15, MaxRequestHoldTime 60); torvalds/linux v6.12 fabrics.h (NVMF_DEF_RECONNECT_DELAY 10, CTRL_LOSS_TMO 600, FAIL_FAST_TMO -1) and core.c nvme_fail_nonready_command (REQ_NVME_MPATH → nvme_host_path_error).
- Design impact: The 15 s budget is not loose on Linux iSCSI: the surviving node's ALUA ports must report TRANSITIONING (not STANDBY) during takeover and become active-optimized before ~15 s, or dm-multipath's default no_path_retry=fail will surface EIO. OpenTAP should ship a multipath.conf/hwtable entry (no_path_retry, alua) and Windows/ESXi tuning guidance, and should re-derive detection/lease timers from LinkDownTime 15 s and open-iscsi's effective 15 s rather than from the quoted 10/20/120/600 s figures.

### [CONSENSUS-8] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: mdadm provides no protection against two hosts assembling the same array from shared disks except through md-cluster (which requires DLM/Corosync), and the Dell HBA355e is a supported external 12G SAS HBA for the R740xd that can attach an MD1400/MD1420 in redundant-path (dual-EMM) mode.
- Correction: (1) mdadm part is true but understated: md-cluster is not even an option on Rocky — CentOS Stream 9 and 10 kernel configs both carry "# CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER is not set", and Red Hat's HA policy explicitly refuses support for MD-RAID built on cluster-shared block devices. Upstream md-cluster is RAID1 (RAID10 preview) only and needs Corosync+DLM (SUSE HA). Non-cluster mdadm offers only homehost/AUTO/DEVICE filters, no fencing. (2) HBA355e is Dell-supported on R740xd (R740xd tech specs list "PERC H840, 12Gbps SAS HBA, HBA355e" as external controllers; HBA355 UG lists R740xd), and supports MD1400/MD1420 in dual-path mode. BUT "redundant path (dual-EMM)" in Dell's definition is ONE host with both EMMs cabled to the same controller; Dell states MD1400/MD1420 "can only be attached to a single server" and drives "cannot be accessed in split mode between two servers"; its enclosure rules give "Maximum number of servers: 1". A two-host shared JBOD on MD14xx is physically cable-able (each host to one EMM, no path redundancy per host) but is an unsupported configuration.
- Evidence: CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER unset in RHEL kernels: gitlab.com/redhat/centos-stream/src/kernel/centos-stream-9 and -10 redhat/configs/common/generic/CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER (fetched). Red Hat HA storage policy: https://access.redhat.com/articles/2912911 ("will not provide support ... MD-raid devices consisting of block devices shared throughout the cluster"). SUSE Cluster MD (Corosync+DLM, RAID1/RAID10 preview): https://documentation.suse.com/sle-ha/html/SLE-HA-all/cha-ha-cluster-md.html. NeilBrown on no cross-host assembly guard: https://lore.kernel.org/all/49E9DC9D.5050708@tmr.com/T/. HBA355e+R740xd: https://www.dell.com/support/manuals/en-me/hba-355/hba355_ug/poweredge-systems-supported-by-dell-hba355e-adapter and https://onlineimages.techmikeny.com/CTOPDF/per740xd-technical-specs.pdf. Dell single-server statement: https://www.dell.com/community/PowerVault/Reset-password-MD1400-to-factory-default/td-p/8116173; MD1400/1420 deployment guide defines "Redundant configuration" as both controller ports to one card; Proxmox thread citing Dell "Maximum number of servers 1": https://forum.proxmox.com/threads/dell-md1420-sas-hba-expansion-multi-path.126213/
- Design impact: OpenTAP's daemon must be the sole assembly guard (SCSI-3 PR before assemble, AUTO -all / DEVICE whitelist, udev rules masked) since md-cluster cannot be a fallback on Rocky 9/10 without a custom kernel. The shared MD1400/MD1420 JBOD path is "works but unsupported by Dell": plan for each host on one EMM (no per-host dual path) or move to a Dell-supported multi-host JBOD (e.g., ME484/third-party dual-host JBOD) if vendor support matters.

### [SECURITY-1] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: RHEL/Rocky 9 ships with kernel.io_uring_disabled=2 by default, so io_uring_setup() fails with EPERM for all processes until the sysctl is changed.
- Correction: True for RHEL/Rocky 9.3 and later (kernel >= 5.14.0-362.8.1.el9_3): io_uring is a Technology Preview, CONFIG_IO_URING=y, and kernel.io_uring_disabled defaults to 2, so io_uring_setup() returns -EPERM for every process (existing rings keep working) until the sysctl is set to 0 or 1. Caveats the claim gets wrong or omits: (a) RHEL 9.0-9.2 kernels (5.14.0-70..284) have no such sysctl and no usable io_uring at all (ENOSYS, not EPERM); (b) RHEL/Rocky 10.0 and 10.1 ship exactly the same policy - io_uring is still Tech Preview and disabled by default via the same sysctl (Red Hat MR "RHEL-only: mark io_uring tech preview and disable by default", Nov 2024) - so Rocky 10.1 does NOT remove this obstacle; (c) on both 9 and 10 io_uring remains Tech Preview, i.e. unsupported by Red Hat; (d) 9.3 notes add that io_uring command pass-through (IORING_OP_URING_CMD, which libublk depends on) needs a custom SELinux policy module, and container seccomp profiles return ENOSYS regardless of the sysctl.
- Evidence: RHEL 9.3 Technology Previews (io_uring entry, BZ 2068237): "By default, this feature is disabled"; value 2 "io_uring_setup always fails with -EPERM ... This is the default setting" - https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/9/html/9.3_release_notes/technology-previews (repeated in 9.6 notes). RHEL 10.1 Technology Preview features: io_uring "available as a Technology Preview. By default, this feature is disabled in RHEL 10", enable via kernel/io_uring_disabled - https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/10.1_release_notes/technology-preview-features ; RHEL-65347 and https://gitlab.com/redhat/centos-stream/src/kernel/centos-stream-10/-/merge_requests/34 confirm the RHEL 10 default-off policy. Rocky 9.3 forum: io_uring needs >= 5.14.0-362.8.1.el9_3, CONFIG_IO_URING=y - https://forums.rockylinux.org/t/io-uring-support-rocky-9-3/12384 . Upstream sysctl semantics (0/1/2, EPERM at 2, existing rings unaffected) - https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/2308.2/00994.html . Jeff Moyer's original RHEL-only "disable io_uring" patch - https://lists.pagure.io/archives/list/kernel@lists.fedoraproject.org/message/VDYV6YPNDF77IWO5WOH7OXAMM5F65EXU/ .
- Design impact: The sysctl is a one-line install-time setting (kernel.io_uring_disabled=0 in /etc/sysctl.d plus an SELinux module for URING_CMD), not a reason to pick Rocky 10.1 over 9 - Rocky 10.x has the identical default. The real Rocky-10 argument must rest on 6.12 kernel features (ublk maturity, multishot recv, SENDZC, zero-copy) that the 5.14-based 9 kernel lacks or only partially backports, and on the fact that io_uring is unsupported Tech Preview on both, which matters for a product's support story.

### [SECURITY-2] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: ublk_drv is not shipped in RHEL 9's 5.14-based kernel and is first enabled (as Technology Preview) in RHEL/Rocky 10.1; Rocky 10.0 may lack the module.
- Correction: ublk_drv is absent from the RHEL/Rocky 9 5.14-based kernel (no drivers/block/ublk_drv.c in the CentOS Stream 9 tree; no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK in the el9 config; RHEL 9.x release notes never list it). But it is NOT first enabled in 10.1: Red Hat's RHEL 10.0 release notes already list "ublk_drv driver is available as a Technology Preview" (Jira RHELDOCS-19891, section 7.4), and the 10.0 kernel (kernel-6.12.0-55.x.el10_0, AlmaLinux a10.0 branch) builds CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m. Rocky 10.0 therefore ships the module. 10.1 (6.12.0-130+) only adds a large ublk backport (zero-copy, UBLK_F_AUTO_BUF_REG, per-IO daemons, UBLK_F_QUIESCE, CVE fixes; RHEL-106845) on top of the already-enabled 10.0 module, and the TP entry is simply carried forward in the 10.1 notes.
- Evidence: RHEL 10.0 release notes, Technology Preview chapter, sec. 7.4 lists ublk_drv (RHELDOCS-19891): https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/10.0_release_notes/technology-preview-features . Same entry in 10.1: https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/10.1_release_notes/technology-preview-features . AlmaLinux a10.0 branch (kernel.spec: 6.12.0-55.43.1.el10_0) kernel-x86_64-rhel.config: CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m: https://git.almalinux.org/rpms/kernel/raw/branch/a10.0/kernel-x86_64-rhel.config . CentOS Stream 10 config file: https://gitlab.com/redhat/centos-stream/src/kernel/centos-stream-10/-/raw/main/redhat/configs/common/generic/CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK (=m); same path in centos-stream-9 and drivers/block/ublk_drv.c in centos-stream-9 return 404. RHEL 9.6 TP chapter has no ublk. kernel-6.12.0-130.el10 changelog shows ~100 ublk backport entries (RHEL-106845) with CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m already present as context: https://git.almalinux.org/rpms/kernel/commit/f170c0ad568be886aee8d914037d6888c1ed81dc . Caveat: Rocky 10.0 GA ISO kernel config not fetched directly; inferred from the el10_0 source branch (Rocky rebuilds RHEL sources 1:1). Web search budget was exhausted mid-task; findings rest on direct fetches.
- Design impact: Rocky 10.0 and later all carry ublk_drv (module, Technology Preview), so Phase 1 can target ublk on any Rocky 10 release without a TCMU fallback; the real constraint is that Rocky 9 is out entirely. Prefer 10.1+ anyway: its 6.12.0-130+ kernel carries the zero-copy/auto-buffer-registration and CVE-fixed ublk code the Rust daemon will want, and TP status means no Red Hat support commitment on the ABI.

### [SECURITY-3] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: TCMU's pgr_support and alua_support attributes are writable since kernel 5.8 so a userspace handler can implement PR and ALUA itself, whereas ublk exposes no block pr_ops, so LIO iblock over ublk cannot pass PR commands to the daemon.
- Correction: Kernel facts hold: pgr_support/alua_support became writable in v5.8 (CONFIGFS_ATTR_RO in v5.7, CONFIGFS_ATTR + *_store in v5.8; present in CentOS Stream 9's 5.14 tree with tcmu setting transport_flags_changeable, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m) and ublk_drv has no pr_ops (ub_fops = owner/open/free_disk/report_zones in mainline and CS10). But "a userspace handler can implement PR itself" is overstated: TCMU's command ring carries only cmd_id/CDB/iovecs, no I_T nexus/initiator identity; the very commit says userspace PR/ALUA "will work only if session/nexus information is sent to userspace", deferred to a Mike Christie series that never landed. tcmu-runner implements RTPG/STPG but no PERSISTENT RESERVE IN/OUT. So TCMU gives you ALUA plus raw PR CDBs without knowing who sent them, which is not spec-compliant multi-initiator PR. Also: LIO iblock-to-bdev pr_ops passthrough only exists since v6.5 (absent v6.4 and in CS9 5.14 iblock), is limited to one target port and cannot pass initiators, and CS9 has no ublk_drv at all. On Rocky 9 the ublk route is impossible for several reasons; on Rocky 10 it is blocked only by ublk lacking pr_ops.
- Evidence: github.com/torvalds/linux/commit/356ba2a8bc8d (commit text incl. nexus caveat); raw v5.7 vs v5.8 drivers/target/target_core_configfs.c; include/uapi/linux/target_core_user.h (tcmu_cmd_entry has no nexus field); open-iscsi/tcmu-runner tcmur_cmd_handler.c and alua.c (RTPG/STPG only, "tcmu does not pass up the target port"); raw drivers/block/ublk_drv.c master and gitlab.com/redhat/centos-stream/.../centos-stream-10 (no pr_ops); v6.4 vs v6.5 target_core_iblock.c (pr_ops passthrough appears in 6.5, with one-port/no-initiator comments); CS9 drivers/block/Makefile has no ublk entry, CS9 iblock has no pr_ops. Web search budget ran out before confirming the CS9 CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK file path; absence of the driver source is the evidence.
- Design impact: Neither TCMU nor ublk gives OpenTAP initiator-aware cluster PR; only in-kernel LIO PR emulation (emulate_pr=1, single node, APTPL file) or the planned native userspace iSCSI/NVMe-oF target (which owns the nexus) can. Treat PR state replication as a control-plane problem of the native front end, not a reason to pick TCMU; Active/Standby ALUA in Phase 1 is justified by unreplicated PR/ALUA state, not by the TCMU-vs-ublk difference, and the ublk path is unavailable on Rocky 9 regardless.

### [SECURITY-4] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: Marvell qla2xxx supports FC SCSI target mode via tcm_qla2xxx (dual initiator+target mode via qlini_mode=dual with per-port sysfs control) but has no NVMe/FC target mode and none planned, while Emulex lpfc supports NVMe/FC target but has no in-tree SCSI target.
- Evidence: qla2xxx: tcm_qla2xxx is the in-tree LIO fabric for QLogic FC SCSI target; qlini_mode accepts enabled/disabled/dual/exclusive, and commit 0645cb83 "scsi: qla2xxx: Add mode control for each physical port" (Quinn Tran, 2018, merged ~4.20) adds /sys/class/scsi_host/hostX/qlini_mode per-port switching, with the caveat that the module must be loaded with qlini_mode=dual so resources are allocated (https://github.com/torvalds/linux/commit/0645cb8350cdb60bfbf91caa722984b81c215add). Both Rocky 9 (5.14) and Rocky 10 (6.12) kernels carry this. NVMe/FC target on qla2xxx: a 2018 Cavium series adding qla_nvmet.c was never merged; in April 2025 Hannes Reinecke (SUSE) wrote "Driver support for nvmet on qla2xxx is missing, and Marvell has no intention of bringing it back... the status is 'not supported'", and Daniel Wagner in the same thread got NVMe/FC target working only with an Emulex HBA (https://lists.infradead.org/pipermail/linux-nvme/2025-April/055140.html). SUSE docs' "FC-NVMe enabled by default in qla2xxx" refers to host side only. lpfc: in-tree lpfc_nvmet.c provides the nvmet-fc target, enabled per WWPN via lpfc_enable_nvmet; SUSE docs state "A Fibre Channel port can either be configured as target or as initiator" (https://documentation.suse.com/en-us/sles/15-SP6/html/SLES-all/cha-nvmeof.html). No tcm_lpfc was ever merged; Emulex SCSI target existed only out of tree via SCST (lpfc_scst) and abandoned 2010/2015 LIO WIP patches (https://lkml.iu.edu/1007.2/01452.html, https://patchwork.kernel.org/comment/13255711). Search budget was exhausted before confirming the exact release tag of 0645cb83; it is certainly older than 5.14.
- Design impact: Claim stands: FC SCSI target = QLogic + tcm_qla2xxx, FC-NVMe target = Emulex only. Additional caveat that strengthens the "FC mirror is fragile" argument: lpfc NVMe target ports cannot also be initiators, so an NVMe/FC mirror needs two Emulex ports per node (one target, one initiator), whereas qla2xxx dual mode can do both on one port but only for SCSI; whichever path is chosen, the kernel target stack (LIO or nvmet) is in the data path, not OpenTAP's userspace front end.

### [SECURITY-5] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: No usable pure-Rust iSCSI target exists, and the only Rust NVMe/TCP+RDMA target (ioutgt) is early-stage (about 4 GitHub stars, requires Linux 6.11+, lacks in-band auth).
- Correction: The ioutgt details are accurate: 4 stars / 2 forks, "Early development", requires Linux >= 6.11 (io_uring DEFER_TASKRUN + multishot; developed on 7.1), Rust >= 1.88, NVMe/TCP + NVMe/RDMA only, in-band authentication is a roadmap item (no TLS/DH-HMAC-CHAP mentioned), not published to crates.io (publish = false). But "no pure-Rust iSCSI target exists" is wrong: `iscsi-target` 1.0.0 (lawless-m/iscsi-crate) is on crates.io since 2026-06-12, pure Rust, no kernel dependency, one-way and mutual CHAP, SendTargets discovery, immediate data + multi-PDU Data-Out, CRC32C digests, open-iscsi interop claimed, RFC 3720 conformance corpus. It is hobby-scale (3 stars, 153 downloads, synchronous ScsiBlockDevice trait, no ERL>0, no MC/S, no Windows/VMware interop) so "no production-usable" would be the defensible wording. Also "only Rust NVMe target" is slightly off: glennswest/stormblock (1 star) claims NVMe-oF/TCP + iSCSI targets. Kernel caveat: Rocky 9 (5.14) cannot run ioutgt; Rocky 10 (6.12) satisfies >= 6.11.
- Evidence: github.com/io-target/ioutgt and raw README (stars 4, "Linux >= 6.11", roadmap "In-band authentication", "not published to crates.io", xfstests quick passes vs kernel host, self-reported +11%/+104% vs nvmet on RDMA). crates.io/api/v1/crates/iscsi-target (v1.0.0, created 2026-06-12, 153 downloads). docs.rs/iscsi-target and github.com/lawless-m/iscsi-crate (3 stars, CHAP, open-iscsi interop, sync trait). GitHub repo search "iscsi target language:Rust" and "nvme target language:Rust" also surfaced sephiron99/iscsi-target-server (Windows-only, ERL0, WIP, 0 stars), milanalfandiismail/Simple-Iscsi (Windows, 0 stars), LGDan/iscsi-s3 (alpha), glennswest/stormblock (1 star). Web search budget ran out mid-task; findings above come from direct fetches.
- Design impact: The conclusion stands: Phase 1 on kernel LIO/nvmet is still the right call, and no Rust crate is a drop-in production target. But the iscsi-target crate and ioutgt are worth mining as reference code (PDU parsing, login state machine, CHAP; io_uring queue-per-thread design) rather than assuming a from-scratch effort; and any ioutgt-style userspace path implies Rocky 10 (6.12), not Rocky 9 (5.14).

### [SECURITY-6] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: Windows has no generally available NVMe/TCP initiator as of mid-2026 (Insider preview only, without multipathing), so Windows clients require iSCSI.
- Correction: Windows has no *inbox/Microsoft-supported* NVMe-oF initiator that is generally available. Microsoft's native initiator (NVMe/TCP and NVMe/RDMA, configured via nvmeofutil.exe) shipped only as a Preview in Windows Server Insider builds on 13 Mar 2026, explicitly with "No multipathing", CLI-only config and "limited recovery behavior in some network failure scenarios"; no GA announcement had appeared on the Windows Server blogs as of the Sep 2026 vNext build 29667 post. However, third-party NVMe/TCP initiators for Windows have been generally available for years: StarWind's NVMe-oF Initiator (TCP and RoCE) was commercialized in 2021 and is Windows-certified for Server 2019/Windows 10 (a free version exists). So "Windows clients require iSCSI" is true only for customers who require an inbox, Microsoft-supported, MPIO-capable initiator, which in practice is most enterprise Windows deployments.
- Evidence: Microsoft, "Introducing the Windows NVMe-oF Initiator Preview in Windows Server Insiders Builds" (13 Mar 2026): https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/windowsservernewsandbestpractices/introducing-the-windows-nvme-of-initiator-preview-in-windows-server-insiders-bui/4501344 (body read via the board RSS feed; limitations quoted above). Blog index through Aug 2026 and the Sep 2026 vNext build 29667 post contain no GA notice. Windows Server 2025 What's New (learn.microsoft.com/.../whats-new-windows-server-2025) lists only local NVMe perf work, no fabrics. StarWind initiator: StorageReview, "Hands On with StarWind NVMe-oF Initiator for Windows" (Oct 2021): https://www.storagereview.com/review/hands-on-with-starwind-nvme-of-initiator-for-windows ; Wikipedia NVM Express lists StarWind initiator with RoCE and TCP transports. Caveat: WebSearch budget was exhausted and StarWind's own site returned 403, so current StarWind version/MPIO details are unverified; the Microsoft preview's build number is not stated in the post.
- Design impact: The conclusion stands for practical purposes: iSCSI (hence LIO or OpenTAP's own iSCSI target) cannot be dropped, because Windows hosts needing a supported, multipath-capable path have only iSCSI today. But the rationale should be restated as "no inbox/MPIO-capable Windows initiator", and the roadmap should expect a Microsoft NVMe/TCP and NVMe/RDMA initiator to reach GA within the product's life, so the NVMe-oF front end should be designed to serve Windows too (ANA multipath, Windows-friendly discovery) rather than treated as ESXi/Linux-only.

### [SECURITY-7] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: LIO implements COMPARE AND WRITE in target_core_sbc as a read-then-write serialized by a per-device semaphore, and keeps persistent reservations per node, so neither is atomic or consistent across two nodes exporting the same LUN.
- Correction: For the emulated backstores OpenTAP would use (iblock/fileio/rd over mdadm or RAM), the claim is exactly right on both Rocky 9 (5.14) and Rocky 10 (6.12): sbc_compare_and_write() does down_interruptible(&dev->caw_sem), a backend READ, then compare_and_write_callback() issues the WRITE and compare_and_write_post() up()s the per-se_device binary semaphore (sema_init(&dev->caw_sem, 1) in target_alloc_device). PR state lives in dev->t10_pr in memory, with APTPL only persisted to a local file $db_root/pr/aptpl_<serial>. Two caveats the claim omits: (a) since 6.4 (so in Rocky 10's 6.12, not Rocky 9's 5.14) iblock registers execute_pr_in/out and, when pgr_support=0 sets TRANSPORT_FLAG_PASSTHROUGH_PGR, forwards PR to the backing block device's pr_ops, which would make PRs cluster-consistent on a shared SCSI/NVMe/dm-multipath backend; md_fops has no .pr_ops, so this does not help an mdadm backend. (b) pscsi defaults to PASSTHROUGH|PASSTHROUGH_PGR and hands both PR and CAW (0x89) to the physical LUN unchanged. Neither applies to OpenTAP's non-shared backend, so the conclusion stands for this design, but "LIO forces Active/Standby" is not a universal property of LIO.
- Evidence: Read kernel sources directly (WebSearch budget was exhausted; WebFetch only): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/target/target_core_sbc.c and the v5.14 copy (caw_sem down/read/write/up sequence identical); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/target/target_core_device.c (sema_init(&dev->caw_sem,1); passthrough_parse_cdb emulates PR unless PASSTHROUGH_PGR, passes CAW through); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/target/target_core_pr.c (in-memory t10_pr, local APTPL file); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/target/target_core_iblock.c (iblock_execute_pr_in/out via bdev pr_ops; absent in v5.14); target_core_configfs.c (pgr_support toggles the flag); target_core_pscsi.c (passthrough flags); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/md/md.c (md_fops lacks pr_ops).
- Design impact: While LIO fronts an mdadm/RAM-cache LUN, Active/Standby ALUA (one AO port group) remains mandatory on both Rocky releases; dual-AO would break VMFS ATS and WSFC PR arbitration. The lasting fix is the planned userspace iSCSI/NVMe-oF target, where OpenTAP owns CAW serialization and PR/registration state and replicates them over the cache-mirror link (and via Raft) before acknowledging, which is what Active/Active would require.

### [SECURITY-8] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Security)
- Claim: A 4K write costs ~3.4 us of serialization on 10GbE and ~30-50 us kernel-TCP round trip between userspace daemons, so a mirrored 4K write via LIO→ublk→D-Blade→partner totals ~90-150 us at QD1 (about 7-11K IOPS per stream), a 1GbE mirror link caps at ~28K 4K writes/s, and an FC-SCSI mirror via tcm_qla2xxx (~60-100 us RTT) is no faster than 10GbE TCP.
- Correction: The arithmetic is sound: 4096 B payload in 3 TCP segments plus ~78 B/frame overhead = ~34.6 kbit, i.e. ~3.5 us at 10 Gb/s and ~28.9K writes/s on 1GbE (29.9K with jumbo frames); 1/(90-150 us) = 6.7-11K IOPS. The caveats: (1) 30-50 us kernel-TCP RTT assumes interrupt coalescing disabled or busy polling; with default adaptive coalescing on typical R740xd NICs (X710/BCM57412/ConnectX) 50-100 us is common, so the budget should be stated as 90-200 us untuned. Rocky 10 (6.12) has io_uring NAPI busy-poll (IORING_REGISTER_NAPI, 6.9+) which can bring RTT to ~20-25 us; Rocky 9's 5.14 base lacks it. (2) The 60-100 us FC-SCSI figure is unsourced; it is plausible only because a userspace-to-userspace mirror over tcm_qla2xxx needs an extra kernel backstore hop (tcmu/ublk) on the target side. Native kernel-to-kernel FC QD1 is likely 40-70 us. FC target mode is also not a Red Hat supported configuration and needs qlini_mode tuning; Rocky inherits that status. (3) QD1 IOPS is not a throughput ceiling: at QD 8-16 a single 10GbE mirror link carries ~250-280K 4K writes/s; the 1GbE cap is bandwidth, not latency.
- Evidence: Arithmetic recomputed (3 x 1448 B MSS segments, 78 B L1/L2/L3/L4 overhead per frame). TCP_RR on 10GbE measured at 37 us with coalescing disabled (https://lists.mpich.org/pipermail/discuss/2014-April/004958.html, via search summary; server unreachable on fetch). ublk data path and overhead notes: https://docs.kernel.org/block/ublk.html (io_uring passthrough; UBLK_F_NEED_GET_DATA adds a round trip; batch mode and zero-copy reduce overhead). RHEL 9 storage docs cover only the FC/FCoE initiator side, no FC target support statement (https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/9/html/managing_storage_devices/configuring-fibre-channel-over-ethernet_managing-storage-devices). Web search budget was exhausted after one query, so the FC RTT number and RHEL FC-target support status could not be independently sourced.
- Design impact: Keep the 1GbE prototype ceiling (~28K 4K writes/s) and the QD1 budget, but plan for 90-200 us untuned and tune coalescing/busy-poll (Rocky 10 preferred). The FC-demotion argument should rest on tcm_qla2xxx being unsupported and needing an extra kernel hop, not on an unverified RTT; the real mirror latency lever is RDMA (RoCE on 10/25GbE, ~5-10 us RTT), which does not require FC at all.

## Round 3: revised positions

### Opus-Network

#### Concessions
- **Record layout (Storage 1).** Dense `64 B + payload + trailer` misaligns every payload after the first for O_DIRECT/`WRITE_FIXED`, so the CP flush would silently memcpy the whole NVLOG. Accepted: 4 KiB-aligned payloads plus a parallel header ring. Storage owns the record; I own only batch and wire framing.
- **fsdax pins (Storage 2).** `FOLL_LONGTERM` is refused on fsdax VMAs, so neither io_uring buffer registration nor an RDMA MR can cover an fsdax arena. The owner's NVLOG moves to devdax; fsdax serves only the kernel-written inbound BlockRing file.
- **Sizing and the ceiling (Storage 3, 5; NETWORK-8).** Space is reusable after the CP superblock FUA, not at CP start: capacity = ingest x (interval + max CP duration). The pair's ceiling is min(front-end link, mirror link, *tuned* md flush net of GC); at 12 drives the array binds and extra link bandwidth buys only burst absorption bounded by NVLOG size.
- **Unmirrored precondition (Consensus 1).** "Only after the partner is fenced" was wrong: mirror down with Ethernet up is two healthy nodes, and each would try to fence the other. The precondition is a quorum-committed `GrantUnmirrored{aggr, epoch}` that disables takeover; fencing is a takeover precondition only; `T_stall` collapses to one Raft commit.
- **BlockRing fencing (Consensus 2, Security 1, NETWORK-3).** `allowed_hosts` removal only calls `nvmet_subsys_disc_changed`; live controllers keep writing; only unlinking the subsystem from the port reaches `nvmet_port_del_ctrls`. And a record epoch cannot separate zombie writes from the legitimate owner's earlier writes under the same epoch, so "reject at replay" was never a fence. The backing file must be pre-written, not fallocated, and mirror writes need FUA for `Durable` to mean crash-readable.
- **ABA (Consensus 3).** "Retries are always safe" is false for unknown-fate writes: a late landing on a reallocated slot verifies and resurrects stale data.
- **Detectors, ports, Raft (Consensus 4).** The data-path detector may only drive redelivery. lpfc ports are target or initiator, so bidirectional dual-path FC is four ports per node, and a write-only ring cannot carry Raft.
- **Epoch (Consensus 5).** `(aggregate_id, owner_epoch)` from the RSM; replay filters against the last CP superblock's epoch, not "current"; volume `gen` deferred.
- **Security 2-5.** Zero copy is not demonstrable through ublk on 6.12 (two kernel copies). The dual-use TCMU handler is withdrawn: SBC/SPC emulation plus ESXi/Windows interop is a product, not 1.5K lines. A stall must flip ALUA/ANA to Transitioning, and stall plus takeover must fit under ~15 s because LIO's ABORT TASK cannot cancel ublk I/O. `req_id` leaves the record.
- **Verdicts.** NETWORK-4: Rocky 10 also ships `io_uring_disabled=2` and calls io_uring Technology Preview. NETWORK-5: my TCP bands were tuning artifacts; 16GFC serialization is 2.34 us at line rate. NETWORK-6: my version numbers were off, and lpfc pt2pt NVMe is a maintained path, so Emulex direct-connect is a comparable bet, not a worse one. NETWORK-7: NVDIMM-N is end-of-life and capped at 192 GB; pmem must be optional.

#### Holds firm
- **The ring shape.** A slot-addressed, idempotent, receiver-optional ring remains the only contract under which in-box FC (`nvmet-fc`, no userspace receiver), NVMe multipath and RDMA write-with-immediate are one thing. Every critique closed a hole in its fencing or reuse rules; none needed a different shape. Storage's `append -> Seq` is this ring's ack where a receiver exists; on BlockRing the NVMe completion is the ack and `nvlog_seq` is sender-assigned. In-line epoch rejection stays on TcpRing/VerbsRing as fail-fast, never as the safety argument.
- **Lazy CRC on BlockRing (Storage 4).** A completed NVMe write on an ADR platform cannot tear, and FC frame CRC plus NVMe/TCP data digests cover the wire; the replay-time CRC catches media or silent corruption, which no transport's pre-ack CRC prevents either. The `consumed` status block I proposed is the continuous consumer Storage asked for; it is now explicit for both disk models and is what Consensus's continuous partner-side indexing runs on.
- **FC is not the latency tier.** NETWORK-5 confirms RDMA < tuned TCP < FC through a kernel target; NETWORK-2 adds that every FC target path is Red Hat-unsupported; NETWORK-6 adds bug churn. Tier 2 should be relabelled an isolation/distance option and 25GbE the performance tier; the owner should hear this now.
- **Emulex `nvmet-fc` over QLogic `tcm_qla2xxx`.** NETWORK-1 confirmed; Marvell has no nvmet-fc plan; `tcm_qla2xxx` is non-kABI and needs rebuild and Secure Boot signing per kernel update.
- **Rocky 10, 10GbE from day one, fixed tuning.** NETWORK-5 strengthens this: a 15-100 us TCP band set by tuning means the spec must pin rx-usecs, C-states and busy-poll, then measure.
- **Unchanged:** timer-free group commit, per-record crc32c, receive-side `clwb+sfence` (NETWORK-7 confirmed), and `Placed`/`Durable` as a logged cluster-wide setting.

#### Revised design changes
1. **One record.** Storage's 4 KiB-aligned `RecHdr` with `(aggregate_id, owner_epoch, nvlog_seq, cp_seq, vol, lba, len, payload_crc, hdr_crc)`, no `req_id`. Ring = header ring of 4 KiB descriptor blocks (one per batch, up to 63 headers) plus a 4 KiB-granular data ring. TcpRing may send headers densely and let the receiver assemble blocks; BlockRing writes blocks as-is. A record is valid when descriptor and payload CRCs both verify; acked implies both writes completed.
2. **Arenas.** Owner NVLOG on devdax (2 MB-aligned, `WRITE_FIXED`- and MR-registrable; verify pins on 6.12); inbound BlockRing on a pre-written fsdax file; `memmap=` emulation provides both on dev boxes.
3. **BlockRing.** FUA on every mirror write by default (descriptor-only if measured and the ordering argument is written down); NVMe/TCP data digest on; native multipath/ANA for dual paths.
4. **Fencing, every transport.** (i) Owner lease checked at *ack* time plus hardware watchdog (Consensus's constants); (ii) survivor-side receiver teardown: TcpRing closes links and rejects stale epochs, BlockRing unlinks the subsystem from the port and waits the measured teardown time plus margin, VerbsRing destroys QPs; (iii) replay everything that verifies, then serve. ACL edits are discovery hygiene, not a fence.
5. **State machine.** Mirrored -> Stalled (ALUA Transitioning / ANA Change at once, no acks) -> Unmirrored only on committed `GrantUnmirrored`, which disables takeover of that aggregate -> Resync. Takeover requires the fence ladder; stall plus takeover capped at 15 s. 20 ms data-path heartbeats drive redelivery only; the control plane's detector decides; Raft and RPC run on Ethernet always.
6. **Reuse.** Unknown-fate slots are quarantined; redelivery goes to fresh slots; replay dedupes by `nvlog_seq`. Quarantine lifts on confirmed teardown or command-lifetime expiry (TCP: close confirmed over the surviving link; NVMe: controller delete plus `io_timeout`; FC: R_A_TOV). Normal retirement follows CP commit with Storage's 50%/90% watermarks as the shared back-pressure contract.
7. **Sizing.** Capacity = ingest x (interval + max CP duration) + quarantine; ingest cap from measured flush rate; pmem optional: DRAM arenas default to `Placed`, `Durable` via NVDIMM-N or a PLP NVMe log; separate power domains per node stated as a product requirement.
8. **FC.** Mirror-only NVMe/FC on LPe32002/LPe35002, two dual-port HBAs per node (two target, two initiator ports), documented as Red Hat-unsupported; direct-connect lab-qualified with a switch-pair fallback. Client FC needs out-of-tree `tcm_qla2xxx` or SCST: owner decision.
9. **Baseline and tuning.** Rocky 10; sysctl.d drop-in (`kernel.io_uring_disabled=1`, daemons in `io_uring_group`); startup EPERM probe; shipped tuning: rx-usecs 0, C-state cap, `SO_BUSY_POLL`, `group_thread_cnt`, `stripe_cache_size`. The N->D SPSC ring works in-process or across daemons, so I defer to Security's single binary plus Consensus's `otap-ctl`.
10. **Dropped.** Dual-use TCMU; the zero-copy-through-ublk criterion; the ACL-removal criterion; `can_reject` as a safety property; any Rocky 9 front door.

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
**Build**
1. `MirrorLog` over Storage's record: slot allocation, timer-free batching, quarantine and fresh-slot redelivery, seq audit, the revised state machine; `LocalRing` on DRAM, `memmap=` devdax/fsdax, and NVDIMM-N if present.
2. `TcpRing` (pinned thread per link, blocking `sendmsg`/`readv` into the arena, busy-poll) and `BlockRing` (io_uring `pwritev` O_DIRECT+FUA to an `nvmet-tcp` namespace on a pre-written fsdax file).
3. Front door and harness: ublk (`libublk`, Rocky 10) behind LIO iSCSI; fio; fault injector (SIGSTOP zombie, link cut, daemon kill, iDRAC power cycle, port unlink); a Jepsen/elle-style checker comparing client-observed acks with replayed state.
4. The same-node N->D SPSC ring and RPC header (Phase 3 seed).
5. Parallel two-week FC spike on target hardware: LPe32002 pair, pt2pt, `nvmet-fc` + fsdax + FUA; record RTT, throughput, firmware target-mode support and association teardown time after port unlink.

**Prove** (two R740xd-class nodes, 10GbE, 9000 MTU, tuning fixed)
- Mirror 4 KiB QD1: TcpRing p50 <= 35 us, p99 <= 100 us; BlockRing over nvmet-tcp with FUA p50 <= 100 us. Client 4 KiB QD1 through ublk+LIO: p50 <= 200 us gate (150 us target), p99 <= 500 us, proposed as the one shared budget.
- 64 KiB QD8 >= 1.1 GB/s mirrored; 4 KiB QD32 >= 200k records/s with one core per side; CP flush from the devdax arena with zero staging copies (counter).
- RPO=0: 10,000 fault iterations with zero acked-but-unreplayed writes under the checker, on DRAM (`Placed`) and pmem (`Durable`, both nodes power-cycled); zero zombie writes admitted in the SIGSTOP test.
- Mirror down, Ethernet up: Stalled within 200 ms, ALUA/ANA Transitioning, `GrantUnmirrored` committed, both nodes serving with takeover disabled; no initiator session drop through a 10 s stall.
- Link cut mid-stream: redelivery over the surviving link, seq audit clean, quarantine honoured.
- FC spike: numbers recorded; go/no-go on direct connect.

#### Top risks
1. **FC may be unsupportable as a product promise.** Every FC target path is Red Hat-unsupported, Linux-to-Linux direct connect is unvalidated, and FC buys no latency; the owner must re-rank Tier 2 before HBAs are bought.
2. **NVLOG capacity vs CP duration.** pmem is end-of-life and capped at 192 GB; the ring must hold interval + CP duration + quarantine; without an ingest cap, RPO=0 and throughput cannot both hold, and a DRAM-only pair carries a dual-power-loss exposure that must be stated.
3. **BlockRing fencing correctness** rests on lease-at-ack, port unlink and an unmeasured nvmet association teardown time; it must be proven by the checker, not argued.
4. **Stall and takeover budget.** Initiator 30 s aborts and ublk's missing per-IO abort force a 15 s ceiling that the specialists' 6-30 s takeover estimates do not all meet.
5. **Tuning and core budget.** Latency is tuning-dominated, io_uring is Technology Preview on Rocky 10, and nobody has summed pinned cores for a 2x16-core SKU.

### Opus-Storage

#### Concessions
- **Stream append is the wrong mirror contract** (Network #1/#3, Security #3, Consensus conflicts 1-2). `append -> Result<Seq, Gap>` presumes an in-order userspace receiver on the ACK path; the FC leg has none and SCSI/NVMe complete out of order. Replaced by sender-chosen slot offsets, self-validating records (header and trailer CRC, seq, lap, owner epoch), out-of-order tolerance, gaps resolved at replay as the maximal contiguous prefix, and `retire` as a sender-written status record. Two traits (data ring, control messages), not one.
- **tcm_qla2xxx is out-of-tree** on Rocky 9 and 10 (`# CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX is not set`, verified twice). My LIO-fileio FC ring falls; Network's in-box `nvmet-fc` file-backed namespace on fsdax over lpfc carries the same bytes.
- **4 KiB-aligned records** (Network #4, Consensus #6) cost 8 KiB per 4 KiB write: double NVLOG and wire bytes, half the 4K ceiling. Byte-packed records, 8-byte atomicity on pmem, 4 KiB alignment only for NVMe batches.
- **fsdax cannot be long-term pinned** (`FOLL_LONGTERM && vma_is_fsdax -> -EOPNOTSUPP`, 5.14 and 6.12): "PMem as the arena" needs devdax, while a ring a kernel target writes must be an fsdax file. The region splits.
- **No owner epoch in `RecHdr`** (Consensus #1); **15-30 s replay** (Consensus #2) plus detection and fencing exceeds every initiator timeout, so the partner must index its ring continuously, off the ACK path.
- **Unmirrored ACK is a consensus decision** (Consensus #3): local PMem is necessary; a quorum-committed grant that disables takeover is the other half.
- **The shadow replica is not free** (Consensus #4, Security #3): no fence, no resync, halved ingest.
- **CAW/PR never reach the D-Blade** through ublk/LIO (Security #1); "CRC at the edge" starts at the ublk buffer, LUNs are 512e with lbppbe=3, advertising discard obliges zeros (Security #2).
- **"Rocky 9 is acceptable" was a non-decision** (Security #4). STORAGE-1 adds that io_uring is off by default and Tech Preview on 10 as well, so the installer ships the sysctl and SELinux policy and the daemon probes at start.
- **STORAGE-2**: `O_DSYNC` on every data write costs FUA plus N member flushes; md swallows member flush errors; PERC VDs hide the platters. **STORAGE-7**: my commit sequence skipped writing back the payload before the first SFENCE; NT stores plus SFENCE are stable Rust. **STORAGE-4/8**: pre-GC drain is ~2 GB/s with modern CMR drives and `group_thread_cnt`, overflow is 43-80 s, and through Tier 1 the mirror link, not spindles, caps ingest. **STORAGE-5/6**: NVDIMM-N is EOL and costs half the DIMM slots; MD14xx dual-host attach is outside Dell's matrix and needs an HBA355e, never a PERC.
- **Latency** (Network #6, Security #5): my 150/500 us assumed interrupt-driven, unpinned TCP. Network owns the table.
- **"One process per aggregate"** (Consensus #5) was unexamined.

#### Holds firm
- **The NVLOG is the only durability mechanism; arena and index are a rebuildable cache.** Nobody attacked this, and the receiver-less ring strengthens it: self-validating records make the ring itself the truth, with no receiver state to lose.
- **RPO=0 needs persistent log media on both nodes.** DRAM mirrored into DRAM loses up to two CPs under dual power loss; DRAM-only stays a labelled prototype mode.
- **Node-local disks cannot do ONTAP-style takeover.** STORAGE-6 narrows the shelf option (dual-host attach unsupported by Dell); it does not reopen node-local disks without a shadow.
- **Write-hole closure by design** (never-overwrite CP flush, superblock-in-a-stripe), no PPL or journal: STORAGE-3 confirms the substance. I keep the write-intent bitmap with a large chunk; it is incompatible only with PPL, and it bounds md's own stripe resync, which our layout does not remove.
- **The throughput premise is still wrong after the corrections.** 2 GB/s pre-GC drain times 1/(1-u) at u=0.3-0.5 is 1.0-1.4 GB/s net per 12-HDD aggregate. "Network speed" is a latency and burst-absorption claim; sustained line rate needs shelves plus a mirror link faster than the array.
- **The SSD tier is mandatory**: 1-2.4K random read IOPS on 12 HDDs and a 38-600 GB map at 100 TB. Uncontested.
- **1GbE prototyping proves nothing about the D-Blade.** The in-process generator stays, layered under Network's ublk front door; Network's zero-copy criterion is what to defer, not the generator.
- **Record format before transport.** Every critic agrees one format must exist before Phase 1 code; they wanted a different shape, which I adopt.
- **Two aggregates per 24-bay node**, accepting Network's consequence: one ring and one credit budget per aggregate.
- **The local-persist component budget** (PMem 5-10 us per 64 KiB, Optane SSD 30-40 us, NAND PLP 50-100 us) and NAND's endurance ceiling stand; persist is the term that must overlap the mirror RTT.

#### Revised design changes
1. **One record** (Network/Consensus shape): 64 B header `{magic, hdr_crc, aggr: u16, owner_epoch: u32, seq: u64, cp_seq: u32, op: u8, vol: u32, lba: u64, len: u32, req_id: u64, ncrc: u8}`, then `ncrc` per-4 KiB CRC32Cs, byte-packed payload, 16 B trailer `{seq, crc}`: 1.02x at 4 KiB. Ops v1: Write, Discard, WriteZeroes, Snap, Clone, CpBoundary, Status (retire carrying epoch), Control. Same bytes in the NVLOG, on the wire and in the partner ring.
2. **Ring**: `RemoteRing::put(slot, bytes) -> Durable`, sender-chosen slots with laps; `Control` messages over TCP, `Control` records as the FC mailbox. Fence = lease; epoch rejection is cleanup done by the indexer, never an ACK-path criterion.
3. **PMem partition**: devdax for the local NVLOG and arena (pinned, registered); one fsdax file per inbound ring, written by nvmet-fc or the TCP receiver, read by the indexer and CP via short-term pins or O_DIRECT. Two aggregates give four slices of 192 GB NVDIMM-N (48 GB each: two CPs at ~1.1 GB/s per aggregate). Optane SSD baseline: own NVLOG and inbound ring on separate devices so each sees ≤2.2 GB/s on PCIe 3.0 x4.
4. **Standby indexer**: one core per inbound ring validates CRC and epoch and maintains a standby dirty index (~0.4 GB per CP). Takeover admits I/O at the new epoch after a ≤1 s residual scan; cold replay is the fallback. The takeover path is identical for shelves and shadow: assemble, apply ring suffix, CP.
5. **CP commit**: plain O_DIRECT data and map-delta writes, one `fdatasync`, superblock-in-a-stripe with `O_DSYNC` (FUA plus flush), then a Status record retiring the log. Startup verifies `/sys/block/*/queue/{fua,write_cache}`, refuses PERC VDs (HBA330/HBA355e IT mode only) and tracks member error counters because md hides member flush failures.
6. **PMem commit**: NT-store payload (`_mm_stream_si128`) + SFENCE; 8 B header seq store + `asm!("clwb")` (CPUID-gated, `_mm_clflush` fallback) + SFENCE.
7. **Throughput model**: drain 2 GB/s pre-GC (16+ TB CMR, `group_thread_cnt`, `stripe_cache_size` 32768), 1.0-1.4 GB/s net; backpressure ordered mirror link, then NVLOG device, then drain; adaptive CP interval.
8. **Front door**: 512e with lbppbe=3; zeros after discard at 4 KiB granularity; CRC32C digests preferred in LIO/nvmet; CAW/PR in Phase 3; ALUA Active/Standby.
9. **HA**: shelves via HBA355e, documented as outside Dell's MD14xx matrix (evaluate ME4/ME5 or a dual-host JBOD). Shadow mode: witness, lease-gated watchdog and iDRAC confirmation before promotion; **physical CP-segment shipping** is the single mechanism for steady state, resync and giveback (the ring still covers the last CP); the link carries up to 2x ingest plus GC, the partner array absorbs 2x, usable capacity and ingest halve (~0.5 GB/s on one 10GbE). Unmirrored ACK only under Consensus's quorum-committed grant.
10. **Topology**: one D-Blade process per node, per-aggregate thread groups, arenas, rings and leases, one watchdog pet via `otap-ctl`'s epoch page. Core ask per aggregate: 2 ingest/NVLOG, 1 indexer, 2 CP/GC, plus md group threads; ~12 of 32 for two aggregates.
11. **Baseline**: Rocky 10.1; installer drops `kernel.io_uring_disabled=1` plus `io_uring_group` and the SELinux policy; EPERM/ENOSYS probe at start; `pwritev2` only as a degraded mode. Latency targets come from Network's table.

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
**Build, in this order**
1. `otap-record` + `otap-nvlog`: the frozen record and slot-addressed ring; backends devdax (NT stores, `clwb`, SFENCE), O_DIRECT NVMe with IOPOLL (4 KiB batches), DRAM behind a loud flag; `put`, `status`, `replay` as a maximal-contiguous-prefix scan with CRC and epoch checks.
2. `otap-arena` + `otap-index`: pinned registered arenas, generation handles, dirty index with read-your-writes, overlap resolution, sector masks, per-aggregate credits and watermarks.
3. TCP ring receiver writing inbound records into the fsdax/O_DIRECT ring exactly as nvmet-fc will, plus the standby indexer.
4. CP-lite on a real 12-drive md RAID6: sorted full-stripe `WRITE_FIXED`, one flush, superblock-in-a-stripe, Status retire.
5. In-process load generator (4-64 KiB, overwrite, discard, write-zeroes) at ≥2 GB/s with no fabric; startup probes for io_uring, CPUID CLWB, member `fua`/`write_cache`, HBA mode.

**Prove**
- Durability: 1,000 crash and power-pull tests (SIGKILL, sysrq-c, `memmap=` PMem in a VM, the lab R740xd): zero ACKed-write loss, zero phantoms, zero stale-epoch records applied after BeginTakeover.
- Record overhead ≤1.03x NVLOG bytes per payload byte at 4 KiB.
- Local persist per 4 KiB record: PMem p50 ≤10 us, p99 ≤30 us; Optane SSD p50 ≤40 us. D-Blade ACK p50 ≤60 us, p99 ≤200 us on one pinned busy-polled 10GbE mirror link, persist and mirror overlapped.
- Ingest: ≥1.0 GB/s payload for 60 s on one 10GbE mirror link (link-bound), the NVLOG device carrying own plus partner, ≤4 cores; ≥2 GB/s with two links and flows.
- Takeover: standby index within 100 ms of the ring tail; residual scan ≤1 s; cold replay of 20 GB ≤20 s.
- Flush: ≥1.6 GB/s platter-average (≥2.0 GB/s outer) on 12x16+ TB CMR at chunk 256/512 KiB, `stripe_cache_size` 32768, `group_thread_cnt` 4; member reads ~0 during a CP; superblock survives power pulls. The measured number replaces 1.4 GB/s in the model.
- Reads: baseline random 4 KiB IOPS (expect 1-3K) to size the SSD tier.
- Integrity: injected corruption and bit flips detected 100%; Miri and fuzzing clean on arena, index and record parser.

#### Top risks
1. **NVLOG medium supply and endurance.** Optane PMem 100, NVDIMM-N and P5800X are EOL or used-market; NAND dies at moderate ingest and cannot carry own plus partner at line rate. Without persistent log media on both nodes, RPO=0 is a label, not a property. Decide before hardware purchase.
2. **The ingest ceiling is the mirror link and NVLOG device, then GC, not spindles.** Net drain is ~1.0-1.4 GB/s per 12-HDD aggregate; "network speed" must be re-scoped to ACK latency and burst absorption, and sustained line rate needs shelves plus a ≥25 Gb/s or FC mirror.
3. **HA data model.** Dual-host MD14xx is outside Dell's support; shadow mode without witness and fence is the one split-brain that corrupts data rather than stalling; physical shipping halves usable ingest and capacity.
4. **Record and ring contract freeze.** Three formats and two ring semantics exist today; no Phase 1 code until one 64 B header, one slot-addressed ring and lease-based fencing are signed off by all four specialists.
5. **Platter-durability chain and kernel posture.** md hides member flush errors, PERC VDs hide platters, io_uring is Tech Preview and off by default on Rocky 10, and the 38-600 GB map at 100 TB makes the NVMe tier non-negotiable.

### Opus-Consensus

#### Concessions
- **Raft over FC** (Network 1). Wrong: lpfc nvmet ports are target-only, so the FC leg is two unidirectional write paths with no receiver. Raft, heartbeats, leases and fence tokens move to a TCP/mTLS `ControlLink` over the cluster and management networks; ring `CONTROL` records are only a polled third liveness source. Two transport traits, not one.
- **"Epochs reject stragglers"** (Network 2). An NVMe/FC target accepts any write from a connected host. The lease plus watchdog bound is the only fence on every transport; the epoch is defence in depth where a receiver exists and mandatory cleanup at replay. The ladder gains the nvmet port-unlink rung.
- **Replay arithmetic** (Storage 1). My "1-2 s per GB" was 5 s/GB, about 94 s for 18.75 GB. The mirror ring must already sit on PMem, and replay (active plus frozen CP) must be capped by record count.
- **Initiator timers** (Security 1; CONSENSUS-7). Wrong knobs. A ForceOff'd target sends no RST, so stock ESXi pauses 25-35 s; Linux is NOP 5+5 s plus fast_io_fail_tmo 5 s with no_path_retry=fail; Windows is LinkDownTime 15 s and a 60 s guest timeout. The floating portal IP with IQN reinstatement under a second TPGT becomes mandatory for iSCSI, survivor ports report TRANSITIONING, and we ship multipath.conf and host tuning.
- **PR/UA/ALUA state in the RSM** (Security 2, 3). No mechanism under LIO. v1 documents that PR and UA are lost on takeover; the RSM holds identity only (NAA/serial, NGUID, TPGT, port IDs, LUN numbers, ANA groups, ACLs) plus a `luns[]` table so every LUN is pre-staged Standby/Inaccessible on the survivor.
- **PR and shelf facts** (CONSENSUS-4, CONSENSUS-8; Storage 2). A preempt fences only under a held WERO reservation; both nodes must register on every path (mpathpersist under dm-multipath); per-drive fencing is non-atomic. MD14xx two-host attach is "maximum servers 1", unsupported; md-cluster is absent from Rocky 9 and 10.
- **The unmirrored grant maroons data** (Storage 4; Network). True unless the NVLOG is durable, self-describing and transplantable; policy now follows NVLOG class.
- Also accepted: logical resync (Network 4); FC at 50-150 us (Network 5); no trust model (Security 4); the NVDIMM "armed" state and twelve-RDIMM DRAM cap (CONSENSUS-5); tcm_qla2xxx absent from Rocky 9 and 10, which also removes FC-SCSI clients (CONSENSUS-3; Security 5).

#### Holds firm
- **Three voters with a witness.** No critique touched it; two voters tolerate zero failures. Security's trust point hardens the witness (mTLS, management VLAN, fsync'd log on SSD). The disk-hosted voter stays a later option: CONSENSUS-4 confirms the single arbiter drive is the only atomic fence.
- **Node-local disks cannot give ONTAP-style takeover.** CONSENSUS-8 strengthens this: with md-cluster absent, our daemon is the only possible assembly guard. "Dell-unsupported" is a support and procurement risk, not a technical refutation; the T10 mechanics hold. The decision remains shelf (risk-accepted MD14xx or a vendor-documented dual-host JBOD; SAS/NL-SAS only) vs full logical replication vs manual failover, before Phase 2.
- **Lease plus lease-gated hardware watchdog is the primary fence; PR and ForceOff are early exits.** Network's "only the lease works everywhere" restates my matrix rule: no admission before the expiry bound plus fence. CONSENSUS-6 (1-3 s TLS, hung iDRACs) keeps 8 s as the hard floor.
- **The Raft-committed, takeover-disabling `GrantUnmirrored` invariant.** Still the only thing that makes "mirror down, then node dies" safe; its precondition changes (armed durable NVLOG), not its shape. Network's rule is the volatile-NVLOG special case.
- **Raft off the data path** (PR OUT excluded, Security 2) and **the aggregate as takeover unit** (Network's ring holds per-aggregate regions and credits).

#### Revised design changes
- **Two transports.** `ControlLink` (TCP, mTLS: Raft, heartbeats, leases, fence tokens) on the cluster and management networks; `MirrorRing` is Network's `RemoteRing` (TCP now; NVMe/FC, RDMA and the SAS-SSD journal later). Liveness uses the union of receive evidence (control links, FC `CONTROL` records, shelf mailbox); mirror capability is per direction, so a one-way FC loss is a mirror state, never a liveness verdict. Resync is logical: ring replay within two CP intervals, record shipping by seq range beyond.
- **One record header**: `{aggregate_id, owner_epoch, nvlog_seq, cp_seq, len, crc32c}`; `owner_epoch` is Raft's, `cp_seq` is Storage's. The superblock stores `owner_epoch` and the last committed `nvlog_seq`; an importer refuses an on-disk epoch newer than its grant.
- **Unified timers (mine).** Ring heartbeat 20 ms (link health only); control heartbeat 100 ms per link; link dead 1 s; partner silent 2 s on all links; `Stalled` at most 2 s, then BUSY, never an ACK; lease L = 3 s renewed every 500 ms, quorum- or partner-attested, carrying `nvlog_armed` and I/O progress; watchdog 5 s, hard bound 8 s; takeover admission at max(fence confirmed, t_last_heard + 3.2 s); survivor ports TRANSITIONING until Active/Optimized within 10 s p99, 15 s hard.
- **Mirror-down policy by NVLOG class.** Volatile or unarmed: never ACK unmirrored. Armed, self-describing NVDIMM-N/PMem: `GrantUnmirrored`, takeover disabled, alert, documented transplant-and-replay (RTO = repair). Shelf with SAS-SSD journal: journal write, takeover stays enabled. Losing `armed` revokes the grant.
- **Fence ladder**: (0) both nodes registered on all paths, owner holds WERO on every member; (1) PREEMPT AND ABORT per drive, arbiter drive first; (2) nvmet port unlink or LIO ACL removal; (3) Redfish ForceOff, PowerState polled, power-control-only account, IPMI-over-LAN off, firmware 7.00.00.172+; (4) the 8 s bound.
- **RSM additions**: `luns[]` identity and export staging, per-node `staged_version` and `nvlog_armed`, CA roots; `BeginTakeover` requires staged standby exports; PR OUT goes to the D-Blade log once passthrough exists. Trust: cluster CA at join, mTLS on `ControlLink`, witness on the management VLAN, read-only root-owned seqlock page.
- **Takeover budget and records cap.** 2 s detect + 3.2 s lease wait (overlap `mdadm --assemble --readonly`, `--readwrite` after the fence) + 3 s replay + 0.5 s commit and ALUA flip, about 9 s. Replay in 3 s needs at most 3 M outstanding records across active plus frozen CP: `max_outstanding_records` is cluster config owned here, implemented by Storage's adaptive CP sizing. Bitmap chunk at least the segment size; 30-60 s degraded flush budgeted after takeover.
- **Baseline and FC**: Rocky 10; FC and md-cluster are identical on 9 and 10. The decision record picks NVMe/FC mirror-only (target-only ports, two per node) vs out-of-tree tcm_qla2xxx (clients and mirror, tracks every kernel update) vs demoting FC behind 25GbE RoCE; I recommend demoting, with FC HBAs only if FC clients are required.

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
Phase 1: the mirror daemon, the control-plane seeds, and the spikes that decide Phase 2.

1. **Transport split and header.** `ControlLink` and `MirrorRing` over TCP with the unified header. Criteria: Raft heartbeats and leases survive loss of either network; a 10,000-seed turmoil matrix (per-link partitions, reorder, a polled-mailbox model for FC, followers paused longer than the lease) shows zero lease overlaps and zero stale-epoch records applied, in-line or at replay.
2. **Detector, lease, `Stalled` bound, lease-gated watchdog.** Criteria: `kill -STOP` resets the node within 8 s; the partner declares silence at 2 s; a unique-payload checker finds zero writes ACKed after lease expiry or past the 2 s `Stalled` bound; no grant without `nvlog_armed`.
3. **Ownership oracle stub and seqlock page** (epoch, lease expiry, grant bit, staged-LUN version). Criterion: an epoch bump fails all in-flight sender writes closed within one RTT.
4. **Fence library.** PR (register all paths, WERO hold, preempt-and-abort), Redfish ForceOff with PowerState polling, nvmet/LIO unlink. Criteria: the fenced node's next write returns RESERVATION CONFLICT on every member; ForceOff confirmed within 5 s p99 on iDRAC9 7.00.00.172+; IPMI-over-LAN verified disabled per unit.
5. **Shelf spike.** MD14xx (risk-accepted) or a vendor-documented dual-host JBOD, two HBA355e, twelve NL-SAS, one-EMM-per-host and dual-path (dm-multipath, mpathpersist) topologies. Criteria: PR conflict through the expanders; FUA observed at the drive; zero member reads during a CP; survivor assembles after preempt with auto-assembly masked; rehearsal takeover reaches A/O within 10 s p99 against ESXi (floating IP, TPGT reinstatement), Linux dm-multipath (shipped multipath.conf, TRANSITIONING) and Windows MPIO, zero guest I/O errors.
6. **Durability spike.** NVDIMM-N with armed-state detection, or PLP NVMe; 50 PDU pulls under load; zero lost ACKed writes.
7. **Decision record before Phase 2**: shelf vs full logical replication vs manual; witness placement; NVLOG class; mirror-down policy; FC scope; `max_outstanding_records`.

#### Top risks
1. **FC cannot be the "primary goal" on stock Rocky.** tcm_qla2xxx and efct are unset on 9 and 10; in-box NVMe/FC is mirror-only on target-only ports; an out-of-tree module must track every kernel update. Decide before buying HBAs.
2. **The shelf is Dell-unsupported.** MD14xx is "maximum servers 1"; it works in the field, but support, dm-multipath and PR-on-all-paths are real costs; SATA is excluded. Without a shelf, takeover means full logical replication or manual failover.
3. **The 10 s takeover budget has no end-to-end owner yet.** It needs the outstanding-record cap (mine; Storage implements), floating IPs and pre-staged exports (Security), TRANSITIONING plus a shipped multipath.conf, and 30-60 s of bitmap flush afterwards.
4. **NVDIMM-N.** Refurbished 2017 part, battery-armed state, DRAM capped at twelve RDIMMs, 192 GB split four ways; a volatile NVLOG makes "RPO=0" false on a rack power event and forces never-ACK-unmirrored.
5. **Control-plane trust and topology.** Witness placement, iDRAC network separation and per-unit IPMI state are per-site facts; a misplaced or compromised witness can commit a takeover.

### Opus-Security

#### Concessions
##### Concessions

- **Rocky 10, but for the right reasons (SECURITY-1, -2).** `kernel.io_uring_disabled=2` is Rocky 10's default too, and io_uring is Tech Preview on both releases: the sysctl is an install-time line, not an OS argument. The real case is `ublk_drv` (absent from 9, in every Rocky 10, bulk-backported in 10.1 under RHEL-106845), NAPI busy-poll, SENDZC, multishot recv and nvmet auth/TLS. Install needs `kernel.io_uring_disabled=0` plus an SELinux module for `IORING_OP_URING_CMD`; unsupported-TP status is a product risk I never listed.
- **TCMU buys no cluster PR (SECURITY-3).** TCMU passes no I_T nexus to userspace and block `pr_ops` carry no initiator identity, so the TCMU passthrough handler and the ublk `pr_ops` patch are withdrawn. Initiator-aware PR exists only as single-node LIO emulation or in a native target that owns the nexus.
- **FC "for free" was wrong (Network #1, Consensus #3).** `CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX` is unset on Rocky 9 and 10, so FC-SCSI, client or mirror, is an out-of-tree kmod. In-box FC is NVMe/FC on Emulex only (ESXi/Linux, not Windows, target-only ports). HBA330 has no external ports; a shared JBOD needs HBA355e.
- **Half a fence (Network #2, Consensus #2).** Inline epoch rejection needs a userspace receiver; nvmet-fc rejects nothing. The lease and watchdog fence on every transport, the witness is needed for every ownership change under partition, and running unmirrored needs a committed grant.
- **Holding I/O on `StaleEpoch` is wrong (Consensus #1):** a lease-expired owner fails closed at once.
- **`USER_RECOVERY` alone aborts in-flight I/O (Network #3):** REISSUE plus `req_id`-keyed idempotent mirroring is needed, and with D in the same process a restart is a 15-30 s replay stall (Storage #3, Consensus #4): N and D must be separate processes.
- **Bottlenecks (Storage #2, Network #4, SECURITY-8).** The cleaner reads segments sequentially; a mirror link equal to the flush rate caps burst absorption, so it must exceed peak client ingest. QD1 is 90-200 us untuned; my FC RTT was unsourced, so FC demotion rests on support status and the extra kernel hop, not latency.
- **Smaller:** PPL/journal withdrawn for never-overwrite plus write-intent bitmap (Storage #1); 512e iSCSI LUNs (Storage #4); PR as ordered `PrUpdate` NVLOG records (Storage #5, Consensus #5); IPMI is off by default on iDRAC9, Redfish accelerates but does not fence (Consensus #6); Windows restated as "no inbox MPIO-capable initiator" (SECURITY-6); a hobby-scale Rust iSCSI crate exists, reference only (SECURITY-5).

#### Holds firm
##### Holds firm

- **Kernel targets in Phase 1, not Rust stacks.** SECURITY-5 confirms nothing production-usable exists; `iscsi-target` and `ioutgt` become reference code, not dependencies. ESXi, Windows MPIO and WSFC interop in week one is what no Rust stack gives.
- **ublk over TCMU on a Rocky 10.1 baseline.** Strengthened, not weakened: ublk ships in every Rocky 10 (no TCMU fallback), TCMU has no PR advantage, and Rocky 9 lacks ublk, busy-poll and nvmet auth/TLS.
- **Active/Standby ALUA, ANA optimized/inaccessible while LIO/nvmet front the LUN.** SECURITY-7 confirms it for an mdadm/RAM backend and every specialist agrees. Network #6 is right that no cross-node data path exists until the native target, so the ownership cache shrinks to LUN -> aggregate -> local `dbladed`; the owner epoch stays in every record for replay-time rejection and cleanup, which is not dead code.
- **FC is not the mirror transport**, now on firmer ground: out-of-tree or target-only kernel stacks in the data path, no inline rejection, no RPC semantics, no vendor support. RoCE is the latency lever.
- **iSCSI/LIO cannot be dropped** (Windows), though the NVMe-oF front end must be built Windows-capable (ANA, discovery) for Microsoft's eventual GA.
- **No ack without a partner-durable ack** is the RPO=0 invariant and holds on every transport; I retract only the rejection half of "the mirror is the fence".
- **PR in the D-Blade log, not Raft**: Storage and Consensus converged on it.
- **The 1GbE ceiling (~28K 4K writes/s), the QD1 arithmetic and the security posture** (CHAP mandatory, `allow_any_host=0`, nftables default-deny on data NICs, management on its own VLAN with mTLS, fuzzed framing) were confirmed and stand.

#### Revised design changes
##### Revised design changes

1. **Fabric matrix, Rocky 10.1 in-box:** iSCSI via LIO (all initiators); NVMe/TCP and NVMe/RDMA via nvmet (ESXi 7.0U3+, Linux, Windows via third-party initiator until Microsoft GA); NVMe/FC via lpfc nvmet (Emulex, ESXi/Linux). FC-SCSI (QLogic + `tcm_qla2xxx` kmod) only if a customer with Windows FC hosts insists. If FC at all: Emulex LPe32002/35002.
2. **Process topology:** one executable, two roles: `nbladed` (ublk server, configfs applier, lease-driven ALUA/ANA flipper) and one `dbladed` per aggregate owning the NVLOG arena (devdax, else DRAM), joined by per-core SPSC rings in shared memory that carry the frozen RPC header. `nbladed` runs with `USER_RECOVERY|USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE|USER_COPY` and puts `req_id` = (ublk tag, daemon generation) in every record so the partner applies duplicates idempotently in seq order; `dbladed` publishes `Recovering{eta}` to the watchdog and a records-per-CP cap bounds replay. Verify whether RHEL-106845 brought `UBLK_F_SUPPORT_ZERO_COPY`/`AUTO_BUF_REG` to 10.1: if so, one copy, landing in the NVLOG slot with the header prepended (Network's slot contract); otherwise two copies are accepted for Phase 1.
3. **One record format, owned by Storage:** 4 KiB-aligned header with `(aggregate_id, owner_epoch, nvlog_seq, cp_seq, req_id, crc32c)` and types `Write`, `PrUpdate`, `Control`, `CpBoundary`. My format is withdrawn.
4. **Two transport traits:** `MirrorRing` (put-at-slot, `durable()` ack, `can_reject`) and `ControlChannel` (TCP messaging for lease, heartbeats, Raft). `fence_epoch` is a ring-side filter used only where `can_reject`. Consensus's failure detector is the only one; my timers go.
5. **Fail closed:** `nbladed` checks the lease seqlock page before completing any ublk I/O; on expiry it flips the TPG to ALUA Standby and the ANA group to Inaccessible via configfs (LIO answers NOT READY 04h/0Bh plus the 2Ah/06h UA; nvmet answers ANA Inaccessible plus an AEN) and fails unmirrored I/O instead of holding it. Holds happen only on the healthy side during an ownership refresh.
6. **Takeover unit = aggregate;** LUNs and namespaces inherit its epoch.
7. **Mirror link >= peak client ingest** (2x10GbE or 25GbE in production).
8. **Geometry:** iSCSI LUNs 512e (`logical_bs_shift=9`, `physical_bs_shift=12`), NVMe namespaces 4 KiB, alignment counters exported.
9. **PR:** `PrUpdate` records ordered by `nvlog_seq`, served to N-Blades at login; TPG tags and relative target port IDs are control-plane constants identical on both nodes. While LIO fronts the LUN, "PR lost on takeover" is documented; Phase 3 may scrape LIO's readable PR attributes and reload them on the partner via `res_aptpl_metadata` (best-effort, unordered; verify).
10. **Fence rungs:** lease + hardware watchdog always; Redfish over a dedicated iDRAC NIC as accelerator; per-drive PR only with a shared SAS JBOD. IPMI removed.
11. **Write hole:** no PPL or journal; write-intent bitmap; full-stripe CP writes to unreferenced stripes only.
12. **Install profile:** `kernel.io_uring_disabled=0`, SELinux `io_uring cmd` policy, `target.service` masked; `nbladed` refuses to start unless `UBLK_U_CMD_GET_FEATURES` reports REISSUE.

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build

- Rocky 10.1 (6.12.0-130+); HBA330 or HBA355e per the disk-topology decision, which is made before the record format is frozen; NVDIMM-N for NVLOG if funded, else DRAM + UPS with `WCE=1`/`VWC=1` advertised and SYNCHRONIZE CACHE forcing a mini-CP.
- `nbladed`: `libublk` + `io-uring`, one ublk device per LUN, queues 1:1 with data cores, REISSUE recovery, 512e/4K geometry, Flush a no-op only on NV NVLOG.
- `dbladed` per aggregate: NVLOG arena, `MirrorRing` over TCP (plus loopback for tests), `ControlChannel` over TCP, lease page, Storage's record format.
- Configfs applier: LIO iSCSI + nvmet-tcp from a declarative spec; CHAP and DH-HMAC-CHAP mandatory, `allow_any_host=0`, discovery auth, ALUA/ANA groups, identical `vpd_unit_serial`/NGUID and port IDs on both nodes.

##### Prove

1. **Interop:** ESXi 8 (iSCSI, NVMe/TCP), Windows Server 2022 (iSCSI MPIO), Rocky/Ubuntu (both) log in with CHAP, format, run 24 h `fio --verify`; `libiscsi` suite and `blktests nvme` pass.
2. **Performance:** 4K write QD1 <= 200 us untuned, <= 120 us with NAPI busy-poll over 10GbE (<= 350 us on 1GbE); QD32 >= 100K 4K write IOPS per node over 10GbE, >= 20K over 1GbE; the table republished as the Phase 3 baseline.
3. **RPO=0:** iDRAC power-off of the owner during `fio --verify`, 50 runs, zero acknowledged writes missing from the partner's NVLOG.
4. **Restart:** `nbladed` restart under load, 50 runs: no path drop, no I/O error, no duplicate-induced verify failure; `dbladed` restart: `Recovering` published, replay <= 5 s.
5. **Takeover (manual command in Phase 1, lease-gated):** old owner Standby/Inaccessible strictly before survivor AO/Optimized, both flips visible <= 3 s; ESXi and Windows failover with zero guest I/O errors; end-to-end inside Consensus's 6-14 s band, 25 s hard ceiling.
6. **Fence:** 100 partition trials (mirror cut, witness unreachable): the stale owner acknowledges zero writes after lease expiry.
7. **Security:** 24 h `cargo-fuzz` on record and control parsers without crash or OOM; a 1,000-session login flood under nftables connlimit stays under the memory cap; unauthenticated data and discovery sessions are rejected.

#### Top risks
##### Top risks

1. **Node-local disks make takeover theatre.** Shared SAS JBOD (HBA355e, SAS-only drives) or full replication must be chosen before the record format; it decides whether Phase 1's ALUA failover is real.
2. **Tech Preview foundations.** io_uring and ublk are unsupported on Rocky 9 and 10, the ublk ABI is being backported in bulk, and SELinux/sysctl gate both; no vendor supports the data path.
3. **FC expectations.** In-box FC is NVMe/FC on Emulex only (no Windows, target-only ports, no inline rejection); FC-SCSI needs an out-of-tree kmod. "FC primary goal" must be restated as isolation, not latency.
4. **PR/CAW are single-node until the native target.** WSFC and VMware in-guest clustering lose reservations on takeover; Active/Standby only; no cross-node data path in Phases 1-3.
5. **Takeover time vs guest timers.** 6-14 s plus any replay; needs continuous partner-side indexing, the fail-closed flip, and tolerance of the 8 s watchdog floor.

## Critics

### Completeness critic

#### Missing topics
**Before or during Phase 1**
- **Lab plan and CI without the target hardware.** Every success criterion assumes two R740xd-class nodes, 10GbE and pmem; Consensus and Network each add a shelf or FC spike; nobody wrote the procurement list or a CI story. Needed: a BOM with lead times (2x R740xd, PLP NVMe or used NVDIMM-N plus battery and 3.5-in mid tray, 2x10GbE/25GbE NICs, 2x LPe32002, 2x HBA355e, one dual-EMM NL-SAS shelf, switched PDU, witness host, ESXi/Windows/Linux initiators) and a VM matrix that runs most of Phase 1 without it: `memmap=` pmem, nvmet-tcp as the BlockRing stand-in (Network), and `nvmet-fcloop`, which is `=m` on both Rocky kernels (NETWORK-1 evidence), exercises the nvmet-fc target and host code on one box with no HBA, and was mentioned by nobody. Consensus's turmoil/loom/stateright plan covers only the control plane.
- **Observability as a contract.** Storage lists hdrhistogram/prometheus; Security wants read-miss and alignment counters; nobody specified per-stage histograms (ingest, persist, mirror ack, CP), ring fill, CP duration, GC amplification, link health, lease and `nvlog_armed` state, or alerts for states that silently weaken RPO=0 (`GrantUnmirrored`, NVDIMM write-protect, DRAM mode). The Phase 1 criteria ("zero staging copies", "zero acked-but-unreplayed") are checkable only through these counters.
- **Performance methodology.** NETWORK-5 showed the TCP band is tuning-dominated (15-100 us). Network listed a tuning profile; nobody owns fio job files, warm-up, array aging state (fresh vs half-full with GC running), the measurement boundary, or a CI regression gate. Without it the latency tables get re-litigated at every run.
- **Cold start after dual power loss.** Consensus's matrix has the row and Network's criterion power-cycles both nodes, but no reconcile protocol exists: each node holds its own NVLOG plus the partner's ring; who replays what, in what order, before anyone serves? Needed before the durability spike.
- **Core, NUMA and memory budget per SKU.** Security noted nobody summed pinned cores; Storage's "12 of 32" is D-Blade only (no LIO/nvmet threads, ublk queues, mirror links, standby indexer, md group threads, Raft). NVDIMM-N caps DRAM at 12 RDIMMs (STORAGE-5), so NVLOG, arenas, map cache and read cache share one budget. Decides the SKU and NIC count ordered.
- **Mirror link isolation.** Every customer byte crosses the mirror in clear; Consensus puts mTLS only on `ControlLink`. Decide: physically private point-to-point link, or kTLS/NVMe-TCP TLS, which changes TcpRing and the latency table.
- **Privilege model, secrets and licences.** The daemons need CAP_SYS_ADMIN, SG_IO, `/dev/watchdog`, configfs, devdax and iDRAC credentials; no privilege separation, daemon SELinux policy, secret storage (CHAP, iDRAC, CA keys) or `cargo-deny`/vendoring plan exists. Network flagged two crate licences (async-rdma GPL, sideway MPL); there is no pass over the full tree, the Broadcom userspace driver, or any out-of-tree module.

**Phase 2**
- **Read path and SSD tier.** Storage's read path is the most complete piece, but cache fill policy (on CP vs on miss), persistence across restart, coldness after takeover (the survivor has nothing cached for the partner's aggregate) and the device BOM are open; reads are where Storage says "network speed" is 100-1000x off.
- **Capacity over months.** Free-space reserve, write amplification near full, map growth (38-600 GB at 100 TB), fragmentation under random overwrite, deadlists versus GC, thin provisioning and out-of-space semantics (space-allocation thresholds; what a thin LUN sees when the aggregate fills); no aging soak test proposed.
- **Data reduction and encryption at rest.** Mentioned by nobody. Compression needs physical length separate from logical length in the map entry and decides whether the mirror carries compressed payload (and what the CRC covers); encryption must sit below md (SED/dm-crypt) or inside the LFS (per-volume keys). Reserve both in the Phase 2 formats even if shipped later.
- **Snapshots and async replication.** Snap/Clone/deadlists exist (Storage); schedules, retention, consistency groups and a SnapMirror equivalent (Network's one line; Consensus's logical record shipping is the seed) are unscoped.

**Formats in Phase 1, policy by Phase 4**
- **Upgrades and rollback.** Consensus covers rolling upgrade and a versioned `Cmd`; Storage's revised header has no version field, and no superblock/segment/map versioning or downgrade rule exists. Tech Preview ublk/io_uring ABIs are bulk-backported (RHEL-106845), so each kernel update is a compatibility event.
- **Management plane.** "gRPC with mTLS on its own VLAN" (Security) plus a configfs applier is the entire design: no API surface (volumes, LUNs, igroups, snapshots, takeover/giveback, cluster join), CLI, RBAC, audit log, or statement of whether desired state lives in the RSM. Phase 1 needs only the ownership-oracle CLI.
- **Runbooks and host utilities.** Disk replacement (30 h rebuilds per Storage), battery and NVDIMM transplant-and-replay, node replacement, firmware updates, capacity-full recovery, and the multipath.conf plus ESXi/Windows tuning package Consensus made mandatory, which nobody owns.

#### Unresolved conflicts
1. **Record layout: crossed concessions.** Network conceded to 4 KiB-aligned payloads with a parallel header ring; Storage simultaneously conceded to Network's byte-packed stream (64 B header, packed payload, 16 B trailer, "1.02x"); Security says "4 KiB-aligned header". Storage's own round-2 argument refutes Storage's revision: packed payloads misalign every O_DIRECT/`WRITE_FIXED` iovec and turn each CP flush into a 22 GB memcpy. Either a single packed stream (one put per record, simplest replay and FC put-at-offset) or Network's split (dense descriptor blocks plus a 4 KiB-granular data ring: ~1.02x and zero-copy CP, two writes per batch on BlockRing). Sub-dispute: `req_id` dropped by Network, required by Security for `USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE` dedupe, kept by Storage.
2. **Shadow-replica mechanism.** Storage's revision makes physical CP-segment shipping "the single mechanism for steady state, resync and giveback"; Consensus's revision (and Network in round 2) make it logical: ring replay within two CPs, record shipping by seq beyond. Physical: one sequential copy path, no allocator or index work on the partner, map deltas ship with the segments. Logical: no identical-geometry requirement, the partner keeps its own allocator and GC, and it is the same code as ring replay. Moot with shelves, decisive without.
3. **Process topology.** Security: one `dbladed` per aggregate; Storage: one D-Blade process per node with per-aggregate thread groups; Consensus only requires that one restart never stops the node's watchdog pet. Per-aggregate buys crash and replay isolation matching the takeover unit; per-node gives one io_uring/arena/NUMA owner for a shared NVLOG device and one progress counter.
4. **DRAM-only semantics.** Security's build advertises WCE=1/VWC=1 with SYNCHRONIZE CACHE forcing a mini-CP when no NV NVLOG exists; Network defaults DRAM to a `Placed` ack with a written dual-power-loss caveat; Storage calls DRAM a labelled prototype. WCE=1 is protocol-honest but, in Security's own round-1 words, "destroys the sequential-CP premise under fsync-heavy VMs" and is a third write path to build; `Placed` plus caveat matches the brief's literal single-node RPO but misstates the SCSI contract.
5. **FC in Phase 1.** Network keeps a two-week Emulex NVMe/FC direct-connect spike; Consensus demotes FC behind 25GbE RoCE with HBAs only if FC clients are required; Security: "if FC at all, Emulex". For: the brief calls FC the primary goal and the FC leg (target-only ports, receiver-less, port-unlink fence, FUA) is what stresses the ring contract.
